// pages read as one book is the EDITS table in that script.

[[markup]]
== Decoded Markup and Controls

BOO topic content decodes to a compact BookManager control stream. It is not raw
SGML, XML, or HTML. The decoded stream does preserve IBM GML and BookMaster
concepts such as heading tags, highlighted phrases, figures, tables, lists, and
cross references.

The examples below use the current experimental decoder's text rendering. That
decoder still has imperfect spacing and case recovery, so control names should
be treated as case-insensitive unless a byte-level rule says otherwise.

`BOO/packet.script` is an authoritative BookMaster source file for
`BOO/packet.boo`. Use that source file as the preferred authority for tag names,
tag punctuation, and source-level tag usage. BOO output generated by
`boorender --raw` should therefore use BookMaster-style colon tags such as
`:h1.`, `:p.`, `:hp1.`, `:fig.`, and `:toc.` rather than libgeist-invented
projection tags where a source-level equivalent is known.

[[markup_historical_lineage]]
=== Historical Lineage

External historical references confirm the lineage:

* IBM Generalized Markup Language (GML) was an IBM SCRIPT/DCF macro/tag set and
used colon-prefixed tags such as `:h1`, `:p`, `:ol`, and `:li`.
* SGML descended from IBM GML and was standardized as ISO 8879.
* IBM SCRIPT-family documentation describes BookMaster and BookManager BUILD as
later systems built on the SCRIPT/GML foundation, with BookManager electronic
books compiled into `.BOO` files and served by BookServer.

References: link:https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM_Generalized_Markup_Language[IBM Generalized Markup Language],
link:https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SCRIPT_(markup[SCRIPT markup]), and
link:https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Generalized_Markup_Language[SGML].

The BOO evidence is consistent with that history: decoded records contain GML
style names such as `:h1`, `:h2`, `:figlist`, `:tlist`, and `HP1`, but the
stored representation is a tokenized BookManager format rather than SGML text.

[[markup_control_syntax]]
=== Control Syntax

Decoded controls are word-like commands embedded in logical records. Some use
`KEY=value`, some use `KEY <fields>`, and some are concatenated with their
primary id.

[cols="<14,<31,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Form |Example |Meaning

|`KEY=value` |`CFONTDEF=1 Hp1` |Book/global property or definition.
|`KEY <fields>` |`CTOCE 0 1 1.0 Introduction` |Structured control with positional fields.
|`KEY<id>` |`SHcontents`, `SRFIGfig4302hp1` |Control whose first operand is concatenated to the control word.
|`:tag` value |`CHDLEVEL :h1` |GML-derived tag name used as a BookManager topic kind or style.
|===

Older traces printed separator/control placeholders as bare `?` characters.
That is ambiguous because `?` is also literal document punctuation. Current
analysis traces must instead preserve literal question marks as text and expand
decoded placeholders to diagnostic markers of the form
`<geist-placeholder kind='...' offset='...' len='...'>`. The `kind` value
records why the byte was treated as a placeholder, for example
`control-boundary`, `decoded-question-run`, `line-marker-boundary`, or
`decoder-separator`. Offsets are byte indexes in the decoded logical-record
string shown by `bootrace`, not original BOO file offsets.

Those placeholders are decoder artifacts or unresolved control spacing and
should not be treated as literal document text without byte-level confirmation.
They also are not paragraph boundaries by themselves. In the `BOO/packet.boo`
`NOTICES` topic, the decoded text contains a placeholder run between `second cover page` and `if you wish.`; BookServer renders this as continuous paragraph
text. A raw projection should split at a placeholder only when the following
non-space content is a recognizable BookManager/GML control such as `CZ`,
`CFONT`, or `CSELECT`. Otherwise the placeholder run is padding/spacing and
should collapse to normal whitespace.

`libgeist` raw topic output projects the decoded control stream into a
BookMaster-style GML script. This projection is intentionally not Markdown and
not final rendering. It is a readable, colon-tagged representation of the
BookManager controls that have been identified so far. Decoder placeholder bytes
such as `?` are not emitted as text in this projection.

[[markup_bookmaster_style_raw_projection]]
=== BookMaster-Style Raw Projection

The raw projection uses one colon-prefixed BookMaster-style tag per identified
control or text span. The source control names remain documented here so a
reader can map the projection back to decoded BOO controls. Generated
BookManager controls that do not have a direct source tag are either suppressed,
represented with the nearest BookMaster tag shape, or emitted in raw/debug
projection as `:unknown-control name='<CONTROL>' raw='<decoded segment>'.` when
the segment is control-shaped but not yet modeled. Markdown rendering suppresses
these diagnostics; use `boorender --raw` or `bootrace` when analyzing them.

[cols="<45,<45,<45,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Decoded control |Raw projection |Field mapping |Status

|`SH<id>` |suppressed |Topic id from the concatenated suffix; used internally for lookup. |Verified topic header control.
|`CTOPICN <n>` |suppressed |1-based topic number; generated navigation metadata. |Verified topic header control.
|`CPARENT <id>` |suppressed |Parent topic id; generated navigation metadata. |Verified topic header control.
|`CFORWARDLEVEL <id>` |suppressed |Next topic id; generated navigation metadata. |Verified topic header control.
|`CBACKLEVEL <id>` |suppressed |Previous topic id; generated navigation metadata. |Verified topic header control.
|`CSUMMARY <a> <b> <c>` |suppressed |`a` = `c` = the topic's display-row count; `b` = its direct-child count. |Verified over all 10,502 topics; see <<topics_csummary_a_b_c>>.
|`CHDLEVEL :<tag>` plus `ST <title>` |`:<tag>.<title>` for titled tags, or `:<tag>.` for untitled structural tags |GML/BookMaster topic kind and title. `:title` normalizes to source-style `:tipage.`. |Verified against `packet.script` and topic headers.
|Structural `CHDLEVEL` plus `ST <title> . <fixed-body>` |`:<tag>.` followed by `:xmp.` / `:xline.` / `:exmp.` |For structural topics, `ST` can contain both the display title and the complete fixed-layout body. A standalone dot surrounded by whitespace separates the title from the body and is not visible text. Do not discard the suffix merely because the `CHDLEVEL` tag itself is untitled. |Verified against `GG24-4302-00.boo` topic `ABSTRACT` and hosted BookServer output.
|`CSOURCEFN <name>` |suppressed |Original source member/file name. |Observed in topic headers.
|`ST <title/text>` without a pending `CHDLEVEL` |`:p.<text>` |Text fallback. |Projection fallback.
|`CTOCDEF=<style> <fields>` |`:toc.` once for the generated TOC block |TOC style definition. Source BookMaster uses `:toc.` and does not contain expanded generated entries. |Verified in `packet.script` and `CONTENTS` topics.
|`CTOCE <level> <style> <id> <title>` |`:li.<title>` |One generated TOC entry represented as list item text. |Source-style approximation.
|`CZ OFF ETOC` |`:etoc.` |End of the generated TOC region. |There is *no standalone `ETOC` control*. Only the five `CZ`-dialect books close `CONTENTS` this way; the other 29 have no terminator and the TOC ends with the topic's record range. See <<toc_there_is_no_standalone_etoc_control>>.
|`CFONTDEF=<code> <name>` |suppressed |Font/style code definition. |The current source-style projection does not yet reconstruct inline `:hpN.` spans from these definitions.
|`CFONT <triples...> [text]` |trailing text preserved as inline `:hpN.` spans when present; span-only records are applied to the following plain text segment |Repeated `<offset> <length> <font_code>` triples, sometimes followed by decoded visible text. Offsets are absolute display-column coordinates in the fixed display line. Apply them before source-style whitespace collapse, using the active `CZ FLOW` indent column for same-line text. Span-only continuation records use the following physical text line's paragraph column. Do not recover by scoring word boundaries. |Verified against `packet.boo` topic `1.3`: `CFONT 27 5 3 33 10 3` has no trailing text, but hosted BookServer applies it to the following `FM radio through its audio interface;` line and emphasizes `audio interface;`; later `CFONT 17 3 2 ... 64 13 2` emphasizes whole words `you cannot use your radio's VOX control for bidirectional`.
|`CSELECT <col> <len> <target> [text]` l|:pinline.<before> :hdref refid='<target>'.<selected>:ehdref. <after> |Selectable link/cross-reference. The target may be a topic id, anchor id, or footnote id. `col` is an absolute zero-based display-cell column and `len` is the selected display-cell count; neither is relative to the end of the decoded fragment. |Verified against `GG24-4302-00.boo` topic `NOTICES` and `packet.boo` topic `1.1`.
|`CSELECT <col> <len> PIC<n> [text]` inside a figure |`:image resource='<n>'.` plus `:figcap.<caption>` |Selectable embedded picture placeholder. The `PIC<n>` selection is replaced by the rendered picture; surrounding figure caption text remains ordinary caption text. A leading generated `PICTURE <n>` label is reader metadata and is not part of the visible caption. Markdown keeps the BOO resource as `resource:<n>` so exporters can resolve it. |Verified against `packet.boo` topic `1.3`, where BookServer renders `PIC1` as pass:[<literal>/bookmgr/</literal><literal>pictures/</literal><literal>packet.20260614112503.</literal><literal>P1.GIF</literal>], followed by caption `Figure 1. VHF/UHF LMR audio frequency range`.
|`CSELECT <col> <len> PIC<n> [text]` inside `SRTBL` |`:image resource='<n>'.` plus the visible table text |Some generated product-overview layouts use a table solely to place a picture beside prose. `PIC<n>` still denotes the BOO resource; `PICTURE <n>` and adjacent `SI` keys are non-visible metadata. The selector's `<col> <len>` are the columns the image replaces on the following display line; see "Selectors inside a table envelope". |Verified against `GG24-395.boo` logical records 528 (`PIC68`, topic `3.3.7`), 581--582 (`PIC81`, topic `3.3.11`), and 635 (`PIC91`, topic `3.3.15`), compared with BookServer DT `19941215160749`.
|`CSELECT <col> <len> LNK <kind> ... <target> ... [text]` |Inline image or external Markdown link |`LNK` alternatives describe presentation and transport. Observed targets include BOO-relative `/bookmgr/...`, `http://`, and `ftp://` paths. Empty `<>` alternatives and symbolic names are metadata, not visible prose. |Verified against `XWEBDEMO.boo` topics `1.0`, `1.4.1`--`1.4.4`: pass:[<literal>/bookmgr/</literal><literal>product.</literal><literal>gif</literal>], pass:[<literal>/bookmgr/</literal><literal>monetley.</literal><literal>jpg</literal>], pass:[<literal>/bookmgr/</literal><literal>*.avi\|*.</literal><literal>mpg\|*.wav</literal>], FTP, and HTTP targets; BookServer DT `19970423182524`.
|`CMENU` |`:ul type='menu'.` |Start of generated selectable menu/list. |hosted BookServer emits a `Subtopics:` heading and an HTML `<ul>` for PACKET topic `1.0`; raw output keeps it distinct from ordinary source `:ul.` lists.
|`CMITEM <id> <text>` |`:li refid='<id>'.<id> <text>` |Menu item target and visible label. |Verified against `packet.boo` topic `1.0`: hosted BookServer emits pass:[<literal>&lt;li&gt;&#160;&#160;&lt;a href="1.1?..."&gt; 1.1 Original Packet Radio&lt;/a&gt;</literal>], so the first token is both the href target and the leading words of the visible label.
|`CEMENU` |`:eul.` |End of menu/list. |Observed menu/list terminator.
|`SR<id>` |`:anchor id='<id>'.` |Generic spot/section anchor. `CSELECT` can target this id directly. |Verified by `QS3X36CM.BOO` links such as `sptproc`, `sptcontrol`, and `sptocl`.
|`SRFTN<id>` followed by `CZ FLOW FN ...` |`:fn id='<id>'.<text>` |Start of a generated footnote body. |`SREFTN` |`:efn.` |End of a generated footnote body. |`SRFIG<id>` |`:fig id='<id>'.` plus optional figure body records |Figure anchor/start id. |`SREFIG [text]` |`:efig.` plus optional following body text |Figure end marker. Trailing decoded bytes after `SREFIG` are following visible text, not part of the marker. |`SRTBL<id>` |`:table id='<id>'.` |Table anchor/start id. TBLDBCTL51` targets `SRTBLDBCTL51`.
|`SRETBL` |`:etable.` |Table end marker. |Observed.
|`CINDEX` / `CITERM` / `CGPSEP` / `CENDINDEX` |`:index.`, typed `:i1 level= refids=.`, `:grpsep.`, `:eindex.` |Generated index stream. `CITERM` retains visible term, hierarchy level, and zero or more topic targets. `CENDINDEX` terminates the generated body; bytes/logical records after it are not topic content. |Verified against `SC31-711.boo`, `packet.boo`, and `GG24-4302-00.boo` generated indexes.
|`SI <index-term>` |suppressed; the whole line displays nothing |Subject-index metadata embedded in body flow. The same holds for `packet.boo` `3.2` and `QSYSNEWG.BOO` `1.2`.
|`CZ Flow <tag> <left> <indent> <text>` |`:<tag>.<text>` |Flowing paragraph/list/heading control. |`CZ Break <n> <text>` |`:p.<text>` when text is present |Break/layout control with optional visible text. |Source-style approximation.
|`CZ Off <tag>` |matching end tag for known block tags, otherwise suppressed |End/disable layout mode. |Verified for table controls against hosted BookServer output; see the table/layout evidence below.
|`CZ Off XMP` / `CZ Off EXMP` |`:xmp.` / `:exmp.` with `:xline.` records for intervening visible lines |Literal example/preformatted mode. Visible text between the controls is line-preserving; style-only `CFONT` records inside the block do not create blank lines. |Verified against `packet.boo` topic `3.2` and source `packet.script` `:xmp.` blocks for `/etc/ax25/axports`, pass:[<literal>/etc/ax25/</literal><literal>nrbroadcast</literal>], and `/etc/ax25/rsports`.
|`ST` reflow-off body followed by inline continuation controls |`:xmp inline='html'.` / `:xline.` / `:exmp.` when the fixed block contains inline markup |Generated fixed-width topic-body blocks can span logical records that contain inline `CFONT` or `CSELECT` controls. |Same-target `CSELECT` spans split across fixed display rows |Separate adjacent semantic links with the complete visible phrase preserved |A physical row boundary can divide one human-readable reference into multiple selector objects. Preserve each selector and normalize only the display whitespace; target equality alone does not prove that selectors should be collapsed. |`SC31-711.boo` topic `5.0`, logical records 172--173, selects `Chapter 2, "Problem` at column 56 and `Determination" in topic 2.0` at column 3 on the continuation row. BookServer emits two adjacent `2.0#HDRPROBS` anchors. `QSYSNEWG.BOO` `PREFACE` and `GG24-4302-00.boo` `NOTICES` independently require separate same-target selectors.
l|CITERM <term><sep><level>[<sep><target>...] l|:i1 level='<level>' refids='<targets>'.<term> |Generated index entry. Level is positive hierarchy depth and is not visible text. |`CGPSEP <fields>` |`:grpsep.<fields>` |Index group separator. |Source-style approximation.
|Other `C...` controls |suppressed |Generated or unresolved control-like words. |Fallback behavior for source-style raw output.
|Plain text span |`:p.<text>`, or `:pinline.<text>` for marker-led wrapped continuations |Remaining decoded prose after known controls are separated. |===

[[markup_structural_st_titlebody_form]]
==== Structural ST Title/Body Form

`GG24-4302-00.boo` logical record 6 contains the complete `ABSTRACT` topic in
one decoded control stream:

----
... CHDLEVEL :ABSTRACT ... CSOURCEFN 4302ABST ...
ST  Abstract .    This document is unique ... ?    assumed.?    (217 pages)
----

The `ST` control token must be recognized at a token boundary. In particular,
the trailing letters `ST` in the preceding source filename `4302ABST` are not a
control. After locating the real control, parse this form as follows:

. Match the TOC title (`Abstract`) at the beginning of the `ST` operand.
. Consume the following whitespace.
. If the next character is a standalone `.` followed by whitespace or the end
of the operand, consume the dot and following whitespace as a title/body
delimiter.
. Preserve the remaining fixed-layout body, including decoded row and
paragraph boundaries. Structural topic kinds such as `:abstract` do not
imply that the `ST` suffix is empty.

If the suffix begins with another decoded control (`CTOCDEF`, `CFONT`,
`CIDELM`, and so on), it is a continuation of the control stream rather than a
fixed visible body. Parse it as controls; do not project their spellings as
preformatted text. This distinction is required by the `CONTENTS`, `EDITION`,
and `INDEX` topics in the same fixture.

Physical evidence is BOO page 72, local compact record 0. The compact length
byte is at page offset `0x0004` (file offset `0x48004`), the 53-byte payload
starts at page/file offset `0x0005` / `0x48005`, and begins:

----
1d 10 35 54 b6 d1 c1 1d 00 2d 59 d6 6e 54 dc 4d
00 20 00 b6 d1 c1 33 ea 2d 00 1f 02 e4 e9 0f 10
----

BookServer renders the body inside `<pre width="80">`, with blank rows between
its four prose paragraphs and the final `(217 pages)` row.

Decoded wrapped lines can also carry the same printable line-marker byte inside
visible text fields, not only as standalone plain text spans. In `packet.boo`
topic `1.1`, examples include pass:[<literal>*&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;or affordable-to-construct</literal>], pass:[<literal>!&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;connect their sites</literal>], pass:[<literal>$&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;system</literal>], and pass:[<literal>-&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;equipment</literal>]; topic `1.3` adds pass:[<literal>)&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;? TNC...</literal>] and pass:[<literal>:&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;? As such...</literal>]. Hosted BookServer suppresses these marker bytes and
keeps only the wrapped prose. Source-style projection therefore removes a
leading marker when it is followed by at least two whitespace bytes, or when a
marker is stranded at the end of a wrapped segment, and appears at the start of
a visible text run or after whitespace. This keeps ordinary punctuation such as
`(which preceded...)` intact while matching the reader's line-wrap behavior.

Notice-page topics use a small set of BookMaster source tags that are visible
in `BOO/packet.script` and recoverable from the decoded controls. The `EDITION`
topic has this source sequence:

----
:vnotice.
:vnhd.First Edition, January 27, 2026
:note.This is the initial version of this document, so it is likely to
have all manner of errors.
:coprnote.&copr. Evie Cooper 2026
:coprext.Not for use where life or limb may be at risk.
:evnotice.
----

Decoded `BOO/packet.boo` evidence:

[cols="<45,<45,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Decoded evidence |Source-style projection |Rendering implication

|Topic header `CHDLEVEL :VNOTICE ... ST Edition Notice` |`:vnotice.` |Starts a visual notice topic; the `ST` value is topic metadata, not the visible first body line.
|First body `CFONT` after `:vnotice.` with HP2 spans over `First Edition, January 27, 2026` |`:vnhd.First Edition, January 27, 2026` |Render the visual notice heading as emphasized/bold text.
|`CZ FLOW NOTE ...` followed by `CFONT ... Note: ...` |`:note.<body>` |BookServer renders the generated `Note:` label in bold. The decoded `CFONT` span covers the label, not the whole note body.
|Copyright token word `0x00a9` followed by author/year text |`:coprnote.© Evie Cooper 2026` |Render the copyright sign as text. See <<encoding>> for the token-word mapping.
|Following `CZ BREAK 3 Not for use where life or limb may be at risk.` after a copyright note |`:coprext.Not for use where life or limb may be at risk.` |Render as the copyright-note extension, with a line break after the copyright line rather than a new unrelated paragraph.
|===

Generated fixed-row visual notices preserve paragraph structure in their
spacing and display markers rather than in explicit `CZ FLOW` controls.
`GG24-4302-00.boo` topic `EDITION`, logical record 4, is a verified example:
the decoded row has paragraph boundaries after stream offsets 193, 479, 645,
831, 1010, and 1427.  A 63-byte `?` display run begins at offset 918 inside
the mailing-address block.  A padded `(` precedes the final information-license
paragraph, and the fixed notice body ends with `*`.  BookServer does not display
those two marker bytes.  The recovered source-style projection is the edition
heading followed by separate applicability, ordering, feedback, address, and
license paragraphs; display-row breaks within the address remain line breaks.
The final `*` is also a hard boundary before the following copyright record, so
an inline-font copyright continuation must not merge into the license paragraph.

[[markup_source_owned_fixed_layout_evidence_in_sc31_711]]
=== Source-Owned Fixed Layout Evidence In SC31-711

The following observations are facts about the encoded token stream in
`BOO/SC31-711.boo`; the proposed row interpretation is called out separately.
See <<logical-controls_payload_indexing_and_encoded_token_identity>>
for the exact payload ranges and token threshold.

[[markup_box_drawing_form_in_logical_records_74__75]]
==== Box-drawing form in logical records 74--75

Topic `2.4.4`, `AIX NetView/6000 Considerations`, contains a fixed form whose
structure survives dictionary resolution as Unicode token words.  The following
raw reference-to-token mappings occur in logical records 74 and 75:

[cols="<17,>8,<21,<44",options="header"]
|===
|Encoded reference |Width |Resolved token words |Observed role in the decoded form

|`03` |1 |`U+2666` |Repeated non-text symbol in the form stream.
|`84` |1 |`U+2502` |Repeated vertical stroke.
|`d8 22` |2 |`U+250C` |Top-left junction.
|`d8 21` |2 |`U+2510` |Top-right junction.
|`d8 23` |2 |`U+2514` |Bottom-left junction.
|`d8 24` |2 |`U+2518` |Bottom-right junction.
|`d8 36` |2 |`U+251C` |Left-side junction.
|`d8 38` |2 |`U+2524` |Right-side junction.
|`d8 35` |2 |`U+252C` |Top/interior junction.
|`d8 37` |2 |`U+2534` |Bottom/interior junction.
|`d8 25` |2 |`U+253C` |Interior crossing.
|`d8 31` |2 |43 copies of `U+2500` |Long horizontal run.
|`d8 2d` |2 |28 copies of `U+2500` |Medium horizontal run.
|`d8 2c` |2 |26 copies of `U+2500` |Short horizontal run.
|`d8 2a` |2 |22 copies of `U+2500` |Shorter horizontal run.
|===

The top rule in logical record 74 is the source-token sequence `d8 22`,
`d8 31`, `d8 35`, `d8 2d`, `d8 21`, with spacing-control tokens between some
of those references.  Logical record 75 ends with the corresponding bottom
sequence `d8 23`, `d8 31`, `d8 37`, `d8 2d`, `d8 24`.  These paired corners,
junctions, vertical strokes, and fixed-length horizontal runs verify that the
code points carry physical form structure in this fixture.  They are not
ordinary prose characters and must remain associated with their source tokens
until row/cell ownership has been recovered.

`U+2666` occurs elsewhere in topic metadata as well as among form rows.  Its
presence alone does not prove a checkbox, ballot, row boundary, or any other
specific source-level construct.  Mapping a particular occurrence to visible
`[ ]` is a renderer decision that needs positional and BookServer evidence.

[[markup_repeated_row_control_signatures_in_logical_records_19__23]]
==== Repeated row-control signatures in logical records 19--23

Two compact raw-token signatures repeat around the fixed-width directory and
process lists.  All bytes below are one-byte token references because the
SC31-711 threshold is `0xd8`:

[cols="<9,<40,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Signature |Raw reference bytes |Resolved words relevant to geometry

|A |`09 17 00 7a 00 17 00` |three spaces, `/`, spacing control `1`, `usr`, spacing control `1`, `/`, spacing control `1`
|B |`01 00 ww 09` where `0x0b <= ww <= 0x20` |`.` with prefix `1`, control-only prefix `1`, a variable-width marker/space token, then three spaces
|===

Direct payload scanning gives these counts and locations:

[cols=">14,>11,>45,<31",options="header"]
|===
|Logical record |Signature A |Signature B |First/last matching file offset

|19 |11 |1 (`ww=17`) |`0x0000b36b` / `0x0000b4a6`
|20 |7 |0 |`0x0000b4c0` / `0x0000b56e`
|21 |9 |0 |`0x0000b61a` / `0x0000b715`
|22 |0 |3 (`ww=18,14,12`) |`0x0000b87a` / `0x0000b8aa`
|23 |0 |14 (pass:[<literal>ww=0e,17,15,</literal><literal>0f,0f,0f,12,</literal><literal>0f,0b,0e,10,</literal><literal>14,0f,13</literal>]) |`0x0000b8e6` / `0x0000ba0d`
|===

The decoded `CFONT` header in logical record 19 has spans `(3,9)`, `(28,4)`,
`(33,2)`, and `(36,5)` and labels `Directory` / `Type of Files`; records
19--20 contain 18 directory rows.  The header in logical record 22 has spans
`(3,7)`, `(11,4)`, and `(18,11)` and labels `Process Name` / `Description`;
records 22--23 contain 19 process rows.  The raw signatures recur at the
physical row starts and retain boundaries that disappear after ordinary text
flattening.

It is a renderer-supported hypothesis, not a verified IBM format definition,
that signatures A and B are two general row-control grammars.  The evidence
justifies using them only together with repeated column geometry and a
multi-column `CFONT` header.  Logical record 21 independently contains nine A
signatures in topic `1.2`, showing why neither signature nor `CFONT` alone is a
safe semantic-table activation rule.  The meanings of `ww` and the printable
punctuation produced by some marker tokens remain unresolved.

Signature B also marks paragraph starts inside flattened `CFONT` prose. In
`SC31-711.boo` topic `4.1.3`, logical record 105 stores `resource` (token 6),
`.` with spacing prefix `1` (token 7), a control-only prefix-`1` token (token
8, BOO byte `0x10da7`), the width-1 marker token `as` (token 9, byte
`0x10da8`), and a three-space origin (token 10) before `The traps in this section`; tokens 61--63 repeat the pattern as `agent`, `.`, control-only `1`,
`bridge`, three spaces before `The redirected traps`.  BookServer renders a
blank line at both positions.  Wrapped rows in the same records (`critical < state`, pass:[<literal>Version (&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;2.0</literal>]) carry the glyph marker after an ordinary inserted
space and no control-only token, and BookServer joins them.  The same
control-only token followed by ordinary text (`OS`+`/`+`2`, `Note`+`:`,
`2`+`.`+`0`) is inter-token attachment, not a boundary.  A control-only token
that attaches a wide padding run after sentence punctuation (`actions.` +
control-only `1` + 18 spaces + `When`, logical record 104 tokens 175--180) is
the in-row form of the same blank line.  `libgeist` uses exactly this
geometry to restore the 4.1.3 introduction
paragraphs and to keep `?`-run and record-continuation soft wraps (topic
`4.4` messages 1 and 2, topic `4.1.3` messages 329 and 444) inside their
paragraph.

Two refinements of signature B were established while typing the remaining
SC31-711 SRMSG trap catalogs (topics `4.1.1`--`4.3.4` and `4.4`) against the
hosted BookServer rendering:

* The sentence stop is part of the signature.  A control-only prefix-`1`
token after an unterminated word followed by a width-1 slot and three
spaces attaches a wrapped display line of the same paragraph: topic `4.3.4`
logical record 145 stores `bridge`, control-only `1`, `can`, three spaces,
`application.` and BookServer renders one paragraph; the same record ends
`...21.5.1` before a control-only token and logical record 146 continues
`enterprise ID` in the same paragraph.
* A width-1 dictionary word or sentence-punctuation slot at the exact
three-space origin is the row boundary control only when the origin token is
followed directly by the row text (`actions.` `can` three spaces `The`,
`in this` `:` three spaces `SIC`).  When a padding token or a decoder
placeholder follows the origin, the slot word is the wrapped last word of the
previous display line and BookServer renders it: topic `4.2.2` logical
record 134 `send the` `trap` three spaces + four-space padding `to AIX`;
topic `4.4` logical record 165 `segment` `.` three spaces + separator
placeholder `Segment is resynchronized.`.  A lone layout glyph (`< ( ) - / > = "`) that closes its decoded segment or precedes a padding run of two or
more spaces is a boundary slot whose origin the next control swallowed
(`each of /` before `CFONT 25 3 C` in topic `4.4`), unless it closes a
delimiter the same segment left open (`256 (snmp_br_dot1dStpPortState)`).

A third refinement separates the hanging last word of a display line from a
row-boundary control whose one-byte value happens to resolve to a dictionary
word.  Neither the encoded value nor the spelling decides it (`N2AH1MST.BOO`
value `28` = `access`, `27` = `a`, `42` = `continues` are boundary controls,
while value `90` = `the`, `87` = `system`, `56` = `is` are text; in
`SC31-711.boo` value `28` = `a` and `37` = `are` are text).  The evidence is
the trailing line fill: a hanging word is followed by _two_ space-run tokens,
the fill of the line it ends and the origin run of the next line.  Topic `18.0`
logical record 1555, BOO byte `0x93fb5`: `5a 0c 10 57` = `the`, 7-space fill,
14-space origin, `system` (hosted line `... in a background job step, the` is
73 columns, 73 + 7 = 80); byte `0x93fd8`: `57 10 10` = `system`, fill, origin.
`SC31-711.boo` logical record 106 byte `0x10fe3`: `38 0c 09` =
`correlation`, 6-space fill, 3-space origin; record 152 byte `0x147e9`:
`25 12 09` = `are`, 18-space fill, 3-space origin; record 153 token 295
`was` + 1-space fill + 3-space origin.  A boundary control is followed by the
origin run alone: `N2AH1MST.BOO` byte `0x93f2c`: `ea 75 1c 0d d6 75` =
`virtual`, `access`, 10-space origin, `lookaside` (hosted: `start the virtual lookaside facility (VLF)`); topic `23.0` record 2038 byte `0xbd9cb`:
`SETROPTS` `access` 10-space origin `MLACTIVE(FAILURES)`; topic `26.0` record
2251 byte `0xcffd9`: `security` `a` 10-space origin `administrator.`.  The
layout glyphs `( ) - / = <` in the same slot show the same single-run shape
(`01 00 14 0d` = `.`, control-only, `(`, 10 spaces before `start VLF`).
Checked against the hosted pages of N2AH1MST topics `4.0`, `18.0`, `22.0`,
`23.0`, `26.0` and SC31-711 `4.1.3`, `4.4` (about 650 marker slots): every
slot with the two-run shape whose phrase could be located is hosted text, and
no single-run slot word appears in hosted text except numeric return-code
labels inside fixed tables (pass:[<literal>12&#160;&#160;Unable to</literal>]), which a
reader should leave untyped (fail closed).

List items inside a record continuation are not soft wraps even though the
record geometry continues the display run.  `N2AH1MST.BOO` topic `22.0`
records 1984 and 1985 each open `14 0d 03 07` = `(`, 10-space origin, the
decoder-separator sentinel (BookServer's `°` bullet), 2 spaces, `Increase` /
`Decrease`; topic `23.0` record 2009 opens `18 0d` + `1`, `.` with spacing
prefix `1`, a 1-space run, `During` (hosted pass:[<literal>1.&#160;&#160;During the index search</literal>]).
Genuine continuation wraps in the same book (`4.0` record 287 `:` 10 spaces
`modified.`, `16.0` record 1280 `-` 10 spaces `provided by the MVS`) carry no
such prefix.

The catalog labels themselves come from `CFONT` span geometry, not from a word
list: in every entry the leading operand triples that start at the catalog
origin column (`3`) and follow one another with single-space gaps (`3 3 2 7 3 2 11 3 2 15 9 2` = `LNM` `for` `AIX` `Response:`, `3 12 2` = `Description:`,
`3 7 2` = `Action:`) spell one label when the covered text ends with `:`; the
first such chain of an entry that does not end with `:` and covers the whole
line is the entry headline (`3 1 2 5 19 2` = `1 (fddiRPUNoResponse)`).  A
chain that begins with the entry's own message id is the headline even when
it ends with `:`.  `N2AH1MST` topic `15.0` opens its first entry, record 757,
with line 10 `SRMSG CBP100I`, line 12
`cfont 3 7 2 11 8 2 20 10 2 31 8 2 41 7 2 49 5 2 55 9 2 65 7 2` and line 13
`   CBP100I VALIDATE PROCESSING ASSUMED.  INVALID INPUT PARAMETER STRING:`.
No label has appeared yet, so the label column (`10` in this book) is not
yet known.  Hosted (DT `19910329000100`) serves line 13 and its
continuation line `{string|NONE SPECIFIED}` as the headline of `CBP100I`.
In N2AH1MST labels stand at column `10`, right of the headline origin, and
that column also separates a colon-ending headline from a label in later
entries.  A label stands two blanks before its value, and the same `cfont`
can go on to style that value.  The label then ends at the span whose text
ends with `:` and is followed by a gap of two or more columns; spans one
blank apart (`System` `Action:`) are words of one label.  `N2AH1MST` record
1464 line 16 is `cfont 10 12 2 24 7 V 39 11 V`, and line 17 draws
`          Explanation:  ctcname module module-name stops processing when a`
with `Explanation:` in cells 10--21 and `ctcname` in cells 24--30.  Hosted
`16.0` (DT `19910329000100`) serves
`<B>Explanation:</B>  <var>ctcname</var> module <var>module-name</var>` as the
`Explanation:` field of entry `CBR9915I`.  Such a label is drawn in one
style (`2` here) and its value opens in another (`V`).  Two shapes with
the same geometry are not labels.  A headline continuation row such as
`2.0`'s `          prm:  text` mixes styles before its colon (`V`, `2`,
then `V`), and hosted serves `<var>prm</var><B>:</B>  <var>text</var>`
inside the headline.  A cited title such as `26.0`'s
`Catalog Administration:  Access Method Services Reference` (a row that
opens on `Catalog`, all `cite`) keeps one style
across its colon, and hosted serves it inside its paragraph.  The
ordered label sequence repeated by every entry that carries fields is the
catalog vocabulary (an entry that shares the next entry's fields, below,
has none of its own); a
labelled line that only some entries carry (`3 5 2` `Note:` in topic `4.4`
entry `805306372`) is entry-local.  A field can restate the label of the
field directly before it, and both are fields; the entry still carries
that label once in its sequence.  `N2AH1MST` entry `AVM035I` draws
`System Action:` under `cfont 10 6 2 17 7 2 ...` on record 669 line 29 and
again on line 41, with no other label between.  Hosted `11.0`
(DT `19910329000100`) serves two `<B>System</B> <B>Action:</B>` paragraphs.
No label is observed to return after a different field.

Some catalogues have no label that every entry repeats.  Their entries then
fall into two kinds.  Some draw a label sequence that is a strict ordered
subsequence of another entry's sequence (the same labels, in the same order,
with some left out).  The rest repeat a common sequence.  `N2AH1MST` topic
`23.0` (DT `19910329000100`) has 130 entries:

[cols="<22,<38,<40",options="header"]
|===
|Entry |Source |Labels

|`ICH404I` |`SRMSG` at `0xBA8D9` (record 2003 line 4); label `cfont` lines at `0xBA947` (line 11), `0xBAA06` (line 24), `0xBAA21`, `0xBAA4D`, `0xBAA67` (record 2004 lines 2, 6, 9) |`Explanation:`, `System Action:`, `Operator Response:`, `Application Programmer Response:`, `Problem Determination:`; no `Source:`
|`ICH408I` (`LOGON/JOB INITIATION - NOT AUTHORIZED TO APPLICATION`) |`SRMSG` at `0xBCAF3` (record 2027 line 18); `cfont 10 6 2 17 7 2` at `0xBCB2F` (line 23), `cfont 10 4 2 15 9 2` at `0xBCB52` (line 27) |`System Action:`, `User Response:`; no `Explanation:`
|other `ICH408I` entries |  |`Explanation:`, `Source:`, `System Action:`, `User Response:`
|===

Each label is drawn the way every other label in the topic is drawn: a
`cfont` run at column 10 that ends in `:`, followed by the value.  Hosted
`23.0` serves exactly those fields for each entry.  The second `ICH408I`
entry is a subsequence of the last row.  `ICH404I` is a subsequence of no
entry, since no other entry draws `Problem Determination:`.  Without the
subsequence entries, every entry still repeats `Explanation:`, and that is
the vocabulary.  `SC09-138` topic `H.0` (DT `19910321130500`) is the same
case: 72 entries draw only `Recovery:` and 15 only `Explanation:`, against
266 with `Explanation:` and `Recovery:`.  A catalogue whose entries share
no label even this way, such as an entry whose only label no other entry
draws, has no vocabulary.
A `CFONT` with an empty payload at a
record end takes the next record's leading text segment as its payload (topic
`4.4` logical records 153/154 `LNM for AIX Response:` and 154/155 `7 (fddiControlCartridgeError)`).
The next record's first display line is that payload even when its length
byte happens to spell a control word.  `N2AH1MST` record 657 ends with line
37, `cfont 3 7 2 11 9 2 21 4 V ...`, directly after `SRMSG AVM007I`.  Record
658 opens at `0x492F1` with `25`, the length (37) of line 0, whose dictionary
word is `cfont`; then `08` (a three-cell blank run) and `AVM007I` (`B0 3E`
at `0x492F3`).  The byte is the row's length, not a control, and hosted
`11.0` (DT `19910329000100`) serves line 0,
`AVM007I SUBSYSTEM ssid ASID asid IS NOW THE {ACTIVE|BACKUP} ELEMENT OF RSE`,
as the entry's headline under that `cfont`.  A record that opens on such a
row with no `cfont` waiting at the end of the record before it is drawn text
ahead of the headline, and no entry is read from it.

A catalogue can hold a single entry.  `SC34-425` topic `2.4.29`
(`FLMTMJI Interface to JOVIAL Compiler`) has one `SRMSG`, `SRMSG 64`, after
its introduction.  Its headline is `cfont 8 2 2` over `   ____ 64 ____...`,
and its fields are `Explanation:`, `User Response:` and `Project
Administrator Response:`, as in the sibling topics that list several return
codes.  Hosted (DT `19921112160049`) serves it as it serves those siblings,
`<a name="MSG 64">` and the bolded labels.  The number of entries does not
decide whether a topic is a section-label catalogue.

Two messages can share one set of fields.  The first entry is then its
headline alone, and the next `SRMSG` stands on the display line directly
after that headline, with no rule row of its own.  `SC09-138` topic `G.0`,
record 2071:

[cols="<10,<90",options="header"]
|===
|Line |Content

|10 |`SRMSG EDC0762 10`
|11 |the entry's rule row (`   ____...`)
|12--13 |the headline `cfont` and `   EDC0762 10 A text deck marked fetchable was not specified`
|14 |`SRMSG EDC0763 40`, with no rule row after it
|15--16 |the headline `cfont` and `   EDC0763 40 Error in loading segment EDCCOMP RC=&1`
|18--19 |`cfont 3 12 2` and `   Explanation:  An attempt to load segment EDCCOMP failed.`
|===

Hosted G.0 (DT `19910321130500`) serves `<a name="MSG EDC0762 10"><hr>`,
EDC0762's headline, then `<a name="MSG EDC0763 40">` around EDC0763's
headline with no `<hr>` and no `<p>` between the two, then the
`Explanation:` field.  The field belongs to both messages.  Topic `H.0`
record 2103 lines 24--28 are the same shape (`EDC0132`, then `SRMSG
EDC0133` on line 28).  So is record 2110 lines 20--24 (`EDC0148`, then
`SRMSG EDC0150`), where the second `SRMSG`'s line is followed by the length
byte `2D` (45) of line 25, whose dictionary word is `@`.  A severity after
the message id (`40`) is on the `SRMSG`'s own line; neither it nor a
following length byte is a rule row.

Several headline-only entries can follow each other.  They all share the
fields of the entry that ends the run.  `H.0` record 2112 line 3 is
`SRMSG EDC0166` (length byte `03` at `0x10D34E`), then a rule row and the
headline `cfont` with `   EDC0166 30 A non-lvalue array cannot be
subscripted.` (lines 4--6).  Line 7 is `SRMSG EDC0167` (`03` at
`0x10D387`), with no rule row, and its headline is on lines 8--9.
`EDC0168` (lines 10--12) and `EDC0169` (lines 13--15) are the same.
Line 16 is `SRMSG EDC0170`, and its headline (lines 17--18) is followed by
an empty line, `cfont 3 12 2` and `Explanation:` (lines 19--21).  Hosted H.0
(DT `19910321130500`) serves `<a name="MSG EDC0166"><hr>`, then the five
headlines one per line, each of the last four inside its own
`<a name="MSG EDC016n">`, then `<p>` and EDC0170's `Explanation:` and
`Recovery:`.  A run that is not ended by an entry with fields has nothing
to share.

A headline-only entry is not this shape when anything else follows it.
An entry can instead draw a closed box after its headline, with no
envelope control around it.  `H.0` record 2199 line 30 to record 2200
line 3 is entry `EDC0553` (`SRMSG` at `0x1146F9`), a rule row and a
two-row headline.  Line 4 is `c.cc 50` and line 5 is empty.  Then:

[cols="<10,<30,<60",options="header"]
|===
|Line |Length byte |Content

|6 |`07` at `0x114756` |`cfont 16 3 2 20 8 2` (`cfont` `3A` at `0x114757`)
|7 |`0F` (15) at `0x11475E` |`┌───` (U+250C at column 11, word `B0 46` at `0x114760`), `TPF Messages` (columns 16--18 and 20--27, the two runs), U+2500 rules, and U+2510 at column 76 (`B0 44` at `0x11476C`)
|8 |`05` at `0x11476E` |U+2502 at column 11 (`6C` at `0x114770`) and at column 76 (`6C` at `0x114773`), blanks between
|9--13 |  |two `cfont` lines over `│ EDC0700 to EDC0715 are TPF messages and will only be generated │` and `│ by the C/370 compiler if you are running in a TPF environment. │`, and an empty `│ ... │` row
|14 |`08` at `0x1147F9` |U+2514 at column 11 (`B0 4A` at `0x1147FB`), U+2500 rules, and `─┘` ending in U+2518 at column 76 (`B0 61` at `0x114800`)
|15 |`00` at `0x114802` |empty line
|16 |`03` at `0x114803` |`SRMSG EDC0700` (`SRMSG` at `0x114804`), with no rule row after it
|===

The top row starts and ends with the two upper corners, every later row
draws U+2502 in the same two columns and nothing outside them, and the
bottom row has the two lower corners there.  Only `cfont` lines stand
between those rows.  Hosted H.0 (DT `19910321130500`) serves the box inside
`EDC0553`, after its headline and a `<p>`: the rows are drawn as the record
has them, with the box glyphs as ASCII (`___`, `|`), and with each `cfont`
run in bold (`<B>TPF</B> <B>Messages</B>`, then every word of the note).
`EDC0553` has no field.  The box belongs to the entry: it draws no
`Explanation:` or `Recovery:` of its own, and the next message,
`EDC0700`, has its own.

A label does not require a non-empty value.  In `N2AH1MST` topic `10.0`,
logical record 593 segment 2 is `cfont 10 6 2` and its complete native row is
the label `value:` (the row marker is at byte `0x43D5D`); segment 3 starts the
separate `Explanation:` field.  The empty `value:` row therefore remains a
field with an empty body rather than becoming part of either adjacent field.

Every span of an entry's `cfont` keeps its own style, in the headline as
in a field.  The headline is wholly highlighted, but not wholly bold.
`N2AH1MST` topic `2.0` record 151 line 9 is the `cfont` at token 39
(`25` at `0x1E704`) whose spans `3 7 2 ... 39 6 2 46 3 V 50 3 2 ...` style
`   ADY001I THE DAE PARAMETER RECORD IN MEMBER mem HAS A SYNTAX ERROR ...`;
the span `46 3 V` is tokens 61--63 (`46` = `EE 6F` at `0x1E71B`, `3` =
`9A` at `0x1E71D`, `V` = `83` at `0x1E71E`) and covers `mem` (token 91,
`D7 88` at `0x1E747`).  Hosted (DT `19910329000100`) serves
`<B>MEMBER</B> <var>mem</var> <B>HAS</B>`: a `V` span is a variable inside
the headline, not part of its bold.  The same holds in a field:
`28.0` serves `use the <B>DELETE</B> <B>'</B><var>sys1.vvols.vxxxxxx</var><B>'</B>`
and `the variable <var>sfierror</var> in message IEC331I`.

A continuation row of the headline or of a field can stand deeper than
both the catalogue's origin and its label column, and its `cfont` still
styles it, starting at the row's first drawn cell.  Record 151 line 11 is
the `cfont` at token 99 (`25` at `0x1E754`): `10` (`85`), `3` (`9A`), `V`
(`83`), `13` (`88`), `1` (`84`), `2` (`8F`), `16` (`8B`), `4` (`A2`), `V`
(`83`).  The row below it is a ten-cell blank run (`0D` at `0x1E75F`),
`prm` (`DC E2` at `0x1E760`) in cells 10--12, `:` in cell 13 and `text` in
cells 16--19.  Hosted serves `<var>prm</var><B>:</B>  <var>text</var>` as
the second line of the `ADY001I` headline.  Line 13 (`cfont` at token 118,
`0x1E768`) is `cfont 14 4 2 19 2 2 22 2 2 25 7 2 33 3 2 37 4 2` over
`              THIS IS AN INVALID DAE VERB`, a row of the
`text is one of the following:` field, served as
`<B>THIS</B> <B>IS</B> <B>AN</B> <B>INVALID</B> <B>DAE</B> <B>VERB</B>`.
The row's cells decide: each span names the cells of its word, begins and
ends on a drawn glyph and meets a blank, the row's edge or the next span.
`16.0` serves `<var>parms</var><B>.</B>` and `<B><I>libname</B></I>` on
such rows the same way.  The run so read opens on the row's first drawn
glyph: a span that starts further right does not open it, even when its
glyphs repeat the row's first characters.  `16.0` record 928 line 0 is
`cfont 36 1 V 61 4 V` over line 1,
`                        orientation o associated with volume vol1 in the`,
whose first glyph is `orientation` in cells 24--34 and whose cell 36 is the
standalone `o`; hosted serves `orientation <var>o</var>`, and cell 24 is
not styled although it is also `o`.

The headline and each field are separate paragraphs.  Hosted BookServer
serves `SC31-711` topic `4.1.1` (DT `19941010174546`) as `<B>0</B>`, `<p>`,
`<B>Description:</B>  coldStart`, `<p>`, and so on; no character stands
between the headline and the first label in the source or on the served page.

An entry can carry figure and footnote envelopes, and both stand where the
source draws them.  In `N2AH1MST` topic `10.0` (hosted DT `19910329000100`):

[cols="<14,<18,<34,<34",options="header"]
|===
|Record, line |Bytes |Content |Served as

|533, 15 |`02`, then `E4 87` at `0x3EDE8` (value 58503) |`SRFIGFIGUNIQ20` on a line of its own, inside entry `ATB101I` |`<a name="FIGFIGUNIQ20">` on the first drawn row that follows
|533, 16 - 534, 4 |— |alternating `cfont` lines and six drawn rows |the six rows verbatim, one per line, highlighted as their `cfont` lines state
|534, 5 |`02`, then `E4 7B` at `0x3EEEF` (value 58491) |`SREFIG` |nothing; it ends the figure, and the `Explanation:` field follows
|599, 12 |`02`, then `E4 8F` at `0x44496` (value 58511) |`SRFTNFTNUNIQ19`, between the native list rows `TPALIAS` and `TPDELETE` of entry `ATB348I` |`<a name="FTNFTNUNIQ19"><hr><h5>` at that point of the entry
|599, 13 |— |` \|  (*) VTAM is a trademark of the IBM Corporation.` |the footnote text
|599, 14 |`02`, then `E4 7D` at `0x444AF` (value 58493) |`SREFTN` |`</h5></a>`; no anchor of its own
|===

Each `cfont` line inside the figure styles the drawn row directly below it,
and its columns index that row's own display cells, counting the leading
blank and the revision bar.  Record 533 line 16 is
`cfont 12 6 2 19 4 2 25 8 2 34 4 2 40 7 2 48 4 2 54 11 2 66 4 2`, and line 17
draws ` |          ACTIVE LU'S  OUTBOUND LU'S  PENDING LU'S  TERMINATING LU'S`
with `ACTIVE` in cells 12--17 and `LU'S` in cells 19--22.  Hosted serves
`<B>ACTIVE</B> <B>LU'S</B>` at those columns.  Two spans can split one word:
line 20 is `cfont 12 6 2 18 8 V ...` over ` |          [LLUN=unitname ...`,
served as `<B>[LLUN=</B><var>unitname</var>`.  Every span begins and ends on
a drawn glyph and meets a blank, the row's edge or the next span.

The ` |` in front of these rows is the revision bar.  It is the same mark as
on the entry's reflowed rows: a one-cell blank, then a width-one `|` or
`U+2502` in cell 1, then a blank.  Its placement is the evidence.
`N2AH1MST` topic `28.0` serves
` |          100    Point the ACB pointer ...` for the rows revised in this
edition and `            87-98  Contact your ...` for the unrevised row
before them, inside one drawn region.  A rule that belongs to a drawing does
not have this shape.  `SC24-546` topic `A.0` draws its table rules at cell 3,
and the `packet` fixture topic `1.3` draws a left rule at cell 3 with a
matching right rule.

A bullet row keeps its bullet behind the revision bar.  Record 599 line 9
stores the length byte, a one-cell blank (`06` at `0x44485`), `U+2502`
(`61` at `0x44486`), a 17-cell blank run (`11` at `0x44487`), the bullet
`U+2666` (`03` at `0x44488`), a two-cell blank run (`07`) and `TPADD`
(`E8 42` at `0x4448A`).  Hosted serves ` |                  °   TPADD`, the
same bullet row as an unrevised list, so `TPADD` and `TPALIAS` are list
items.  So are the eleven rows after the three footnotes, `TPDELETE` through
`DBMODIFY` on lines 24--34.

[[markup_trap_revision_margin]]
The same margin stands in front of an entry's headline, its field labels and
the rows of the catalogue's introduction, and a `cfont` line's columns count
across it.  `SC34-425` (DT `19921112160049`) revises whole translator topics
this way.  Topic `2.4.21`, record 1602, lines 22--23:

[cols="<16,<12,<14,<58",options="header"]
|===
|Offset |Bytes |Line, cell |Content

|`0x1653F2` |`04` |22 |length byte, then `39 AA 88 93`: `cfont 8 1 2`
|`0x1653F7` |`09` |23 |length byte of the headline row
|`0x1653F8` |`0F` |23, cell 0 |a one-cell blank
|`0x1653F9` |`70` |23, cell 1 |`\|`, the revision bar
|— |— |23, cell 2 |the blank the reader draws after the bar's word
|`0x1653FA` |`AC 56` |23, cells 3--6 |a four-cell `U+2500` rule
|`0x1653FC` |`87` |23, cell 8 |`0`, the entry id (cell 7 is the blank after the rule)
|`0x1653FD` |`0C` |23, cells 10-- |a 63-cell `U+2500` rule
|===

Cell 8 is the `0` the `cfont` names.  The unrevised sibling `2.4.22`,
record 1604 line 49, draws `   ____ 0 ____...` under the same
`cfont 8 1 2`, with its `0` in the same cell.  The three margin cells are
there whether or not a bar is drawn, and the bar is not part of the entry.
Hosted serves `<a name="MSG 0"> | ____ <B>0</B> ____...</a>` for the revised
headline and ` | <B>User</B> <B>Response:</B>  None.` for record 1602 line
29 (` | User Response:  None.` under `cfont 3 4 2 8 9 2`): the bar stays in
the margin and the spans fall on `User` (cells 3--6) and `Response:`
(cells 8--16).

The bar is the margin only when the row's cells say so: a one-cell blank
token in cell 0, a single-word `|` or `U+2502` token in cell 1, a blank in
cell 2 and drawn cells after it.  A span that covers cell 1 makes the bar a
styled cell of the row instead.  The cells, not the stored words, also decide
what each span covers.  `APPENDIX1.5.3` record 2540 line 9 starts at
`0x1AE77E` with `16 0F 70 84 7D`: length 22, the blank, `|`, `User`,
`Response`.  Its `cfont 3 4 2 8 9 2 58 3 V 66 3 V` covers `User` and
`Response:`, and hosted serves
` | <B>User</B> <B>Response:</B>  Contact the project administrator with <var>aaa</var> ...`.
A reader that counts span columns from the first stored word
reads `| Us` for the first span, and a reader that drops the bar and then
counts from the first word kept reads a misaligned span the same way; only
the cells give the right run.

A one-word layout glyph inside an entry is drawn when its own display line
draws cells both before and after it.  A row break stands only at a display
line's length byte, so such a glyph cannot be the slot of one, whatever a
geometry heuristic suggests.  `APPENDIX1.5.3` record 2941 line 34 is
`   non-editable etc.)  which is incompatible with the status specified in
the`, with `)` (`22` at `0x1D0551`) then a one-cell blank and `which`.
Hosted serves `etc.)  which`.  The same holds for closing parentheses in
`N2AH1MST` topics `20.0`, `25.0`, `26.0` and `28.0` (`(a)  The relative key
positions are not equal.`, DT `19910329000100`), `SC24-546` `A.0` (`a period
(.)  in a name`) and `SC34-425` `2.4.19` (`PROJECT.GROUP.TYPE.CUQUAL.)  The
maximum length`).

A glyph that ends a field's row closes a bracket the field opened, even
where `cselect` lines split the field's rows between controls.
`APPENDIX1.5.3` entry `FLM04030` (record 2592, lines 26--31) draws
`by SCLM  (See Chapter 10, "Defining the Project Environment" in`,
`topic 2.1 for information on the correct key lengths for the VSAM` and
`data sets)` as an item of its return-code list, with `cselect 23 49
HDRJLIB` and `cselect 9 10 HDRJLIB` between the rows.  Hosted serves
`data sets)`.

A comma that ends a drawn row in front of a control line is that row's
text.  The comma is stored as an attach-control token (`02`: the attach
control `01` and `,`) inside the row's own display line, and the next line
opens with a control such as `cfont`.  `APPENDIX1.5.3` record 2827 line 11
is `   The codes for the PASCAL run-time error bbb are described in Chapter
20,`; its last token (147, `02` at `0x1C6B1F`) is that comma, and line 12
(length byte `14` at `0x1C6B20`) is `cfont 32 2 C 35 6 C ...`.  Hosted
(DT `19921112160049`) serves `Chapter 20,`.  A reader that splits the
record's words at `, <control>` loses this comma between two controls; the
display line framing puts it on line 11.  The same byte as a display line's
length is not drawn: `SC09-138` `F.1` record 2061 token 60 is `02`, the
length of line 5, whose dictionary word is `,`, and hosted F.1
(DT `19910321130500`) shows no comma after `incorrect format.`.  `N2AH1MST`
(DT `19910329000100`) has 32 such row-end commas in topics `3.0`, `6.0`,
`9.0`, `10.0`, `11.0`, `14.0`, `16.0`, `17.0`, `18.0`, `20.0`, `21.0` and
`28.0`, among them `Ucde,` and `symbol,` in `20.0` and `Planning,` in
`28.0`; hosted serves every one.

A run inside a field is checked against its own row's cells the same way.
That holds when its `cfont` closes one record and the row opens the next.
`APPENDIX1.5.3` record 2547 ends with line 33, `cfont 3 8 1 21 8 1`, in the
`Explanation:` field of entry `FLM01004`.  Record 2548 line 0 is
`   dddddddd in group bbbbbbbb.`, and its cells 3--10 and 21--28 are the two
variables.  Hosted serves `<I>dddddddd</I> in group <I>bbbbbbbb</I>.` there,
inside the field's paragraph.

A bullet can follow the bar with no blank token of its own, only the blank
the reader draws after the bar.  `2.4.15.1` record 1565 line 33 is the
blank, `|`, `U+2666`, a two-cell blank run and `Mismatched quotes ...`, with
the bullet in cell 3.  Hosted serves ` | °   Mismatched quotes ...`, a list
item like the unrevised one above it.

More facts about the introduction:

* A `cfont` row whose first span begins at the row's first drawn cell, at an
  indent other than the three-cell origin, does not by itself start a
  paragraph.  Consecutive rows at one indent, each under its own `cfont`, are
  lines of one paragraph unless an empty display line separates them.
  `2.4.12.4` record 1517 lines 5--16 are six `cfont 7 6 2` rows
  (`       LANG=A         Assembler only`, `       LANG=T ...`).  Hosted
  serves all six as consecutive lines between one pair of `<p>` marks.
  `SC24-546` `A.0` (DT `19940323131240`) is the same shape across a record
  boundary.  Record 967 lines 19--20 are `cfont 5 4 E ...` over
  `     HELP MSG DMSnnnE  (where nnn is the CMS error number`, and record 968
  lines 0--1 are `cfont 5 3 E ...` over
  `     and error type is either 'E' or'T')`.  The second row finishes the
  first row's sentence, and hosted serves both inside one `<p>`.
* Such a row boundary is a line break inside the paragraph, not a join of
  wrapped text, when the later row's `cfont` opens it (its first span begins
  at the row's first drawn cell; a margin revision bar is not a drawn cell)
  and either of two things holds.  Hosted serves every such row as a line of
  its own inside the one `<p>`.  Hosted prints every row of a `<pre>` topic
  on its own line, so it cannot by itself tell a deliberate line from a
  wrapped one; the two conditions separate them.  A row opened by its
  `cfont` after a row that filled the paragraph's width is wrapped text whose
  next word the source styles: `2.4.31` serves `see the <cite>DCF</cite>` /
  `<cite>SCRIPT/VS</cite> documentation`, one sentence across two rows.
  Columns below count from 0 at the row's first cell, and a row's end is the
  column after its last drawn cell.
** both rows are example rows: every drawn cell of each lies under an
   example (`E`, `X`, `4`) or variable (`V`) span of its own `cfont`, and at
   least one span is an example span.  Example text is never filled, so its
   row ends are the author's.  `SC24-546` `A.0` is this case: both rows
   above are wholly example text, and the first ends at column 57, the
   paragraph's widest row, so its length alone proves nothing.  So is
   `2.4.31` (DT `19921112160049`) record 1638 lines 4--7,
   `OPTIONS=(@@FLMUID/DEV(3800N8),...,CO,      C` (`cfont 20 50 E 76 1 E`,
   ending at column 77) over `B(7,7),M(DELAY,TRACE,ID))` (`cfont 20 25 E`),
   and the fourteen example rows and fifteen word-table rows of `2.4.15.1`
   (`123456*<-Column 7 ...`, `ACCEPT EVALUATE PERFORM`, ...; record 1561 line
   34 `cfont 27 6 E 42 6 E 57 7 E` styles record 1562 line 0
   `CANCEL GOBACK REWRITE`).
** the earlier row left room for the later row's first word: its last drawn
   cell, one blank and that word all end at or before the last drawn cell of
   the paragraph's widest row.  A filling formatter would have set the word
   on the earlier row, so the row was ended on purpose.  In `2.4.12.4` the
   `LANG=` rows end at columns 36, 47, 40 (the unstyled continuation
   `comment delimiters`), 27 and 26; each next `LANG=` word is six cells,
   and the paragraph's widest row (`LANG=R ... /* and */ as`) ends at
   column 70.  The continuation row itself is not opened by a `cfont` and
   joins the row above it.
* A one-word glyph that ends a display line is drawn text when it closes a
  bracket its sentence opened, whatever the next line's length byte spells.
  `2.4.15.1` record 1557 line 17 ends `line`, attach `00`, `)` (`22` at
  `0x161934`).  The next byte, `1F` at `0x161935`, is line 18's length byte,
  and its dictionary word is a 63-blank run.  Hosted serves
  `(treated as a comment line)`.  A glyph followed by more drawn cells on
  its own line is drawn text as well: record 1559 line 18 is
  ` |   001201-` + a 34-cell blank run + `BADCPY1 '.'`, served as
  `<samp>001201-</samp>`.  The same holds for a glyph that ends a record
  before the catalogue's first `SRMSG`.  `2.4.27` record 1627 ends with
  line 26, ` |     invoked:` (`invoked`, attach `00`, `:` = `27` at
  `0x1673C0`), and hosted serves `invoked:`.
* A row's length byte can spell a control word, and the row is still
  introduction text.  `SC31-711` topic `4.3.5` record 151 line 13 opens at
  `0x146F1` with `2D` (45), the row's length, whose dictionary word is
  `cbacklevel`.  Then come `09` (a three-cell blank run) and `the`.  The row
  is `   the 1.3.6.1.4.1.2.6.21.6.1 enterprise ID, which is associated with
  LNM for`, and lines 14 and 15 continue the sentence.  A reader that
  splits controls on dictionary words sees a `cbacklevel` control with
  operand `the`.  Hosted 4.3.5 (DT `19941010174546`) serves all three rows
  in the introduction's one paragraph, `the` included.  This is the same
  length-byte shape as an entry headline row whose length byte spells
  `cfont` (`N2AH1MST` record 658, above).
* An `SI` subject-index line can stand inside an entry.  `2.4.11.1` record
  1509 line 25 is the length byte `06` (`0x15DE1C`), `SI` (`7F` at
  `0x15DE1D`), `translators` (`E3 0B`), attach `02` + `,` and `FLMLPGEN`
  (`BE 44` at `0x15DE21`), after the last field of entry `599`.  Hosted
  shows nothing for it.  It is an index term of the entry: it draws
  nothing, and the field before it does not continue into it.  The line is
  the whole control; drawn rows after it are entry text again, where the
  source draws them.  `APPENDIX1.5.3` record 2845 lines 9--12 are an empty
  line, `SI SHR mode`, `SI modes, SHR` and the bullet row
  `   °   Free the data set so that it can be allocated in SHR mode.`, inside
  entry `FLM80020`'s `User Response:`.  Hosted serves nothing for either
  `SI` line and the bullet as the second item of that field's list, after
  `°   Allocate the required data set ...` on lines 7--8.  The next line's length
  byte is not such text, whatever its dictionary word.  `SC09-138` topic
  `F.1` record 2061 line 17 is `SI run-time, error messages, in a systems
  programming environment` (tokens 201--215); the next byte, `0B` at
  `0x109283`, is line 18's length (11), and its dictionary word is a run of
  26 `U+2500` rules.  Line 18 is the next `SI` line.  A reader that walks
  the words rather than the display lines sees a rule drawn after the term;
  the display lines show none, and hosted F.1 (DT `19910321130500`) draws
  nothing between the entry's last field and the next row.
* Every `cfont` line of the introduction styles the row directly below it,
  wherever on that row its spans fall, and each span keeps the style its
  code names (the code table above).  The spans are not limited to a run
  that opens the row at the catalogue's origin column.  `2.4.10.1` record
  1490 line 13 is the `cfont` at token 154 (`39` at `0x15C4BA`), then `70`
  (`EE AC`), `3` (`9E`) and `1` (`88`): one `HP1` span over cells 70--72 of
  line 14, ` | character string with no separator periods.  A
  separator period is not`, where `not` is token 173 (`56` at
  `0x15C4D5`).  Hosted (DT `19921112160049`) serves `<I>not</I>` in the
  middle of that paragraph.  Record 1494 line 32 is the `cfont` at token
  180 (`39` at `0x15CADE`), `3` (`9E`), `8` (`AA`), `P` (`7B` at
  `0x15CAE1`), over line 33, ` | @@FLMLIS` (`|` = `70` at `0x15CAE4`, `@@` =
  `28`, attach `00`, `FLMLIS` = `BE 3C` at `0x15CAE7`) in cells 3--10,
  left of the catalogue's origin column 8.  Hosted serves
  `<kbd>@@FLMLIS</kbd>`.  The same topic's `E` rows are served as `<samp>`
  word by word and its `2` rows (`Total Lines`) as `<B>`.  A span is read
  only through the cells it names: it begins and ends on a drawn glyph that
  meets a blank, the row's edge or the next span, exactly as in a drawn
  region above.  Only the row directly below the `cfont` line is styled;
  the rows after it that carry no `cfont` of their own are plain even
  where the same columns would fit a word.  `SC31-711` topic `4.1.3`
  (DT `19941010174546`) record 104 line 28 is
  `   state until the condition causing the critical state is resolved.`
  with no `cfont` line above it, and the record's one `cfont 3 5 2` (which
  styles `Note:`) would cover `state` in cells 3--7; hosted serves `state`
  plain.

[[markup_trap_introduction_lists]]
The introduction draws native lists the same way an entry does.  A
paragraph whose first display line opens with a bullet (a blank run, the
single word `U+2666`, a two-cell blank run, then text) is a list item; the
bullet's cell is the list's column.  A record can open on such a row.
`2.4.10.1` record 1490 line 0 starts at `0x15C3EA` with `19` (length 25),
`11` (a three-cell blank run), `03` (`U+2666`, cell 3), `10` (a two-cell
blank run) and `E6 95` (`When`, cell 7); record 1489 ended on an empty
line.  Line 1 (`       tokens on that line, ...`, text at cell 7) has no
empty line before it and continues the item.  Hosted
(DT `19921112160049`) serves both lines after one `°` inside one `<p>`.

A later paragraph whose first glyph stands right of the open list's
bullets is a further paragraph of the same item; one at or left of the
bullet column ends the list.  Record 1489 line 29 is `14` (length 20) at
`0x15C38A`, `15` (a seven-cell blank run) and `If` (`79` at `0x15C38C`,
cell 7), after an empty line that follows the bullet item
`When an uncommented, non-continuation line has COPY ...`.  Hosted serves
`If the member name is enclosed ...` in its own `<p>`, indented to the
item's text column.  Line 32 opens the next bullet at cell 3.  Bullets of
one list stand in one column.  In every introduction examined, each bullet
row follows an empty line; a nested introduction list (a bullet right of
an open list's column) is not observed.

[[markup_trap_introduction_selectors]]
A `cselect` line in the introduction marks cells of the display line after
it, exactly as inside an entry.  `2.4.32` record 1641 lines 30--32:

[cols="<10,<20,<70",options="header"]
|===
|Line |Bytes |Content

|30 |`05` at `0x168478`, `40` |`cselect 3 8 TBLADINFMT`
|31 |`06` at `0x16847E`, `40` |`cselect 50 11 FIGADAIN`
|32 |`19` at `0x168485` |`   Table 18 for a description of the ADAIN format.  Figure 88 shows a sample`
|===

Both selectors queue onto line 32.  Hosted serves
`<a href="...#TBLADINFMT">Table 18</a>` on cells 3--10 and
`<a href="...#FIGADAIN">  Figure 88</a>` on cells 50--60.

A line that only names a destination draws nothing, so a selector skips
it too.  `2.4.33` record 1647 line 6 is `cselect 33 11 FIGADAOUT` (`05` at
`0x168D2D`).  Line 7 is the length byte `02` at `0x168D33` and
`SRSPTTPST` (`DF 4F`), alone on its line.  Line 8 is
`   the format of the ADAOUT file.  Figure 89 shows a sample ADAOUT file.`
Hosted links `  Figure 89`, cells 33--43 of line 8.

A selector on the last line of its record marks the first line of the
next record.  `2.4.33` record 1648 ends with line 29, `cselect 13 9
FIGADAOUT` (`05` at `0x168FF2`).  Record 1649 line 0 begins at `0x169005`:
`16` (length 22), `11` (three blanks), `Notice` (`CD CE`), `in` (`4B`),
`Figure` (`BC DD`) and `89` (`EF 51`).  Hosted serves
`Notice in <a href="...#FIGADAOUT">Figure 89</a> that ...`; cells 13--21
are `Figure 89`.  A selector can name a destination in another topic.
`2.4.33` record 1650 line 0 is `cselect 3 25 FIGADAIN`, and hosted links
`Figure 88 in topic 2.4.32` to `2.4.32#FIGADAIN`.

The row after a destination line is drawn text.  `2.4.32` record 1641
line 33 is `02` + `SRSPTTPRE` (`DF 4D` at `0x1684A0`).  Line 34 is `05`
at `0x1684A2`, then `11` (three blanks), `input` (`C8 0B`), `list` (`50`)
and attach `01` + `.`.  Hosted serves
`<a name="SPTTPRE">   input list.</a>`, the end of the sentence that line 32
began.

[[markup_trap_introduction_figures]]
An `SRFIG<id>` ... `SREFIG` envelope in the introduction is drawn like one
inside an entry.  `2.4.32` record 1642 line 12 is `02` + `SRFIGADAIN`
(`DA 9D` at `0x16855F`).  Line 30 is `02` + `SREFIG` (`DA 8B` at
`0x16863F`).  Between them are the box rows of the sample and line 29,
`   Figure 88. ADAIN Example`.  Hosted serves those rows verbatim under
`<a name="FIGADAIN">`, caption included, and resumes the prose after
`SREFIG`.  `2.4.14.1`'s `SRFIGRSRVWD` envelope runs from record 1535
line 27 to record 1548 line 7 and is served the same way.

A list item's column is the bullet's cell on the drawn row, not the sum of
the stored words in front of it, because the row also draws blanks that no
token spells.  In `N2AH1MST` topic `28.0`, record 2297 line 15 is a
ten-blank run followed by the bullet.  Line 19 is ` `, `U+2502`, then a
seven-blank run (`0C` at `0xD3BFE`) before the bullet.  The row draws one
blank between the bar and the run, so both bullets stand at column 10 and
belong to one list.

A record boundary does not end a list.  Record 492 line 27 of topic `10.0`
opens the bullet `Create a new parmlib member with only the corrected`.
Record 493 line 0 continues it at column 14, deeper than the bullet, with
`Then ask the operator to enter a SET APPC command to process`.  Hosted
serves both rows in one item.  A record can also open on a bullet row.
Topic `17.0` record 1484 begins with a ten-blank run (`0D` at `0x8DEE7`),
`U+2666` (`03` at `0x8DEE8`) and a two-blank run before
`Not the first record in the member`.  This is the second item of the list
whose first item, `Missing`, closes record 1483 line 35.  No segment payload
covers the record's leading blank run and bullet, but the bullet is that
row's marker all the same.

A footnote envelope can also stand between an entry's `SRMSG` and its
headline.  `N2AH1MST` topic `21.0` (hosted DT `19910329000100`), record
1936:

[cols="<12,<26,<62",options="header"]
|===
|Line |Bytes |Content

|10 |— |`SRMSG ERB415I`
|11 |— |the entry's rule row
|13 |`02` at `0xB4F2D`, then `E4 9D` |`SRFTNFTNUNIQ31`
|14 |— |`    (*) PR/SM is a trademark of the IBM Corporation.`
|15 |`02` at `0xB4F49`, then `E4 7D` |`SREFTN`
|17 |`02` at `0xB4F4D`, then `E4 9F` |`SRFTNFTNUNIQ32`
|18 |— |`    (*) VM is a trademark of the IBM Corporation.`
|19 |`02` at `0xB4F64`, then `E4 7D` |`SREFTN`
|20--21 |— |the headline `cfont` and `   ERB415I sid:  [MEASUREMENT|DELAY] VALUES ...`
|===

Hosted serves `<a name="MSG ERB415I"><hr></a>`, then
`<a name="FTNFTNUNIQ31"><hr><h5> ... </h5></a>` and
`<a name="FTNFTNUNIQ32"><h5> ... </h5></a>`, then the headline.  The
footnotes stand after the entry's anchor and before its first row, as the
source orders them.  Only footnote envelopes are observed there; no drawn
text stands between an entry's `SRMSG` and its headline.  The `(*)` marks
that select these footnotes are in an earlier entry (record 1886 lines
38--41, `cselect 26 4 FTNFTNUNIQ31` and `cselect 46 4 FTNFTNUNIQ32`).

The figure and footnote ids are the opening opcode without `SR`; the closers
`SREFIG` and `SREFTN` are end markers and name no destination.  Two more
footnote envelopes follow on lines 16-19 and 21-23 of record 599, served in
the same place in source order.

A bare `SRMSG` inside an entry -- the opcode alone on its display line, with no
operand and no payload -- is likewise a destination where it stands:
`N2AH1MST` topic `2.0` serves `<a name="MSG">` at the end of its last entry,
immediately before the footnote `FTNFTNUNIQ16` that follows it in the source.

The operand of an `SRMSG` is on the `SRMSG`'s own display line.  A reader
that walks words across line ends can take the next line's length byte for
an operand.  `SC09-138` topic `F.1` record 2061 line 9 is the length byte
`01` and `SRMSG` (`77` at `0x1091C9`).  The next byte, `24` at `0x1091CA`,
is line 10's length (36), and its dictionary word is `;`.  Line 10 is a
`cfont` line.  The `SRMSG` is bare, and hosted F.1 (DT `19910321130500`)
serves `<a name="MSG">` there, not an entry `MSG ;`.

What follows a bare `SRMSG` inside an entry differs:

* A plain row continues the entry's text.  `SC24-546` topic `A.0` record 971
  ends with line 32, a bare `SRMSG`, after the last field of `DMSREX255T`.
  Record 972 line 0 is `   The following are the REXX error messages:`, and
  hosted A.0 (DT `19940323131240`) serves that row inside `<a name="MSG">`,
  before the next entry's rule.
* A styled row can open a heading between two groups of entries.  In
  `SC09-138` `F.1` the bare `SRMSG` above follows entry `IBM0537` and the
  destination line `SRHDRCICSMSG` (line 5).  Line 10 is
  `cfont 3 8 K 12 5 K 18 8 K ...` over line 11,
  `   Run-Time Error Messages under CICS or in a Systems Programming
  Environment`.  Nine `SI` lines follow (lines 12--20), then
  `cfont 4 7 2 12 7 2 27 7 2 35 4 2 40 4 2` over
  `    Message Format:        EDCKxxx text <&n> where:` (lines 21--22).  After
  that come three indented `EDCK - indicates ...` / `xxx - error message
  number` rows (record 2061 line 25 to record 2062 line 3).  Hosted serves the
  heading in italics (`<I>Run-Time</I> <I>Error</I> ...`) and the format rows
  in bold, each on its own line, inside `<a name="HDRCICSMSG">` and
  `<a name="MSG">`.  The rows are a heading for the `EDCK` entries that
  follow, not text of `IBM0537`.

The heading's bytes, in display lines (length byte first):

[cols="1,2,3",options="header"]
|===
|Record, line |Length byte |Content
|2061, 9 |`01` at `0x1091C8` |`SRMSG` alone (`77` at `0x1091C9`)
|2061, 10 |`24` (36) at `0x1091CA` |`cfont 3 8 K 12 5 K ...` (`cfont` `3A` at `0x1091CB`): eleven `K` runs
|2061, 11 |`18` (24) at `0x1091EF` |`   Run-Time Error Messages under CICS or in a Systems Programming Environment`; every word lies in a run
|2061, 12--20 |`13` (19) at `0x109208` ... `05` |nine `SI` lines (line 12's `SI` at `0x109209`)
|2061, 21 |`10` (16) at `0x1092A5` |`cfont 4 7 2 12 7 2 27 7 2 35 4 2 40 4 2`
|2061, 22 |`18` (24) at `0x1092B6` |`    Message Format:        EDCKxxx text <&n> where:`; the row opens in the run at column 4, and `where:` lies in none
|2061, 23 |`00` at `0x1092CF` |empty line
|2061, 24--25 |`23` (35) at `0x1092D0`, `11` (17) at `0x1092F4` |`cfont 22 4 2 ...` over `EDCK - indicates that the message is one generated by` at column 22
|2062, 0--1 |`19` (25) at `0x109308`, `0C` (12) at `0x109322` |`cfont 32 3 2 ...` over `the C library when running under CICS` at column 32
|2062, 2--3 |`10` (16) at `0x10932F`, `0B` (11) at `0x109340` |`cfont 24 3 2 ...` over `xxx - error message number` at column 24
|2062, 4--6 |`00` at `0x10934C`--`0x10934E` |three empty lines
|2062, 7 |`03` at `0x10934F` |`SRMSG EDCK001` (`SRMSG` at `0x109350`)
|===

A reader can tell this heading from entry text by its bytes alone.  The
bare `SRMSG` is alone on its line.  The line directly after it is a `cfont`
line, and the row under that line lies wholly inside the font's runs.  Every
row drawn after it, up to the next entry's `SRMSG`, opens inside a run of the
`cfont` line directly above it.  The only other lines are further `cfont`
lines, `SI` lines and empty lines.  Hosted F.1 serves the heading row in
italics (`K`), with `<a name="MSG">` on it.  It serves the rows after it
exactly as drawn, with their styled runs in bold and the empty line as `<p>`,
and then `<a name="MSG EDCK001"><hr>`.  The `SI` lines draw nothing.  A row
that opens unstyled, or a heading row with an unstyled word, is not this
shape.  Neither is SC24-546 A.0's plain row, which stays entry text.

Consecutive selector lines inside an entry queue onto the first drawn row after
them.  Record 563 lines 11 and 12 are `cselect 22 4 FTNFTNUNIQ22` and
`cselect 31 4 FTNFTNUNIQ23` (each a line of its own: length byte `06`, the
two-byte `cselect` word `BD C7` at `0x41606` and `0x4160D`), and line 13 draws
` |        architecture (*) (SAA (*)) common programming interface (CPI)`.
The first selector has no payload of its own.  Hosted serves
`architecture<a href="#FTNFTNUNIQ22"> (*)</a> (SAA<a href="#FTNFTNUNIQ23"> (*)</a>)`:
both selectors mark cells of line 13, and neither replaces the other.

A `cfont` line between a selector and its row draws nothing either.  The
selector marks the row after the `cfont` line, the row that `cfont` styles.
`N2AH1MST` record 685 lines 34--36 are `cselect 53 4 FTNFTNUNIQ24`,
`cfont 11 7 2` and
`           Source:  Hardware configuration definition (*) (HCD)`.  Cells
53--56 of line 36 are ` (*)`, and hosted `14.0` (DT `19910329000100`) serves
`definition<a href="...#FTNFTNUNIQ24"> (*)</a> (HCD)`.  Record 1670 lines
3--5 of topic `19.0` (`cselect 73 4 FTNFTNUNIQ30`, `cfont 10 12 2`,
`          Explanation:  The JCL for a DDNAME asked to create a hiperspace
(*)`) are the same shape, and hosted links that `(*)` too.  The
selector's columns index the styled row; read against the `cfont` line
itself, `cselect 53 4` would fall past that 12-cell line's end.

A selector on the last line of its record marks the first line of the next
record inside an entry too, as it does in the introduction.  `N2AH1MST`
record 1886 ends with line 41, `cselect 46 4 FTNFTNUNIQ32`, inside entry
`ERB265I` of topic `21.0`.  Record 1887 line 0 is
`          01F0   Function not supported by VM. (*) partition is not`,
whose cells 46--49 are ` (*)`.  Hosted (DT `19910329000100`) serves
`VM.<a href="...#FTNFTNUNIQ32"> (*)</a> partition`.  The record's leading
length byte here is `17` (23) at `0xB09CD`, which happens to spell `/`.

A selector queued behind a `cfont` line waits across a record end in the
same way.  `SC09-138` topic `F.1` record 2050 ends with line 30,
`cselect 64 13 HDRFETCH` (length byte `06` at `0x10836E`, `cselect` `41` at
`0x10836F`), and line 31, `cfont 36 5 X 65 5 E` (length byte `08` at
`0x108375`, `cfont` `3A` at `0x108376`).  Record 2051 opens with line 0's
length byte `22` (34) at `0x108380`, and the line is
`   (2)  In the situation of doing a fetch to a load module, see "fetch -- Get`.
Both controls mark that row: cells 64--76 are `"fetch -- Get`, and hosted
`F.1` (DT `19910321130500`) serves
`<a href="8.1.10.5?...#HDRFETCH">&quot;<samp>fetch</samp> -- Get</a>`, with
the `cfont` runs at cells 36 and 65 (`fetch`, twice) in the same row.

The important implementation rule is that this is not a hardcoded packet title
or hardcoded three-line page rule. `:vnotice.` changes the interpretation of
the following controls: the first body `CFONT` is the visual notice heading,
the generated `NOTE` flow plus `CFONT` record supplies a `:note.` body, and a
copyright character followed by text before a `CZ BREAK` can form
`:coprnote.` plus `:coprext.`. Markdown generation is a convenience layer and
should render these recovered source-style records as bold notice heading,
bold `Note:` label, and a line break between `:coprnote.` and `:coprext.`.

Topic-header `ST` values can be adjacent to the first body text in the same
decoded logical record. For topic pages reached through the TOC, the `CTOCE`
title is the verified display-title boundary. The raw GML projection should
therefore split a leading record such as `:h1.Introduction This manual...` into
`:h1.Introduction` followed by `:p.This manual...` when the corresponding TOC
entry title is `Introduction`. Markdown renderers should use the topic id and
TOC title for the visible heading, matching BookServer output such as
`1.0 Introduction`, while preserving the following body paragraph as normal
paragraph text.

Example from `packet.script`:

----
:h1.An Introduction to Packet Radio
:p.Packet radio is a very advanced world, and I assure you that this
----

Corresponding `boorender --raw BOO/packet.boo 1.0` output now starts with:

----
:h1.An Introduction to Packet Radio
:p.Packet radio Is a Very advanced world, And i assure you That this Guide only Barely Does the protocol family Justice...
----

The tag shape matches BookMaster source. Case recovery and some spacing remain
limitations of the experimental logical-record decoder.

Book-level metadata controls use the same fallback or property-specific parser
elsewhere in `libgeist`: `CLANGUAGE=`, `CVERSION=`, `CBLDVERS=`, `CREFLOW=`,
`CTITLE=`, `CSTITLE=`, `CCOPYRIGHT=`, `CSECURITY=`, `CDATE=`, `CAUTHOR=`,
and `CDOCNUM=` are documented in <<logical-controls>>.
They are not normally emitted for an individual TOC topic unless they appear in
that topic's decoded raw record range.

[[markup_topic_and_heading_controls]]
=== Topic And Heading Controls

Topic headers begin with `SH<topic_id>` and are documented in <<topics>>. The heading/type field uses GML-like tag values:

[cols="<40,<45,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Control |Observed values |Role

|`SH<id>` |`SHcontents`, `SH1.0`, `SHpreface.5.1` |Topic identifier.
|`CTOPICN` |integer |1-based topic number.
|`CHDLEVEL` |`:H0`..`:H5`, `:MSGNO`, and the front-matter forms `:TOC`, `:COVER`, `:PREFACE`, `:ABSTRACT`, `:NOTICES`, `:VNOTICE`, `:INDEX`, `:GLOSSARY`, `:SOA`, `:TITLE`, `:BIBLIOG`, `:ABBREV`, `:FIGLIST`, `:TLIST` |Topic kind or heading depth. Complete census with counts in <<topics_chdlevel_observed_values>>. `:H6` does *not* occur in the corpus; `:H0` (the "Part" level, 50 topics) and `:MSGNO` (message-catalog topics, 1,617) were missing from this list. Every front-matter form is served as a level-1 heading; see "Front-matter heading forms".
|`ST` |free text |Topic title.
|`CPARENT`, `CFORWARDLEVEL`, `CBACKLEVEL` |topic ids |Navigation links between topics.
|===

Evidence:

[cols="<18,<45",options="header"]
|===
|File |Decoded record evidence

|`QS3X36CM.BOO` |`SHcontents ... CHDLEVEL :toc ... ST Table Of Contents`
|`QS3X36CM.BOO` |`SH1.0 ... CHDLEVEL :h1 ... ST Introduction`
|`GG24-4302-00.boo` |`SHfigures ... CHDLEVEL :figlist ... ST Figures`
|`GC23-046.boo` |pass:[<literal>SHNOTICES ... CHDLEVEL :NOTICES ... ST&#160;&#160;Notices</literal>] (21 books carry a `:NOTICES` topic)
|===

[[markup_the_st_title_is_one_display_row]]
==== The ST Title Is One Display Row

The `ST` payload is display material like any other: it is laid out in display
rows, and hosted BookServer serves *the first row* as the topic's heading
element. Everything after that row's break is body text.

[cols="<8,<8,<45,<45,<18",options="header"]
|===
|Book |Topic |`ST` payload |Hosted heading |Body starts
