|PRG1SORT |1.4 |pass:[<literal>Chapter 4.&#160;&#160;Sorting Records from a Single File to Produce a Record Address</literal>] + row break + `File` l|<H1> 1.4   Chapter 4.  Sorting Records from a Single File to Produce a Record Address</H1> l|<I>File</I>
|QSYSINFO |2.1.21 |pass:[<literal>SC09-1159, Languages:&#160;&#160;System/38-Compatible COBOL User's Guide and</literal>] + row break + `Reference` |the same row l|<I>Reference</I>
|===

Two consequences for an implementer.

* A row's marker/origin pair may not be read *across the end of the `ST`
control segment*: SC24-546 E.2 record 1169 ends that segment with `)`
(token 35, encoded value 37, width 1) and the fill/origin run that follows
belongs to the next segment's first row. Reading it as this row's marker
slot turns `The File Block (FBLOCK)` into `The File Block (FBLOCK`. The
exception is the documented compact-marker collision: a one-byte token
spelling a whole dictionary _word_ is a row-control byte wherever it stands
(FA1PLMM0 I.6.1 `access`, encoded value 43; SC24-5520-00 3.7.5.2 `and`;
SH20-918 3.33.14 `an`).
* At most one marker slot stands between `ST` and the first title word.
ACPZMST1 5.4 and 5.5 store `ST` + spacing + a placeholder slot + `/` +
`etc` + `/` + `inittab`; the `/` is the first character of the title
(`/etc/inittab File Definitions`), not a second slot.

The marker can be attached to the control rather than separated by spacing.
In `SC34-425.boo` topic `2.4.14.1`, logical record 1530 stores raw bytes
`81 00 70 c3 fb` at `0x15f7b9`: dictionary token `81` resolves to `ST`, `00`
resolves to the one-word attach control `0x0001`, `70` resolves to `|`, and
`c3 fb` resolves to the first title word `Functions`.  Topic `2.4.21` repeats
the form at `0x165202` as `81 00 70 be f2` (`ST`, attach, `|`, `FLMTCCPS`).
All three tokens preceding the title occupy the same native display line; the
attached `|` is the line marker, not part of an `ST|` opcode or of the
displayed title.

The contents title corroborates the title-row extent but does not replace its
spelling.  `SC34-425.boo` topic `2.4.12.4` stores `Using FLMLPGEN as a TEXT
Parser` while the contents entry capitalizes `As`; topics `2.4.21`, `2.4.32`,
and `2.4.33` similarly differ only in ASCII letter case.  The source display
row supplies the rendered title in each case.

The topic title the book catalog carries is a *string* projection of the same
control (it reads the flattened decoded record and stops at the first decoder
boundary), so it neither equals the display row nor is wrong: both are
truncations of one word run. QSYSINFO 2.1.21's catalog title runs on to
`... and Re`; PRG1SORT 1.4's runs on to `... Record Address File`. A reader
that has the display rows should take the heading from the row and use the
catalog string only to corroborate it positionally.

[[markup_cover_and_title_page_rendering]]
=== Cover And Title Page Rendering

BookManager cover and title-page topics are generated from book metadata and
source tags such as `:cover.` and `:tipage.`. They are not simple literal
preformatted text blocks, even though BookServer wraps the body in
`<pre width="80">`.

Evidence from `BOO/packet.script`:

[cols="<12,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Source lines |Evidence

|5-16 |`:title`, the `:library.` body, the `:topic.` body, `:release`, `:docnum`, `:partnum`, `:filenum`, `:date`, and `:author` define the visible metadata.
|21-22 |`:cover artid=prcover.` and `:tipage.` request generated cover/title topics.
|===

For the packet fixture specifically, the `TITLE` leading block has three lines
because the source prolog provides three leading title-block fields:
`Amateur Packet Radio` from the `:library.` body, `A Complete Tutorial` from the
`:topic.` body, and `Evie Cooper` from `:release.Evie Cooper`. A renderer should
not hardcode "three title-page lines" as a format rule. It should treat the
leading generated title block as running until the first generated metadata
label such as `Document Number`, `Part Number`, or `File Number`.

BookServer evidence from the hosted packet book:

[cols="<39,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Topic URL |Observed body behavior

l|/BOOKS/packet/COVER?DT=20260614112503 |The title words are emitted as bold HTML, followed by paragraph breaks between subtitle, author, document number, part number, and file number.
l|/BOOKS/packet/TITLE?DT=20260614112503 |The title, subtitle, and first author line are emitted as one bold title block with line breaks between the three lines, followed by paragraph breaks for document number, date, and author.
|===

What this shows about the format: the `TITLE` and `COVER` topics are treated
differently from ordinary body topics -- their leading lines become one emphasis
block with line breaks between them, rather than separate paragraphs -- and the
book-level metadata controls (`CTITLE=`, `CSTITLE=`, `CDOCNUM=`, `CDATE=`,
`CAUTHOR=`) are separate from that presentation. The metadata controls are
documented in <<container>> and <<logical-controls>>; the title-page projection below is a
rendering decision built on them.

This matters for Markdown conversion: treating the BookServer `<pre>` wrapper
as a Markdown fenced code block preserves line breaks but incorrectly suppresses
the intended title emphasis. A Markdown renderer should preserve the
title-page paragraph boundaries, apply emphasis to the generated leading title
lines, and preserve the `:tipage.` line breaks inside the leading title block
rather than turning each line into a separate paragraph. The number of leading
lines is source/prolog dependent; in the packet fixture it is three because the
prolog contains `:library`, `:topic`, and `:release` title-block fields. Exact
inline reconstruction for ordinary body text comes from the `CFONT`/`CFONTDEF`
pipeline, but generated cover/title-page `CFONT` controls are display-layout
records. Do not apply those title-page `CFONT` spans to collapsed text as normal
body highlighted-phrase spans; in `packet.boo` this incorrectly maps the final
title-line spans into `Document Number`. Preserve the visual title-page text and
then split the verified metadata labels during title-page Markdown rendering.

The current raw projection can still collapse adjacent generated metadata
fields into one `:p.` record. That is a projection limitation, not evidence that
BookServer joins those labels on one line. The Markdown renderer splits the
verified generated-cover labels (`Document Number`, `Part Number`, and
`File Number`) back into separate paragraphs to match BookServer's visual
structure.

The `File Number PACKET` value specifically verifies the logical-record spacing
rule documented in <<encoding>>: the `PACKET` token is present in
full in the BOO token stream, followed by two consecutive prefix-0 spacing
controls. Both controls suppress pending synthetic spacing; they must not delete
the visible final `T`.

[[markup_table_of_contents_controls]]
=== Table-Of-Contents Controls

The `CONTENTS` topic stores literal TOC controls:

[cols="<13,<45,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Control |Syntax |Role

|`CTOCDEF` |`CTOCDEF=<style> <fields...>` |Defines TOC presentation styles. The exact numeric field meanings are not fully resolved.
|`CTOCE` |`CTOCE <nesting> <toc_style> <topic_id> <title>` |One displayed TOC entry.
|`CZ OFF ETOC` |`CZ OFF ETOC 0 0` |End of the generated TOC region, in the five `CZ`-dialect books only. No standalone `ETOC` control exists.
|===

Example from `QS3X36CM.BOO`:

----
CTOCE 0 1 1.0 Introduction
----

This points to topic header:

----
SH1.0 ... CTOPICN 4 ... CHDLEVEL :h1 ... ST Introduction
----

[[markup_font_and_highlight_controls]]
=== Font And Highlight Controls

Book-level `CFONTDEF` controls map compact font/style codes to semantic style
names. Body-level `CFONT` controls then apply those compact codes to spans of
text in a logical record.

The map is *byte-identical in all 34 `BOO/` fixtures*: exactly 35 codes, the
same name for every code in every book, with no book-local additions. Codes are
stored uppercase; the earlier lowercase spelling in this table came from a
case-folding decoder and the reader treats them case-insensitively.

[cols="<15,<13,<26",options="header"]
|===
|`CFONTDEF` code |Semantic name |Note

|`0` |`H0` |Plain/base.
|`1`..`9` |`HP1`..`HP9` |Highlighted phrase levels.
|`H`..`M` |`H1`..`H6` |In-topic heading levels.
|`A` |`APL` |
|`B` |`CAUTION` |
|`C` |`CIT` |Citation.
|`D` |`DANGER` |
|`E` |`XMP` |Example/preformatted.
|`F` |`CAUTIONTEXT` |
|`G` |`WARNINGTEXT` |
|`O` |`DANGERTEXT` |
|`P` |`PK` |
|`Q` |`PKDEF` |
|`R` |`RK` |
|`T` |`TP` |
|`U` |`MD` |
|`V` |`PV` |
|`W` |`WARNING` |
|`X` |`XPH` |
|`Y` |`MDQUAL` |
|`Z` |`PVDEF` |
|`_` |`UNDERSCORE` |
|===

The letters `N` and `S` are not defined by any book. The six admonition codes
`B` `CAUTION`, `D` `DANGER`, `W` `WARNING`, `F` `CAUTIONTEXT`,
`G` `WARNINGTEXT` and `O` `DANGERTEXT` were missing from the earlier version of
this table.  All six now have a verified hosted presentation: the warning pair
is emphasised, the caution and danger pairs are bold (see the bold-code census
under "Style-Code Presentation Verified Against Hosted BookServer").

Observed body examples:

[cols="<28,<45,<45",options="header"]
|===
|File |Decoded record evidence |Interpretation

|`QS3X36CM.BOO` |`CFONT 7 2 1 10 9 1` |Two spans use style code `1`, which the book header defines as `HP1`.
|`QS3X36CM.BOO` |`CFONT 12 2 x 15 6 x` |Two spans use style code `x`, defined as `xph`.
|`packet.boo` topic `PREFACE` |Source line `:hp3.wec@bam.moe:ehp3.!`; raw projection `:hp3.wec@bam.moe:ehp3.!`; hosted BookServer HTML `<B><I>wec@bam.moe</B></I>!` |`HP3` is an inline highlighted phrase span. BookServer renders this PACKET `HP3` span as bold plus italic, and punctuation after `:ehp3.` remains outside the span.
|`IBMMMSTR.boo` topic `1.5.125` |Logical record 203 carries `CFONT 26 1 A`; the covered token is stored at file bytes `0x1c3da`--`0x1c3dc` as compressed value `0xcfc0`, expands to byte `0x8a`, and projects through code page 37 as `U+00AB` |`A` is a code-page selector, not decoration. `CFONTDEF` names it `APL`; code page 293 maps byte `0x8a` to `U+2191` (`UPWARDS ARROW`). The printed message example places that arrow below the offending source cell. The same one-cell form occurs in 26 topics of this book; `1.5.330` covers two adjacent arrows.
|===

An `APL` span must therefore decode its covered source cells with code page
293.  Decoding the book through code page 37 first and then retaining its
`U+00AB` projection changes visible content.  Cells outside the span remain in
the book's ordinary code page even when they contain the same byte value.
The hosted page presents an `APL` span as monospace, one `<code>` element per
covered word: SH45-5547-00 `2.15.2` (DT `19950203080455`) serves its
`cfont 5 10 A 16 2 A 19 3 A 23 4 A` row as `<code>'DASAPLPK'</code>
<code>11</code> <code>±NA</code> <code>'AS'</code>.  That is one book, not a
census, and its third word shows that the hosted page does not apply the
code-page rule above: the cell decodes to the degree sign in the book's own
code page, and the hosted page serves every degree sign as `±`, in running
text too (SY27-0350-04 `C.1.4`, DT `19980701130105`, stores
`10°C to 40.6°C` and serves `10±C to 40.6±C`).  The `±` is therefore an
artefact of the hosted output, not a character of the book.

The `CFONT` field layout observed so far is repeated triples:

----
CFONT <column_or_offset> <span_length> <font_code> ...
----

*A style code is one character of the book's own `CFONTDEF` table*, with the
`,` operand separator optionally glued to the last triple's code.  The table
is byte-identical in all 34 corpus books (swept with `bootrace --fonts`):
`0`-`9`, `A`-`M`, `O`-`R`, `T`-`Z` and `_`; there is no `N` and no `S`.  A
reader must stop the triple walk at the first word that is not such a code,
because the row's own display text can begin with numbers and would otherwise
be eaten as another triple: `SC33-033.boo` record 493 segment 12 is
`CFONT 17 3 E 26 3 E 35 3 E 44 3 E` over the display row
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;190&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;195&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;200&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;205</literal>], and reading
`190 195 200` as a fifth triple both invents a span with the impossible code
`200` and hides three numbers from the row.  `FA1PLMM0.boo` record 655
segment 4 is the same shape over
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;3350&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;30&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;(results in 1410 entries)</literal>].

The first field is a display-column coordinate, not an offset in already
collapsed Markdown/plain text. A decoder expands BOO logical records into a
display line, tracks the active layout indent from `CZ FLOW <tag> <left> <indent>`, applies font spans in that display coordinate space, and only then
collapses the source-style spacing for flowing text. For example, PACKET topic
`3.2` has a list item:

----
CZ FLOW LI 3 7
CFONT 12 9 1 23 4 1                 ?   The  interface  name, ...
----

Using the active indent column `7`, the spans apply to display columns for
`interface` and `name`. A renderer that first collapses the doubled display
space after `The` and then guesses word boundaries will tear this into
fragments such as `The inter` and `ce n`.

Display columns count decoded characters, not encoded UTF-8 bytes. In
`SC24-546.boo` topics `2.1.3`, `2.2.3`, `2.4.3`, and `6.2.6`, and
`PRG1SORT.boo` topic `C.1`, the visible stream contains multibyte characters
inside or adjacent to `CFONT` spans. A renderer may keep byte offsets for
output insertion, but its display-column map must advance once per decoded
character and may place span boundaries only at complete UTF-8 character
boundaries.

Some fixed rows begin with a decoder separator or visual row marker followed
by padding.  That prefix is outside the visible text but establishes the row's
column origin; it must be consumed before ordinary whitespace normalization.
Verified examples include:

[cols="<29,<45,<45",options="header"]
|===
|File/topic |Decoded evidence |Intended projection

|`SH12-565.boo`, `APPENDIX1.8` l|CFONT 11 1 2 ?            2    Delete |The span covers the numeric label `2`, not the `e` in `Delete`.
|`SC24-546.boo`, `2.1.3` l|CFONT 54 3 1 ?              A literal string ... any characters |The HP1 span covers the complete word `any`.
|`SC41-485.boo`, `1.2.2` l|CFONT 7 5 2 ?  \|     *APPC ... |The visual `\|` is structural; the span covers the complete `*APPC` term.
|`SC31-711.boo`, `4.2.2` l|CFONT 3 12 2 <    Description: ... |The leading `<` is a continuation marker; the span covers `Description:`.
|===

The `CFONT` column is zero-based once the active visual-row origin is known;
bar-prefixed definition rows retain a one-column structural bar immediately
before their visible term.  Span metadata can also be separated from its text
by a logical-record boundary.  A pending span must be consumed by the marker-
led continuation itself, not left active for the next ordinary record.

If damaged or still-unreconstructed fixed-layout input would place a span
boundary inside an ASCII word, emitting that partial span corrupts the text.
The safe fallback is to retain the complete word without that style.  Exact
whole-word recovery is permitted only when the ordered span lengths identify
unambiguous word tokens; table and preformatted-row reconstruction remains a
separate structural step.

Generated BookMaster output can also carry presentation-only tokens between
typed controls and visible rows.  `c.sp <distance> p c` is a spacing directive,
not topic-title or paragraph text.  Empty selector alternatives (`<>`) and the
kind names `<IMAGE>`, `<INTERNET>`, and `<OTHER>` are metadata, including when
they remain in a neighboring `ST`, `CZ`, or `CFONT` display fragment rather
than the parsed `CSELECT` operand.  Fixed-row sentinels such as `/`, `//`, and
`:H1`--`:H6` are non-visible only when followed by the decoded layout padding;
ordinary slashes and colon-prefixed prose must be preserved.  XWEBDEMO topics
`TITLE`, `FIGURES`, `1.0`, `1.1`, `1.3`, `1.4.1`, and `1.4.3` provide the
cross-context evidence.

`SRMSG <message-id> [symbolic-name]` is generated message-catalog metadata.  It
starts a new message entry and supplies an anchor identity; neither `SRMSG` nor
its operand is body prose.  The following fixed rows normally use `CFONT` to
identify the message number/name, `Description:`, and response label.  Readers
must keep those rows associated across logical-record boundaries.  Some
catalog introductions are wrapped in `CZ OFF XMP`/`CZ OFF EXMP` even though
the hosted reader presents them as ordinary prose followed by message entries;
the wrapper must not turn intervening `SRMSG` and `CFONT` controls into literal
preformatted text.  Verified examples are `SC31-711.boo` topics `4.1.1`,
`4.2.1`, `4.2.2`, `4.3.2`, `4.3.4`, and `4.4`.

The same control also occurs inside the metadata envelope of an individually
indexed message topic.  In `IBMMMSTR.boo`, 1,617 topic source ranges carry an
`SRMSG` line between `CSUMMARY` and `CHDLEVEL :MSGNO`; the following
`CSOURCEFN` and `ST` retain their ordinary envelope meanings.  For example,
logical record 1257 begins topic `3.6.1` with `SRMSG IBM002I`, while logical
record 1066 begins topic `1.6.1` at BOO bytes
`0x4E775`--`0x4E782`: `04 28 75 71 75 04 68 CD CE 51 02 24 12`.  Native row
framing divides that sequence into `CSUMMARY 11 0 11`,
`SRMSG IEL2233I E`, and `CHDLEVEL :MSGNO`; their token-provenance ranges are
respectively `0x4E776`--`0x4E77A`, `0x4E77B`--`0x4E77F`, and
`0x4E780`--`0x4E782`.  The complete text after `SRMSG`, including the optional
symbolic tail, forms the `MSG ` anchor identity.  It remains metadata rather
than visible prose; the `ST`, `SI` and styled message rows independently carry
the visible title, index identity and message fields.  `:MSGNO` is therefore a
topic heading form, not a displayed body token; the topic is headed at level 6
(see "Message topics are headed at level 6").

An individually indexed message can also carry a second, bare `SRMSG` after
its visible message fields.  This is a local `MSG` destination rather than a
new message envelope, and it does not consume the following prose row.
`IBMMMSTR.boo` topic `3.7.14`, logical record 1631, stores the control as the
native row `01 68`: length byte `01`, then the sole content byte `68`, which
decodes as `SRMSG`.  The next bytes, `19 06`, are the length and three-cell
origin of the following explanatory row.  The 74-cell
`U+2500` row immediately before the control remains a horizontal rule.  Thus
native row framing, rather than the flattened control segment's apparent
operands, determines the extent of the bare destination.

Span-only `CFONT` records apply to the next physical text segment. PACKET
topic `1.3` stores `CFONT 27 5 3 33 10 3` at the end of one logical record and
the next record starts with pass:[<literal>FM&#160;&#160;radio&#160;&#160;through&#160;&#160;its audio interface; ...</literal>].
BookServer renders the following line with `audio` and `interface;`
highlighted. This continuation case uses the following paragraph line's
display column, not the previous logical record's accumulated text offset.

Some reflow-off books also compile callout boxes as visual rows carried by
`CFONT` records rather than explicit `CZ OFF LBLBOX` controls. In
`QSYSNEWG.BOO` topic `1.0`, logical record 18 contains:

----
CFONT 8 2 2 11 1 2 13 6 2      ???? In a Hurry? ...
CFONT 5 8 2 14 12 2 27 7 2 ... ? Official Introductory Chapter
----

The hosted BookServer page
`/BOOKS/QSYSNEWG/1.0?DT=19910524085706&SHELF=` renders those rows inside
`<pre width="80">` as bold whole words: `In`, `a`, `Hurry?`, `Official`,
`Introductory`, and `Chapter`. The `?` after `Hurry` is literal punctuation in
this context even though the same decoded byte is otherwise used as a visual
border/separator marker. The local renderer therefore treats multi-span
`CFONT` records with visual border runs as visual-box candidates only when the
spans match consecutive leading words; single-span wrapped glossary/body rows
remain ordinary flowing text.

`QSYSNEWG.BOO` topics `2.0` and `2.1` show two more fixed-layout `CFONT`
patterns. Rows beginning with the visual marker `|` keep their pre-collapse
display columns for span placement. Topic `2.1` logical record 40 has:

----
CFONT 28 4 2 33 2 2      | After a few seconds, the Sign On display comes on.
----

The hosted BookServer renders `Sign` and `On` as separate bold words. Topic
`2.0` uses the same rule for `user`, `name`, and `password` inside pipe-led
body rows. Highly indented fixed rows can also carry spans whose first stored
offset precedes the first visible character. Topic `2.0` logical record 38
stores:

----
CFONT 7 8 1 16 8 1 25 3 1 29 8 1 53 10 1 64 5 1
             Security Concepts and Planning manual and the Operator's Guide.
----

BookServer renders `Security`, `Concepts`, `and`, `Planning`, `Operator's`,
and `Guide` in `HP1`. A compatible renderer must not apply those offsets after
paragraph collapse, which tears the title into partial words.

Fixed text carried by `SRFIG` must be parsed before decoded separator cleanup.
`QSYSNEWG.BOO` topic `2.1` stores the Sign On display as rows separated and
bordered by decoded `?` bytes. Long runs of `?` form the horizontal rules;
single `?` bytes delimit the left/right borders and row payloads. Payload rows
are 82 display columns wide; the hosted BookServer wraps them in
`<pre width="80">` and emits 28 visible rows from the top rule through the
bottom rule. The last blank row in the `SRFIG` segment is represented only by
trailing separator bytes, so a decoder must not call generic trimming that
treats terminal `?` as disposable punctuation before fixed-row recovery.
`SRFIG` can also wrap structured tables (`SRTBL`/`SRETBL`), as in
`QSYSNEWG.BOO` topic `F.1`; table parsing must take precedence over fixed-screen
row recovery while the table scope is active.

*Figure regions nest.* A captioned figure that frames a ruled table is
stored as an outer `SRFIG<caption-id>` around an inner
`SRFIG<table-id> ... SRTBL<table-id> ... SRETBL SREFIG`, closed by a second
`SREFIG`; the outer region may carry display lines of its own before the
inner opener and after the inner `SREFIG`.  `DREICMST.boo` records 79-84 of
topic `1.2.1` spell, in order, `SRFIGLOGPROC`, the display line
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;split=yes.</literal>], `SRFIGXXX`, `SRTBLXXX`, the drawn table, `SRETBL`, `SREFIG`,
the caption line `Figure 5. Where SLR Gets Its Data`, `SREFIG`.  Hosted
BookServer at DT `19911219125856` serves
pass:[<literal>&lt;a name="FIGLOGPROC"&gt;&#160;&#160;&#160;split=yes.&lt;/a&gt;</literal>] and then
`<a name="FIGXXX"><a name="TBLXXX">` on the table's top rule, so both anchors
and both text lines are real.  The same shape occurs in `FA1PLMM0.boo`
(`SRFIGENV` / `SRFIG1234` / `SRTBL1234`), `SC28-1881-05.boo` `2.2` (two inner
tables under one outer figure) and `GC28-183.boo` `COVER`.  A decoder that
treats a second `SRFIG` as evidence that the first was unterminated loses the
outer figure's anchor, its lead line and its caption.

The outer region's own display lines are ordinary display lines token for
token, including the one-byte attach-prefixed punctuation tokens that,
between controls, are only separators.  `SC24-5527-02.boo` `COMMENTS` frames
table `TBLUNIQ549` in figure `FIGUNIQ548`; record 978 stores `comments`
(file offset `0x62090`, `d3 09`), the one-byte token `02` at `0x62092`
(attach control plus `,`) and `you` (`0x62093`, `89`), and hosted BookServer
(DT `19921218151459`) serves `If you wish to make comments, you may print
out this topic, fill in your`.  `FA1PLMM0.boo` `2.2` (DT `19910927114801`)
record 100 stores its caption as `VSE` (`0x2426a`, `8c`), attach (`00`),
`/` (`23`), attach (`00`), `ESA` (`72`), served as `Figure 4. VSE/ESA
Predefined Environments`.  Only a display line's length byte is structure
there.

*An image figure can have an unnumbered caption.* In `GA19-5486-13.boo`
(build date `10/12/95 16:12:06`), topic `2.1` has this sequence:
`SRFIGFIGUNIQ2`, `cz OFF FIG`, `cselect 3 9 PIC1`, the standalone display
line `   PICTURE 1`, the standalone display line
`   AS/400 Layered Architecture`, and `SREFIG`. The title has no generated
`Figure <n>.` prefix. The picture placeholder ends logical record 42;
blank lines intervene before the title starts record 43. A logical-record
boundary therefore does not terminate the figure or its caption.

The title line starts at BOO byte `0xA34A3` with length byte `0A`, followed
by tokens `14 75 00 22 00 BC D5 58 C8 9F` at `0xA34A4`--`0xA34AD`:
three spaces, `AS`, attach, `/`, attach, `400`, `Layered`, `Architecture`.
The next line starts at `0xA34AE` with length byte `02`; its token
`E0 CA` at `0xA34AF` spells `SREFIG`. The following `c.cp`,
`cz OFF EFIG 0 0`, and `cz FLOW P 3 3` precede the next body paragraph.
This observation establishes a single unnumbered title between a picture
placeholder and its figure end; it does not establish how to distinguish
arbitrary prose or multiple unnumbered title lines within a figure.

*A picture region is an ordered sequence, not an image followed by a required
caption.* `GA19-5486-13.boo` topic `5.0`, logical record 134, contains
`cselect 3 9 PIC8`, the picture placeholder, a separately styled
`9402 Model 236` line, and the number-only line `Figure 1.`, before `SREFIG`.
At file offset `0xAADAC`, length byte `05` introduces tokens
`44 B9 C3 DA 4C` (`cselect 3 9 PIC8`). The font-control line begins
at `0xAADB9` with length byte `0A`; the label line itself begins
at `0xAADC4`: `05 14 C4 8D EA 6F`.
The number-only caption begins at `0xAADCB`: `05 14 D1 15 AC 01`;
the next line is `02 E0 CA` (`SREFIG`). The `01` belongs to the caption's
encoded punctuation; the next line's `02` is its length, not caption text.
The label's `CFONT` spans must be applied independently of whether the text
resembles a generated caption.

Conversely, `SC09-2417-00.boo` topic `1.3.3.4`, record 134, places
the wrapped `Figure 7. OPM-ILE Program Scenario.` caption *before* its
picture selector. `GA19-5486-13.boo` topic `19.5`, record 677, places
three styled labels before pictures 73, 74 and 75. Moving all images ahead
of all labels or captions changes the stored order.
The generated caption separator is not invariably a period:
`GA19-5486-13.boo` topics `23.1` and `23.3` use `Figure 1:` and
`Figure 2:`. These spellings describe observed captions, not delimiters
that authorize discarding other visible text inside a figure.

*A numbered figure caption is a run of display lines, and its continuations are
indented to the title column.* The caption line is `Figure <n>. <title>`;
every line that continues it starts at exactly the column where `<title>`
starts on the first line, and hosted BookServer joins them into one caption.
`SH20-918.boo` topic `1.5` record 50 lines 21-24 are

----
   Figure 1. Processing Documents with GML.  The profile provides the mapping
             between the tags you have used in your source document, which
             identify elements of text, and APFs located in the host system
             library, which provide formatting functions.
----

-- caption at column 3, title and every continuation at column 13 -- and
hosted DT `19910520154851` prints exactly those four lines.  The same shape
holds in `DREICMST.boo` `1.1.1.2.1` (records 61-62, eight lines),
`ITPPIBOK.BOO` `5.2` (record 169 lines 26-27) and `SC09-2417-00.boo`
`2.2.4.5` (record 236 lines 10-11, continuation `Length` at column 14
because the number is `16.`).  A `CFONT`/`CSELECT` control line or an `SI`
subject-index line may stand between a caption and its continuation
(`SH20-918.boo` `2.5` record 103 lines 13-15, `PRG1SORT.boo` `1.1.4.3.2`);
hosted joins across them.

The Layout IR's physical rows do not delimit a caption.  A row opens at the
marker word in front of the title -- the word `Figure` itself in
`IEAC6MST.BOO` `1.2` record 50 token 130, the sentence `.` in `ITPPIBOK.BOO`
`5.2` record 169 token 192 -- and ends at the length byte that opens the
continuation line, so a caption read row by row is truncated at its head and
looks like prose at its tail.

*A `CSELECT` inside a figure region covers a span of the display line it
precedes.* The operands are `<column> <length> <target>`, both zero-based
display columns of the _next_ line that carries text.  `SH20-918.boo` `2.1`
record 59 line 20 is `cselect 41 8 FIGTITEM` and line 21 is

----
             paragraph unit are shown in Figure 4.  An implied paragraph is
----

whose columns 41..48 are `Figure 4`; hosted DT `19910520154851` serves
`... are shown in <a href="...#FIGTITEM">Figure 4</a>.`  Line 23's
`cselect 22 23 FIGTABLE` covers `Figure 8 in topic 2.2.6` the same way.  The
covered span is taken literally, padding and box border included:
`SC09-138.boo` `NOTICES` record 5 line 15 is `cselect 51 22 HDRNOTICES` and
hosted wraps pass:[<literal>&amp;quot;Notices&amp;quot; in&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;|</literal>] -- nine spaces and the box's
right rule -- in the anchor.  A selector may sit inside the drawn body as
well as inside the caption (`FA1PLMM0.boo` `5.0` record 272 line 29
`cselect 5 24 FIGVMSUM` covers `Figure 18 in topic 5.1.1` inside the box;
`SC09-138.boo` `8.5.4.5` record 1625 line 12 `cselect 26 3 FIGFREEC3` covers
the footnote marker `132`).

*A bare `SRSPT<id>` inside a figure region is a second anchor*, opened on
the display line that follows it.  `SC34-425.boo` `2.5.3` record 1746 line 8
is `SRSPTRCC11` and hosted DT `19921112160049` serves
pass:[<literal>&lt;a name="FIGFIGUNIQ77"&gt;&lt;a name="SPTRCC11"&gt;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;COUNT&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;: 4 bytes&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/a&gt;</literal>].
`FA1PLMM0.boo` `H.5` (`SPTIESWP`) opens one in the middle of a box and
`SC24-5520-00.boo` `3.8.3.6` carries two (`SPTFCIR`, `SPTRFLUSH`).  A word
of a drawn line that is merely _spelled_ like a structural opcode is not one:
`SH12-565.boo` `APPENDIX1.9.5.2.1` record 796 line 30 is the body text
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;SRVPREF&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;(server prefix)</literal>], whose first content token is the leading
space run, not the opcode.  As with the `C`-controls, a genuine control
stands at `prefix_token + 1`.

*A figure region may carry several picture selectors under one caption.*
`SC26-457.boo` `3.2.1` spells `cselect 3 9 PIC1` / pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;PICTURE 1</literal>] and then
`cselect 3 9 PIC2` / pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;PICTURE 2</literal>] before the single caption
`Figure 2. ALTER Parameters and the Entry Types to Which Each Applies`, and
hosted DT `19911220230217` stacks both images under the `FIGVSAMATT` anchor.
`SC26-457.boo` `B.1.3` (`PIC4` + `PIC5`) and `SC34-425.boo` `2.1.2` (`PIC21`

* `PIC22`) are the same shape.

Bold and emphasis should therefore be implemented through the `CFONT` plus
`CFONTDEF` pipeline, not by searching for literal `<b>` markup. `HP1`, `HP2`,
and `HP3` are GML-derived highlighted-phrase levels. The PACKET `PREFACE`
fixture confirms that BookServer renders `HP3` as bold plus italic in normal
body text.

`W` and `G` are the two halves of one GML warning block: `W` styles its
`Warning:` lead and `G` every word of its body, and the block is anchored
`<a name="WRN">`.  SH12-565 `FRONT_1.1` (DT `19941206115523`) serves

----
<a name="WRN">   <em>Warning:</em> <em>Do</em> <em>not</em> <em>use</em> <em>this</em> <em>Diagnosis,</em> ...</a>
----

and SC26-457 `1.6.5`, SC31-711 `3.3` and GC23-046 `6.9.3` the same shape.
Corpus-wide the two codes occur only in those four books: a `bootrace --fonts` sweep of all 7,362 topics finds 7 `W` spans and 363 `G` spans.

`Z` (`PVDEF`) presents exactly as `PKDEF` does.  The same sweep finds `Z`
only in SC26-457 `1.3`, and a sample of 87 further books from the hosted
shelf finds it in exactly one more: SC26-4905-00 `1.3`.  Both store
`cfont 3 7 P 11 10 Z 22 4 P 27 1 P 29 10 Z 39 1 P` over
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;COMMAND parameters .... [ terminator]</literal>].  Hosted
serves both rows (DTs `19911220191142` and `19930625182638`) as
`<kbd>COMMAND</kbd> <dfn>parameters</dfn> <kbd>....</kbd> <kbd>[</kbd> <dfn>terminator</dfn><kbd>]</kbd>`.
The two witnesses are two books serving one shared sentence, not two
independent uses of the code.
The `Q` census below finds `Z` in four more books, each served as `<dfn>`
(see there).  Six books in all is still too few to call the rule verified
over the library; it remains an open question.

[[markup_style_code_underscore_census]]
`_` (`UNDERSCORE`) underscores every column its span covers, the blank ones
included.  Hosted BookServer serves the span as one lowercase `<u>` element
(`HP5`..`HP9` are served with an uppercase `<U>` per word) whose content is
the covered columns with every blank column replaced by `_`.  A span over a
row's blank columns is therefore a fill-in slot drawn as a run of
underscores, one per column, and a span over words joins them with `_`:

* SC24-5527-02 `COMMENTS` (DT `19921218151459`) record 978 segment 4 is
  `cfont 3 5 2 9 3 2 13 4 2 18 1 2 20 6 2 29 3 _ 32 3 2 37 3 _ 41 2 2` over
  pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;Would you like a reply?&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;YES&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;NO</literal>].
  Columns 26-31 between `reply?` and `YES` are the word separator blank
  after `reply?` and then the five-cell space-run token 275 at file offset
  `0x621e7` (byte `1c`); the `_` triple `29 3` covers the run's last three
  cells.  Hosted serves
  pass:[<literal>&lt;B&gt;reply?&lt;/B&gt;&#160;&#160;&#160;&lt;u&gt;___&lt;/u&gt;&lt;B&gt;YES&lt;/B&gt;&#160;&#160;&lt;u&gt;___&lt;/u&gt;&#160;&lt;B&gt;NO&lt;/B&gt;</literal>].
* SL26-0018-02 `COVER` (DT `19951023164345`) record 6 segment 11 is
  `cfont 3 17 _` over `CMS/TSO Pipelines`; hosted serves
  `<u>CMS/TSO_Pipelines</u>`.  EOY03MST and SC23-0450-08 `2.8.2` serve
  `cfont ... 39 24 _` over `BookMaster User's Guide.` as
  `<u>BookMaster_User's_Guide.</u>`.
* A span can reach past the last column the row stores.  CNMYZ0MS `1.2.3`
  (DT `19911016130733`) record 133 stores `cfont 39 20 _` in front of the
  row `DSIPARM data set name`, whose stored text ends at column 24; hosted
  pads the row with blanks to column 39 and serves
  `<u>____________________</u>` there.

The census drew every book carrying `_` from a seeded random sample of 1,500
books of the hosted shelf, plus the four books named by an earlier sample
and SC24-5527-02 from the corpus; every book's `CFONTDEF` table maps `_` to
the same name `UNDERSCORE`.  For one topic per book (a topic other than the
reader's-comment form where the book has one) the hosted page was fetched,
its `<base href>` and title checked against the book, and every `_` span of
the topic compared with the page's lowercase `<u>` elements in order: 83
books, 321 spans, 299 of them over blank columns only.  Every span is served
as exactly one `<u>`, and 313 of the 321 match the rule above character for
character.  The eight that do not are all one shape: a `_` span laid over
words that other triples of the same control also style.  There hosted
interleaves the per-word tags with the `<u>` and draws only the first blank
of the span as `_`, leaving the rest as spaces: SG24-3910-01 `2.1` record 33
`cfont 7 3 3 11 4 3 ... 47 3 3 7 43 _` is served
`<B><I><u>IBM</B></I>_<B><I>High</B></I> <B><I>Level</B></I> ... </u>`,
SH35-0069-06 `4.1.2.2` record 129 `cfont 6 4 2 6 5 _ ... 16 22 _` is served
`<B><u>Name</B>_</u>` and `<B><u>Value</B>_<B>Set</B> <B>by</B> ...</u>`,
and EOY03MST / SC23-0450-01 `2.8.2` serve their `HP3`/`HP6` sample rows the
same way.  That shape is a property of the hosted renderer's tag nesting, not
of the bytes, and an implementation that does not reproduce it should not
claim to present such a row.  The table quotes the first `_` row of 20 of
the books; the hex is the `CFONT` control's own token bytes from the control
word to the last style code.

[[markup_style_code_k_census]]
`K` (`H4`) is italic, not bold, in every book that uses it.  Its name places
it among the in-topic heading codes `H`..`M`, but the code carries no weight
of its own: every book's `CFONTDEF` table maps `K` to the same name `H4`, so
nothing in the bytes distinguishes one book's `K` from another's, and the
presentation is fixed by the code.  A census drew 30 books that carry `K` --
4 from the 33-book corpus and 26 from a seeded random sample of 400 further
books -- whose hosted copy is catalogued under the same file name with the
same title.  For each, the hosted page of one topic was fetched and every
`K`-styled `CFONT` row in it was matched word for word against the page:
all 119 rows are served as one `<I>` element per covered word, and none as
`<B>` or plain text.  The table quotes the first such row of each book.  The
hex is the `CFONT` control's own token bytes, from the control word to the
last style code; the style-code byte differs per book because each book's
dictionary keys its words independently.

The one sampled book left out, SC26-4723-00, has no hosted copy to compare:
the hosted catalogue serves a different book (_QMF Planning and
Administration Guide for VM_) under that name.  Two further rows quoted
elsewhere in this specification fit the same rule: SC34-425 `2.4.9`, whose
`CFONT 3 6 K 10 5 K` ends record 1483 and styles `Return Codes` at the start
of record 1484, is served `<I>Return</I> <I>Codes</I>`, and SC09-138 `F.1`
record 2061 serves its `K` row between two message entries as
`<I>Run-Time</I> <I>Error</I> <I>Messages</I> ...`.

[[markup_style_code_v_census]]
`V` (`PV`) is a programming variable, and hosted BookServer serves every word
it covers as one `<var>` element.  A census scanned 248 books of a seeded
random sample of the library for `V`, `Q` and `Z` spans; 139 of them carry
`V`.  The first 30 of those whose file name is not all lowercase (the hosted
catalogue misses all-lowercase names) were fetched from the hosted shelf,
pinned to each book's own `DT`, and every `V` span of the first topic
carrying the code was matched against the page.  Two books were left out
because the hosted shelf serves a different book under their name
(SH45-5527-00 is served as SH45-5522-00, SC31-7541-02 as SC31-7540-02).  In
the remaining 28 books all 225 spans are served as `<var>`, and none as
`<I>`, `<em>` or plain text.  The table quotes the first `CFONT` row of each
book that carries a `V` span with drawn text; the hex is the control's own
token bytes from the control word to the last style code (`...` where it
runs on), and the last column counts the topic's matched spans.

[[markup_style_code_q_census]]
`Q` (`PKDEF`) is a defined parameter keyword -- typically the default value
of a parameter -- and hosted BookServer serves every word it covers as one
`<dfn>` element.  The census used the same 248-book sample as the `V` census
above, in which 44 books carry `Q`.  The 39 whose file name is not all
lowercase were fetched from the hosted shelf, pinned to each book's own
`DT`, and every `Q` span with drawn text in the first topic carrying the
code was matched against the page.  SC31-7541-02 was left out because the
hosted shelf serves SC31-7540-02 under its name.  In the remaining 38 books
all 194 spans are served as `<dfn>`, and none as `<kbd>`, `<tt>` or plain
text, although `Q` presents in the same monospace face as `P`.  The table
quotes the first `CFONT` row of each book that carries a `Q` span, in the
same form as the `V` table; the hosted column shows its first `Q` word.

also carries `Q`.  All 13 of their `Z` spans are served as `<dfn>`:

With SC26-457 and SC26-4905-00 that makes six books, against 28 for `V` and
38 for `Q`.  Whether `Z` is `<dfn>` in every book is open; until more books are found, the only verified fact is that it
presents exactly as `Q` does in each book that has been checked.

[[markup_style_code_bold_census]]
`T` (`TP`), `Y` (`MDQUAL`), `B` (`CAUTION`), `F` (`CAUTIONTEXT`), `D`
(`DANGER`) and `O` (`DANGERTEXT`) are bold.  Hosted BookServer serves every
word one of them covers as one bold element of its own: `<B>` for `T` and
`Y`, `<b>` for `B` and `F`, `<strong>` for `D` and `O`.  The three spellings
are the same presentation; which one a code gets is fixed by the code letter,
not by the book, exactly as for `K`.  `B`/`F` and `D`/`O` are the label and
body halves of the caution and danger notices, as `W`/`G` are of the warning
notice, but unlike that pair they are bold rather than emphasised: GC30-3867-01
`1.0` (DT `19970805114346`) record 32 stores `cfont 3 6 D` (`34 c8 d8 e0 41`
at `0x1eb38e`) over `DANGER` and `cfont 5 2 O 8 10 O ...` over the notice
text, and hosted serves `<strong>DANGER</strong>` and
`<strong>An</strong> <strong>electrical</strong> <strong>outlet</strong> ...`.

The census scanned two seeded random samples of the library (2,400 books,
then 2,400 further books) for every `CFONT` span of these codes, and for each
book whose file name is not all lower case (the hosted catalogue does not
list those) fetched the hosted page of the first topic carrying the code,
checked its `<base href>` and, where the page states it, its book path, and
matched every span of that code in the topic against the page.  A span whose
row the topic does not store (a `CFONT` that ends a record) has no text to
match and was not counted.

[cols="<8,<14,<10,<12,<38",options="header"]
|===
|Code |Name |Books |Spans matched |Not matched

|`T` |`TP` |27 |529 of 529 |--
|`Y` |`MDQUAL` |23 |75 of 75 |--
|`B` |`CAUTION` |197 |294 of 295 |GG66-3252-00 `3.8`, a row whose triples overlap (see below)
|`F` |`CAUTIONTEXT` |197 |9,858 of 9,859 |ADMD1MST `2.1.539` (overlap)
|`D` |`DANGER` |82 |255 of 257 |GG66-3252-00 `3.8` (overlap)
|`O` |`DANGERTEXT` |77 |7,082 of 7,084 |SY27-0341-02 `3.0`: two styled list-bullet cells, served `<strong> </strong>`
|===

The match compares text after the page's own escaping (`"` is served as
`&quot;`) and the hosted degree-sign substitution described in
encoding.adoc.  No word is served in any other element or as plain text.
Four books (`B` in three, `D` in one) have no matchable span in the topic
checked, all of them `CFONT` rows at a record end.  The overlap rows
are the shape the `_` census describes: a span laid over columns another
triple of the same control also styles, where hosted nests the elements
(ADMD1MST `2.1.539`, DT `19910930220308`, serves
`<B><b><B>.<</B>/b>URTHER</B>` for `... 15 1 2 15 1 F 15 7 2 ...`); the code
is still served bold there.

`T` occurs in 27 of the 4,800 sampled books, and those 27 are fewer
independent sources than books.  Six JES2 command references (GC23-0084-02,
GC23-0084-03, EZ2M3102, EZ2M4102, HJEA2MST, SC28-1039-01) share one example
list; seven books (IATB6MST, EZ2M4107, IATA6MST, EZ2M3104, N1AB6MST,
SC23-0088-00, SC23-0073-02) share one message-flow figure and five
(HASA3MST, EZ2M3103, EZ2M4103, HJEA3MST, SC23-0082-01) another; CNMYU0MS and
SC31-6127-01 share a figure, and SC26-4374-01/-02 are two editions of one
book.  ADMD2MST, SH19-6924-04, GC23-0100-00, EDFUMST and GC28-1550-00 are
independent of those and of each other: ten sources in all.  The table
quotes the first `T` row of every book, grouped by source; the hex is the
`CFONT` control's own token bytes from the control word to the last style
code.

[[markup_style_code_message_entry_census]]
Inside message entries the style codes present exactly as they do in
running text.  An entry is everything a topic draws from its first `SRMSG`
control on: the headline, the field labels, the field text and the rows an
entry draws.  `E` (`XMP`) is served `<samp>`, `X` (`XPH`) `<tt>`, `P` (`PK`)
`<kbd>`, `4` (`HP4`) `<TT>`, `C` (`CIT`) `<cite>`, and `W` (`WARNING`) and
`G` (`WARNINGTEXT`) `<em>`, one element per span.  Every sampled book's
`CFONTDEF` table maps each of these codes to the same name.  The one book in
the samples whose names differ is `qb3au202`, which appends ` *` to them
(`XMP *`, `PK *`, `CIT *`).  Its lowercase file name is not catalogued by the
hosted shelf, so its presentation is untested.

A span can stand anywhere along its row.  It is not only the run that opens
a row, as the headline and the field labels do.  SC09-138 `F.1`
(DT `19910321130500`) record 2048 stores `cfont 44 6 E 55 6 E` at file
offset `0x10804a` (`3a a5 ac 70 ee 84 ac 70`) over the row
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;... Use the run-time options NOSTAE and NOSPIE for</literal>].
Hosted serves it inside the `Recovery:` paragraph as
`options <samp>NOSTAE</samp> and <samp>NOSPIE</samp> for`.  A span's columns
also state its own end.  A sentence stop directly after the span stays
outside the element: record 2050's `cfont 47 8 X` over `CEESTART.` is served
`<tt>CEESTART</tt>.`.  Headline and
label spans take the same element.  SC09-138 `H.0` record 2229 stores the
`EDC0845` headline control `cfont 3 7 2 ... 51 2 2 54 1 X 55 1 2` at file
offset `0x116ef1`.  Hosted serves it as
`... <B>value</B> <B>of</B> <tt>0</tt><B>.</B>`: the `X` word is `<tt>`
even inside a headline, not in the `<B>` of the `2` words around it.

The census drew three seeded random samples of the hosted shelf's books
(60, 1,500 and 3,000 books, overlapping).  652 of the books carry an `SRMSG`
topic with at least one styled span after its first `SRMSG`.  For each code,
books were drawn at random from those whose file name is not all lowercase.
For each book, the topic with the most spans of the code was fetched.  Each
page's `<base href>` and its title's topic id were checked against the book
and topic.  Every span of the code after the topic's first `SRMSG` was then
matched in order against the page, word by word.

Five pages fail the identity check and are left out.  Hosted serves another
book under SC31-7542-01, SC31-7526-01 (both DT `19920619000100`) and
SC24-5461-01.  It also serves a different edition of SC31-7568-00 `E.0`, whose
cited title row the hosted page does not draw.

[cols="<6,<8,<8,<8,<14",options="header"]
|===
|Code |Books |Topics |Spans |Served as stated

|`E` |33 |34 |6,844 |6,829 `<samp>`
|`X` |28 |29 |1,204 |1,196 `<tt>`
|`P` |26 |26 |4,849 |4,845 `<kbd>`
|`4` |18 |18 |652 |647 `<TT>`
|`C` |34 |35 |1,630 |1,610 `<cite>`
|`W` |17 |18 |855 |855 `<em>`
|`G` |22 |23 |24,656 |24,655 `<em>`
|===

Every remaining span was checked by hand.  None is a span served another
way; each is a fault of the word matcher.  Some matched the word in the
page's `<title>`.  Some matched an earlier unstyled use of the same word,
which also covers spans in a topic's introduction.  Some matched part of a
longer word: `0` in `ARI0277E`.  Some found no match because the page spells
a value differently, or because the word holds a character the page escapes.
HJEA1MST `10.0` record 745 is typical.  Its `C` span `JES2` matched the
plain `JES2` of `JES2 issues this message`.  The span's own row is served
`<cite>JES2</cite> <cite>Initialization</cite>`.  The census did not test a
span whose first column falls inside a word.  How such a span is presented
is unverified.

The table quotes the first span served as stated of 15 books per code.  The
hex is the `CFONT` control's own token bytes, from the control word to the
last style code, truncated after eight bytes.

`HP5`..`HP9` form a family: each is the underscored form of the code five
below it, so `5` underscores `H0` (plain), `6` `HP1`, `7` `HP2`, `8` `HP3` and
`9` `HP4`. The three verified rows above (`5` plain `<U>`, `7` `<B><U>`,
`9` `<TT><U>`) match that pattern exactly.

The last operand triple of a `CFONT` control can carry a `,` separator glued
to its style code as a prefix-1 token, so the decoded operand reads
`CFONT 4 4 R,`. The comma is neither part of the code nor display text:
ACPZMST1 record 6 segment 16 stores `cfont 4 4 R,` and hosted `FRONT_1.1`
serves pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&lt;B&gt;GUPI&lt;/B&gt;</literal>], exactly what record 7 segment 1's comma-less
`cfont 4 4 R 9 3 R` produces for pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&lt;B&gt;GUPI&lt;/B&gt; &lt;B&gt;end&lt;/B&gt;</literal>]. GC28-183 record
146 stores `cfont 17 1 E,` for the `<samp>.</samp>` rows of topic `2.2.1`.

[[markup_spans_and_the_display_row]]
==== Spans And The Display Row

A `CFONT`/`CSELECT` operand addresses display columns of exactly one display
row, and a highlighted phrase never continues onto the next row: a wrapped
phrase carries its own triple on every row it covers (ACPZMST1 `FRONT_1.1`
splits one `:hp1.` phrase into `cfont 44 11 1 56 11 1` and, on the next row,
`cfont 3 10 1 14 3 1 ...`). Three consequences a reader must honour:

* *Trailing padding.* A span may run past the last visible column of the
row; the excess covers the row's stored trailing padding and styles nothing.
ACPZMST1 `8.1` stores `cselect 3 38 HDRXCCOE` for the 40-column row
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;"Check_On_Event (XCCOE)" in topic 8.2</literal>] and hosted links exactly that
text; GG24-395 `PREFACE.3` stores `cselect 3 22 HDRHPRT100` for the
23-column row pass:[<literal>&#160;| Part 1, "Introduction"</literal>].
* *Wide in-row gaps.* A wide space run between two words does not end the
row while a span of that row still reaches past the text written so far;
the gap is column padding of a two-column layout. ACPZMST1 `3.6` stores
`cselect 43 3 SPTUSERID` and `cfont 3 6 2` for the single hosted row
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;Userid&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;User ID (topic 4.3)</literal>]; DREICMST `2.8.1` stores
`cfont 3 3 2 7 4 2 17 3 2 21 4 2` for pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;RFT Name&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;Log Type</literal>].
* *Row markers hold a column.* The revision-bar/box glyph that opens a row
is not prose text, but it occupies its display column and so does the space
behind it; dropping them shifts every span of the row by two. Hosted
ACPZMST1 `1.2.3.1` serves pass:[<literal>&#160;| A local resource ...</literal>] with `local` at column
5 (`cfont 5 5 3`), and GG24-395 `PREFACE.3` serves pass:[<literal>&#160;| Part 1, "Introduction"</literal>] with `Part` at column 3.
* *The instruction line bounds its operands.* A control opcode and its operand
triples belong to their native display line; identical numeric words on the
next line are display cells, not additional operand fields. FA1PLMM0 `3.2.3`
record 187 line 16 stores `cfont 7 1 2 9 1 2 11 1 2 13 1 2` in bytes
`0x2a837` through `0x2a844`. Line 17 begins at byte `0x2a844`, has a
seven-cell origin at `0x2a845`, and stores the distinct visible words
`3 7 2 2 and provide ...` from `0x2a846`; the four digits occupy the four
columns named by the preceding instruction.

A leading glyph is only a row marker when no pending span covers exactly its
columns. When a span covers the glyph itself, the glyph is styled display
text: FA1PLMM0 `3.5.1` `cfont 5 2 E 8 3 E ...` over pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;// JOB COPY ...</literal>] is
served as `<samp>//</samp> <samp>JOB</samp> ...`, and GC28-183 `2.2.3`
`cfont 5 2 E 15 4 E` over pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;//&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;PEND</literal>] as
pass:[<literal>&lt;samp&gt;//&lt;/samp&gt;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&lt;samp&gt;PEND&lt;/samp&gt;</literal>].

The rule holds unchanged inside a verbatim region.  A `CFONT` that stands
alone on its display line between an `SRTBL` line and its `SRETBL` line
displays nothing and styles the display row directly below it, with
columns that index that row's own cells -- the drawn rules and rails
included.  ITPPIBOK `4.3.5.4` record 158 line 27 (length byte at
`0x3F8FE`, `cfont` at `0x3F8FF`) is `cfont 7 4 2 58 5 2 64 4 2`; line 28
(length byte at `0x3F90B`) draws `   | | TASK ... |      START DATE      | |`
with `TASK` at column 7 (token 200, `0x3F90F`), `START` at column 58 and
`DATE` at column 64 (token 205, `0x3F917`).  Hosted (DT `19910628074854`)
serves `<B>TASK</B>`, `<B>START</B>` and `<B>DATE</B>` there.  The same
holds for the corpus at large: DREICMST `1.2.1` serves
`<B>Program</B>`/`<B>Description</B>`/`<B>Release</B>` and
`<samp>3.1.2</samp>`, SC26-457 `3.16` serves `cfont 17 15 P 32 15 Q 47 1
P` over `[INHIBITSOURCE|NOINHIBITSOURCE]` as
`<kbd>[INHIBITSOURCE|</kbd><dfn>NOINHIBITSOURCE</dfn><kbd>]</kbd>`.  A
span whose first or last column is a blank, or that cuts a word without
meeting an abutting span, is not proven by the row and leaves it plain.

The glyph's columns are those of the display word it begins.  A glyph glued
to more text -- an attach prefix or attach byte in front of the next token,
so no column separates them -- is styled display text when a span covers that
whole word exactly.  SC24-546 record 1026 display line 10 (file offset
`0x5F55D`) is `17 15 2E 01 7B 00 21 01 00 7C 00 2C 01 00 7D 00 2B 12 28 11 D6 68 E7 F9`:
length byte `0x17`, a 5-cell origin run `15`, the one-byte word `'` (`2E`),
the attach-prefixed `.` (`01`), and then `A<.B><.C>`, a 2-cell run, `->`, a
1-cell run, `Incorrect` and `symbol`.  The preceding control is
`cfont 5 2 E 8 9 E 20 2 2 24 9 E 34 6 E`; its first triple covers exactly
the two-cell word `'.` at column 5, not the one-cell glyph.  Hosted SC24-546
`B.1.3.1` (DT `19940323131240`) serves the row as
`<samp>'.</samp> <samp>A&lt;.B&gt;&lt;.C&gt;</samp>   <B>-&gt;</B>  <samp>Incorrect</samp> <samp>symbol</samp>`.

The span is evidence, not the condition.  A row marker holds a display column
of its own, so a glyph glued to the text after it is the first cell of a
display word whether or not any span touches it, and a reader keeps it.
SG24-204 record 230 stores, at file offset `0x2D4482`, the display line
`1D 12 1B 01 00 D5 A1 00 ED DB 00 D9 30 00 ED DB 00 D7 D5 00 1E 2B D9 05 5A E7 0A E3 FD`:
length byte `0x1D` (whose dictionary spelling is `*`, and which is the
row-control slot), a 7-cell origin run `12`, the one-byte word `(` (`1B`),
the attach-prefixed `.` (`01`), an attach byte `00` and `.` (`D5 A1`), then
`\`, `cicscli`, `\`, `bin`, each behind an attach byte, and `)`, `and`,
`change`, `the`, ....  The control on the line before it is `cfont 10 12 3`,
whose columns 10-21 are `\cicscli\bin` only when `(` stands at column 7.
Hosted SG24-204 `5.3.3` (DT `19971218054640`) serves the row as
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;(..&lt;B&gt;&lt;I&gt;\cicscli\bin&lt;/B&gt;&lt;/I&gt;)&#160;and&#160;change&#160;the&#160;required&#160;parameters.</literal>].
`5.2.1`, `5.2.2`, `5.2.3`, `5.3.1` and `5.3.2` repeat the row.  Spans that
split the glued word do not change it either: SC24-546 `3.23.5` record 294
token 209 (`0x275E0`) is `>` + attach `.` + `>` under `cfont 3 2 X 5 1 X`,
served `<tt>&gt;.</tt><tt>&gt;</tt>` (DT `19940323131240`).  And the shape
recurs with no span at all: SH20-918 `4.6.4` record 436 token 181
(`0x61977`, `"` + attach `.` + `TEXT`) is served `".TEXT"`, SC28-1881-05
`1.20` record 561 `*.B`, SC09-138 `5.1.5.1` record 706 `*, you will read`,
IBMMMSTR `1.5.311` record 403 `",GOTO LAB;"` and SC09-138 `A.2.16` `(.).`
(DTs `19910520154851`, `19930326130533`, `19910321130500` and
`19911004151140`).

A free-standing glyph -- a cell of space, or a gap run, between it and the
row's text -- is settled by its column.  A revision mark stands in the
row's margin: the only cell in front of it is the one-cell blank the margin
opens with, so it is at column 1 (GC23-046 `7.2.1` record 200 token 281,
`|` directly before `If you obtained ...`; GC28-183's `:` revision marks).
Hosted prints those marks in the margin, outside the row's text.  A glyph
that stands after the row's origin run, at column 2 or beyond, is a cell of
the row's text and hosted prints it there:

[cols="<26,<36,<38",options="header"]
|===
|Book / topic / record |Stored |Hosted

|N2AH1MST `1.2.4` record 148 |7-cell origin, `=` (token 306), 5-cell run, `Marginal` l|       =      Marginal space (track) situation (JSAM).
|SC24-5520-00 `1.1.30` record 272 |7-cell origin, `*` (token 29), `The last entry` l|       * The last entry is indicated by a 1 in bit zero ...
|SC34-425 `1.7.4.4.2` record 461 |7-cell origin, `*` (token 255), 6-cell run, `To report` l|       *       To report on build map and accounting information.
|SC33-033 `4.106` record 648 |`'` (token 235), `'` (236), `gives a complete list` l|       ' ' gives a complete list of file names.
|GC23-046 `F.0` record 284 |4-cell origin, `(` (token 298), `)` (299), `MVS/SP` l|    ( ) MVS/SP and System/370 are trademarks ...
|SC26-457 `3.6.1.2` record 274 |8-cell origin, `//` (token 282), 13-cell run, `VOL=` l|        //              VOL=SER=(volser1,volser2,...),...
|GG24-395 `3.4.3` record 685 |`\|` at column 1, then `-` (token 90), `POSIX` l| \| - POSIX Support" in topic 3.2.1.
|===

The last row shows both readings on one line: the bar is the margin mark,
the `-` behind it is text.  With the geometry of a bullet the glyph is a
list bullet instead (<<markup_a_second_list_level>>).  A decoder placeholder
run (a box glyph or unmapped word) carries no character and stays a marker
wherever it stands, and the display line's own length byte is the
row-control slot whatever word the dictionary spells for it and whatever is
attached behind it.

A dictionary word made only of `?` characters (`0x3F`) is not such a
placeholder.  The decoder spells an unmapped word `U+FFFF`, never `?`.  The
word is drawn wherever the cells around it on its display line are drawn.
`N2AH1MST` record 2014 line 9 is
`              begins, the message includes "NAME (???)."`.  Token 94 is
the two-byte reference `A9 84` at `0xBB84E`, whose word is `???`.  It
stands between `(` (token 92) and `)` (token 96), with an attach control
(`00`, at `0xBB84D` and `0xBB850`) on each side.  Hosted `23.0` (DT `19910329000100`) serves
`"NAME (???)."` in the `Notes:` of `ICH408I`.  The same holds in
`N2AH1MST` `28.0` (`enter a question mark (?)`), `16.0`
(`'??????' for an unknown volser`) and `SC24-546` `A.0`
(`@ # $ ¢ . ? ! _`).  Hosted serves the question marks in each.

A `?` that ends a row is drawn too.  `SC09-138` record 2165 line 12 is
`   EDC0347 30 Syntax Error: possible missing &1 or &2?`.  The `?` is token
131 (`B1 80` at `0x111A5A`, word `0x3F`), attached to `2` by the attach
control at `0x111A59`, and the headline `cfont` styles it.  Hosted `H.0`
(DT `19910321130500`) serves `<B><I>&amp;2</B></I><B>?</B>`.  `EDC0348`
(line 24, `&1?`) is the same.

[[markup_sub_word_span_boundaries]]
==== Sub-word span boundaries

BookServer styles part of one decoded word wherever the operand's columns fall
inside one.  The boundary is a fact of the operand, not an error, and it always
stays _inside_ the word: a triple never starts in one word and ends in another.

[cols="<19,<21,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Book / topic |Operand |Hosted

|GC23-046 `6.0` |`cfont 43 1 1` |`SMPWRK<I>x</I>`
|SG24-204 `5.2.1` |`cfont 33 1 7 34 1 2` |`<B><U>L</B></U><B>U</B>`
|SC09-138 `3.3.1` |`cfont 24 5 4` |`information on using <TT>CLIST</TT>s.`
|SC09-138 `6.2.8.4` |`cfont 7 4 2 11 3 3` |`<B>EDCK</B><B><I>nnn</I></B>`
|SC09-138 `8.1.10.7` |`cfont 18 7 X 25 3 1` |`The fields <TT>__dsorg</TT><I>xxx</I> refers`
|ACPZMST1 `4.6` |`cfont 3 3 V` |`<var>tpn</var>.`
|===

The pattern behind the plural and suffix cases is that the stem is a dictionary
token and the suffix is stored in the same compiled token, so an implementation
that owns whole tokens cannot represent the split; ownership has to reach a byte
range inside the token's decoded word.

A span that starts or ends inside a word _and_ also covers a blank display
column is not this shape.  It is the signature of a row whose left margin the
reader model has not reproduced: every triple of such a row is displaced by the
same amount, so each one lands inside a word and reaches over the gap to the
next.  GG24-4302-00 `PREFACE.2` is the clearest example -- its rows are stored
as `°` + a two-cell origin run and hosted serves pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;°&#160;&#160;&#160;&lt;cite&gt;IMS/ESA&lt;/cite&gt; &lt;cite&gt;V5&lt;/cite&gt; &lt;cite&gt;Release&lt;/cite&gt; ...</literal>] at column 7, so a model that puts the
text at column 2 reads `cfont 7 7 C` as `SA V5 R`.

[[markup_a_selector_label_glued_to_a_word]]
==== A selector label glued to the word in front of it

There is one exception, and it is a `CSELECT` label.  The book compiler can
write a cross-reference label with no space between it and the running text
before it, and the dictionary then holds the glued pair as one word.  The
selector's columns start inside that stored word, at the first cell of the
label, and run over the blanks between the label's own words to the end of
the label.  Hosted BookServer serves the stored text unchanged and opens the
link mid-word:

[cols="<17,<37,<46",options="header"]
|===
|Book / topic |Bytes and operand |Hosted

|SC24-5527-02 `4.2.3` |record 399, token at `0x346D7` is `DA 4B`, the word `inTable`; `cselect 57 9 TBLXREXBL` (`4D EF 4A CE E9 7A` at `0x346BC`) |`in<a href="...#TBLXREXBL">Table 4-8</a> that was` (DT `19921218151459`)
|SG24-204 `5.3.3` |record 233, token at `0x2D49C9` is `E0 24`, the word `inFigure`; `cselect 45 26 FIGTRASR07` |`in<a href="4.2.2.4?...#FIGTRASR07">Figure 90 in topic 4.2.2.4</a>.` (DT `19971218054640`)
|SC21-8295-03 `3.1.3.2` |record 391, token at `0x44390` is `E3 76`, the word `manualS`; `cselect 32 41 LNK ... <SC21-7985> ...` |`manual<a href="../../DOCNUM/SC21-7985/..."><cite>S/36</cite> ... <cite>User's</cite></a>` (DT `19970409135933`)
|===

Such a label both splits a word and covers blank columns, which is also the
signature of a displaced margin, so the label alone does not prove its row.
The reading holds when all of these are true:

* the split falls between two cells of one stored token;
* the label ends on a word boundary or at the end of the row; and
* another `CFONT` or `CSELECT` triple of the same row lands on whole display
words.  In the three rows above these are `cfont 3 2 2` on the list number
`1.`; `cfont 7 6 2 14 8 2 23 10 2` on `TCP/IP Listener Definition`; and
`cfont 37 8 C 46 7 C ...` on `Business Graphic ...`.

A displaced margin moves every triple of its row by the same amount.  A
triple of the row that lands on whole words is therefore evidence that the
row's columns are right.  A `CFONT` triple of this shape has not been
observed, and neither has a label torn at both ends.  Both remain
unverified.

[[markup_the_three_column_left_margin]]
==== The three-column left margin

Every reflowed prose row BookServer serves begins with a three-column margin:
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;</literal>] normally and pass:[<literal>&#160;|&#160;</literal>] when the row carries a revision change bar.  The
columns a `CFONT`/`CSELECT` operand names count from column 0 of that margin.

The change bar is stored as a `U+2502` (or ASCII `|`) width-1 token in the
leading whitespace of the row's display line, before the row's origin run, and
it stands for the whole margin -- the row's origin run then measures only the
indent after it.

Under the display-line model the margin is not inferred at all, it is read:
the line is `<length byte> <space run> <bar> <space run> <first word>`, and the
margin is the display columns the line spends before that first word minus the
cells of the space run that ends them.  The bar occupies one column and the
assembler inserts one space behind it, so a one-cell leading run gives the
documented three columns.  Byte-level, ACPZMST1 record 459 display line 11 is
tokens 114..118 -- length byte (value 26), a one-cell space (value 18), the
`U+2502` bar (value 150), a four-cell space run (value 21) and `XC` -- whose
cells are `' '`, `'|'`, the inserted space, four origin cells and the text at
column 7, exactly the hosted row.  OFCUSEOV record 839 line 0 stores the same
shape with an ASCII bar (value 135) and a `U+2666` bullet behind it.

[cols="<17,<24,<45,<14",options="header"]
|===
|Book / topic |Stored |Hosted |Operand

|ACPZMST1 `8.14.1` |`U+2502` + 4-cell origin l| \|     XC_NOTIFY_MSG, VM PWSCS ... |`cfont 7 13 9`
|GC23-046 `6.1` |`U+2502` + 4-cell origin l| \|     <B>Note:</B>  If you decide ... |`cfont 7 5 2`
|GG24-395 `2.4.1` |`U+2502` + 4-cell origin l| \|     variable until the <I>Thread</I> <I>Y</I> ... |`cfont 35 7 1`
|===

A bullet glyph behind the bar keeps the assembler's space between them, so the
bullet stands at column 3 and the row's text at column 7: GG24-395 `PREFACE.3`
stores `U+2502` + `U+2666` + a two-cell gap for pass:[<literal>&#160;| °&#160;&#160;&#160;Chapter 1, "A Client/Server Overview"</literal>] and links it with `cselect 7 37`; GC23-046 `7.5.4`
stores the same shape for pass:[<literal>&#160;| °&#160;&#160;&#160;Do an APPLY CHECK ... Figure 19 ...</literal>] with
`cselect 53 12`.  OFCUSEOV `6.4.3` proves the same columns from the other side:
its revised list items (pass:[<literal>&#160;|&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;°&#160;&#160;&#160;Leave the prompt blank ...</literal>], record 839)
carry the bullet at column 7 and the text at column 11, the columns its
unrevised siblings (pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;°&#160;&#160;&#160;Type a 1 (Use) ...</literal>]) already carry, so the list
is aligned only when the bar's three columns are counted.

A display line may also spend *two or more* space runs before its first
word, with no change bar at all; the row model reads such a pair as the
fill/origin pair that opens the _next_ row and the row then loses every column
the first run spans.  The line's own cells give the margin here too, and the
`CFONT` operand corroborates it by naming the column of the line's first word.
Title pages are the shape that proves it, one wide row per line:

[cols="<25,<45,<21,>13",options="header"]
|===
|Book / record / line |Stored |Operand |First word at

|SC24-546 record 3 line 17 |length byte (token 83), 63-cell run, 3-cell run, `Release` |`cfont 66 7 2 74 3 2` |66
|N2AH1MST record 2 line 10 |token 33, 63-cell run, 7-cell run, `MVS` |`cfont 70 7 2` |70
|IBMMMSTR record 2 line 12 |token 57, 63-cell run, 2-cell run, `Programming` |`cfont 65 12 2` |65
|===

Hosted serves all three rows at those columns (DTs `19940323131240`,
`19910329000100`, `19911004151140`).  The corroboration is required: without
it the same measurement re-indents the verbatim rows of a `cz OFF XMP`
listing, so it is limited to the flattened dialect and to lines whose leading
whitespace really is more than one run.

[[markup_the_list_bullet_is_display_structure_between_controls]]
==== The list bullet is display structure between controls

A `U+2666` bullet claimed by no control segment is the row's list marker, not
padding, wherever the row's text follows it.  GG24-4302-00 `PREFACE.2`
continues one bibliography list across a record boundary: record 31's first
display line is `<length byte> <three-cell origin> <U+2666> <two-cell gap> IMS`, and the bullet reaches the token stream between the `cfont 7 7 C 15 2 C 18 9 C 28 10 C 39 3 C 43 9 C` that ends record 30 and that control's own
payload.  Hosted (DT 19950308184737) serves the row as pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;°&#160;&#160;&#160;&lt;cite&gt;IMS/ESA&lt;/cite&gt; ...</literal>] exactly like the sixteen sibling rows inside record 30, whose bullets are
control payload; dropping the glyph moves the row's text from column 7 to
column 2 and displaces every operand column of that `cfont`.

[[markup_a_second_list_level]]
==== A second list level draws an ASCII glyph

A list nested in a list item is drawn with the same geometry one level down
and an ASCII glyph in place of `U+2666`: the glyph stands at the parent
item's text column, three blank cells follow it, and the sub-item's text
starts four columns after the glyph.  The glyph is a one-byte content cell of
its display line -- `-` in most books, `>` in SG24-204 -- and the gap is the
same two-cell blank run (value `13`) a `U+2666` bullet is followed by, plus
the assembler's space.  Hosted BookServer prints the glyph in its column.

[cols="<22,<38,<40",options="header"]
|===
|Book / topic / record |Stored (tokens) |Hosted

|ACPZMST1 `1.2` record 32
|116 seven-cell run (`18` at `0xA3BC`), 117 `-` (`24` at `0xA3BD`), 118 two-cell run (`13`), `Resource manager`
l|   °   Communications partners
       -   Resource manager
       -   User program

|SG24-204 `1.1` record 21
|seven-cell run, 105 `>`, two-cell run, `OS/2`
l|   °   IBM Transaction Server platforms, including:

       >   OS/2
       >   AIX

|DREICMST `1.7.2.1` record 262
|eleven-cell run, 131 `-`, two-cell run, `Possible errors ...`
l|       1.  Was the table listed after ...

           -   Possible errors in the table definition ...
|===

The sub-list may follow its parent's row directly (ACPZMST1) or after an
empty display line (QSYSNEWG `1.9`, SG24-204 `1.1`); either way the glyph
column equals the parent's text column (`U+2666` at column 3, text and `-` at
column 7).  Where the rows above are a paragraph rather than a list item
(DREICMST `1.7.2.1`, under the `1.` row's continuation), the glyph rows are a
list of their own at that column.  The ASCII glyph is told apart from the
revision margin by its column: a change bar or revision mark is the only cell
in front of its row's origin run (column 1, after a one-cell blank), while a
list glyph stands after an origin run of at least two cells.  No glyph other
than `-` and `>` occurs in this geometry in the corpus.

[[markup_a_span_holds_its_row_open]]
==== A span holds its row open

A span of the open row that reaches past the display cells written so far
proves the row has not ended, so a one-byte token standing at that point is
display text and not the next display line's length byte.  The record's own
length-prefixed display-line structure is the arbiter: a token that opens one of
those lines is a row boundary whatever the spans say.

[cols="<18,<45,<38",options="header"]
|===
|Book / topic |Row |Operand

|SC24-546 `4.3.1` l|     <samp>ABBREV('Print','Pri')</samp>      -><B>    1</B> |`cfont 5 21 E 32 2 2 38 1 E`
|IEAC6MST `5.1.2` l|          <samp>RECORDSIZE(384 3072)</samp> <samp>)</samp> |the trailing `)` is a styled cell
|SC09-138 `8.4.2.5` l|          a = b*(x*y*z);            /* Duplicates recognized */ |the `;` had been read as a length byte
|===

[[markup_a_wide_gap_is_several_runs]]
==== A wide in-row gap is stored as several runs

The widest single space-run token observed spells 63 cells.  A row whose gap
is wider stores it as consecutive runs, which has the shape of the
fill/origin pair that ends one row and indents the next.  Inside a display
line that the open row is already drawing, a span of that row that names
columns past the cells written so far settles it: the length byte frames the
row, the runs are one gap, and the span's columns land on the row's own
cells.

SC33-033 record 872 line 18 (file offset `0x51A54`) is
`0A 0D 80 00 21 1F 0B 21 09 E8 EE`: length byte `0x0A` (10 bytes), a 5-cell
origin run `0D`, `C`, the attach byte `00`, `*`, a 63-cell run `1F`, a
3-cell run `0B`, `*`, a 1-cell run `09`, and the sequence number
`02100000` (`E8 EE`).  Laid out with the synthetic space after each visible
token, the closing `*` stands at column 74 and the sequence number at 77,
exactly the columns of the preceding `cfont 5 2 E 74 1 E 77 8 E`.  Hosted
SC33-033 `A.3.2` (DT 19930422134757) serves the line as one row,
`<samp>C*</samp>` ... `<samp>*</samp>  <samp>02100000</samp>`.  The same
shape recurs throughout SC33-033 `A.3.2`-`A.3.7`, `B.4.1.1` and `B.4.1.2`.

[[markup_a_styled_row_final_word]]
==== A styled `,` or `?` can end a row in front of a control

A `,` or `?` spelled by a two-byte dictionary word and standing as the last
token of its display line, directly before the display line of the next
control, is claimed by no control segment and has the spelling of decoder
punctuation.  It is the row's last displayed cell exactly when a `CFONT` span
of that row covers its column; with no such span it is padding.

[cols="<22,<34,<44",options="header"]
|===
|Book / topic |Bytes |Row and operand

|SC24-546 `D.0` |record 1118 offset `0x65AB9`: `05 1D D8 64 C7 BD 17` |length 5, 14-cell run, `LTORG`, `,` (`C7 BD`); next byte `17` opens the next line.  `cfont 14 5 E 20 1 E`; hosted (DT 19940323131240) `<samp>LTORG</samp> <samp>,</samp>`
|SC24-546 `E.1` |record 1158 line 6 |`              SPACE ,` under `cfont 14 5 E 20 1 E`; hosted the same
|IEAC6MST `4.8.2` |record 355 line 3, `?` at `0x856BE` (`CF FF`) |`... STACK X; LOCATE ?` under `... 63 6 E 70 1 E`; hosted (DT 19920124000100) `<samp>?</samp>` at column 70
|GG24-4302-00 `4.3.3.2` |record 355 line 0 |`... ICDSN(ICF51.DE03AR05.COPY1) ?` under `... 58 27 E 86 1 E` (stored at the end of record 354); hosted (DT 19950308184737) `<samp>?</samp>`
|===

[[markup_a_span_disproves_an_empty_row]]
==== A span disproves an empty row

The same evidence settles the opposite reading of a marker slot.  A width-1
token carrying a word, followed by a run of spaces, has the shape of the
marker/origin pair that opens the next display row, and a reader that takes it
as one produces a row with no display text at all.  Two different things
produce that shape:

* a genuinely blank display row, whose marker really is a slot -- the
questionnaire and COMMENTS forms of SC31-711 are full of them; and
* the last word of the row that is still open, whose trailing padding the
compact encoder stored as a separate token.

The control's own operand tells them apart, because a `CFONT`/`CSELECT`
operand names the display columns of exactly one display row.  Lay the row's
stored text out from the marker slot that opened it, place the candidate word
at its end, and look for the single left-margin shift under which one triple
covers that word exactly and every other triple of the same control lands on a
whole display word of the same row.  Where that shift exists and is unique, the
word is styled display text and there is no row boundary in front of it.

QSYSINFO record 631 segment 15 stores
`cfont 39 9 1 49 12 1 62 5 1` over
pass:[<literal>___&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;--&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;SX41-9072&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;Automatic Installation Guide</literal>].  Measured
from the `___` marker the three words stand at 36, 46 and 59, so the shift is
the documented three-column margin and column 62 is `Guide`; hosted
`APPENDIX1.4.2.1` (DT 19910524120827) serves
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;___&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;--&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;SX41-9072&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&lt;I&gt;Automatic&lt;/I&gt; &lt;I&gt;Installation&lt;/I&gt; &lt;I&gt;Guide&lt;/I&gt;</literal>].  The same holds for a row stored across the record boundary:
record 631's trailing `cfont 39 5 1 45 6 1 52 3 1 56 8 1 65 5 1` addresses the
row pass:[<literal>___&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;01&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;SC41-0036&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;Basic Backup and Recovery Guide</literal>]
whose bytes are in record 632, the record the run continues into.

The shift is a left margin, so it is never negative.  A row whose stored bytes
run _past_ the columns the operand names -- a decoder placeholder run in front
of the first display cell is the usual cause -- has no proven mapping onto the
operand's coordinates, and such a row keeps its boundary.

A `CFONT` span wholly inside a `CSELECT` span decorates the link text rather
than nesting a separate phrase: hosted ACPZMST1 `8.1` serves
`<a href="8.2..."><B>&quot;Check_On_Event</B> ... <B>8.2</B></a>`.

Exact visual mapping for the style codes not listed above is still a
presentation rule that needs direct renderer confirmation; the storage layer
identifies the semantic style code.

[[markup_cross_references_and_menus]]
=== Cross-References And Menus

Inline links and cross references use `CSELECT` with a target id and display
span information. The target id can name a topic, generic anchor, figure,
table, or picture.

[cols="<11,<39,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Control |Observed syntax |Role

|`CSELECT` |`CSELECT <column> <length> <target_id>` |Link or selectable reference.
|`SR<id>` |`SRsptproc` |Generic anchor target for a `CSELECT` reference.
l|SRFTN<id> |`SRFTNFTNUNIQ1` |Footnote anchor target for a generated footnote body.
|`SREFTN` |`SREFTN` |Ends a generated footnote body.
|`CMENU` |`CMENU` |Starts a menu/list of selectable items.
|`CMITEM` |`CMITEM <topic_id> <text>` |Menu item target and label.
|`CEMENU` |`CEMENU` |Ends a menu/list.
|===

Examples:

[cols="<18,<45",options="header"]
|===
|File |Decoded evidence

|`QS3X36CM.BOO` |`CMENU CMITEM 1.1 Displaying as/400 Commands Online CEMENU`
|`packet.boo` |Topic `1.0`: `CMENU`, `CMITEM 1.1 Original Packet Radio`, `CMITEM 1.2 Ham Packet Radio`, `CMITEM 1.3 Bringing it Together`, `CEMENU`; hosted BookServer renders these as linked `Subtopics:` entries.
|`packet.boo` |Topic `1.1`: `CSELECT 16 4 FTNFTNUNIQ1 ... technologies. (1)` followed later by `SRFTNFTNUNIQ1`, `CZ FLOW FN ...`, and `SREFTN`; hosted BookServer links only `(1)` and renders the footnote at the bottom under anchor `FTNFTNUNIQ1`. Topic `5.1.2` has consecutive selectors `CSELECT 11 5 FTNFTNUNIQ57` and `CSELECT 16 5 FTNFTNUNIQ58`; only the second carries the completed line `locator: (44) (45)`, and the two five-cell spans select `(44)` and `(45)` on that same line.
|`packet.boo` |Full raw render contains 67 footnote records and 67 unique `FTNFTNUNIQ...` ids, so the generated ids can be reused as stable Markdown anchors for this document.
|`QS3X36CM.BOO` |pass:[<literal>CSELECT 33 3 sptproc ... System/36 procedures&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;Page 2.1</literal>] selects `2.1`; target anchor `SRsptproc` appears inside topic `2.1`.
|`GG24-4302-00.boo` |Topic `NOTICES`: `CSELECT 43 30 HDRNOTICES ... to read the general information under "Special Notices" in` selects `"Special Notices" in` plus fixed-layout padding, and `CSELECT 5 13 HDRNOTICES ... topic FRONT_1.` selects `topic FRONT_1` but not the period.
|`GG24-4302-00.boo` |`CSELECT 3 8 fig4302hp1 ... Figure 1.` selects `Figure 1`.
|===

[[markup_cmitem_terminal_marker_slots]]
==== CMITEM terminal marker slots

Verified: a `CMITEM` payload can end with a compact token that belongs to the
physical menu-row boundary rather than to the visible link label.  BookServer
omits that token.  This is not a rule that every one-byte terminal token is
discardable: dictionary tokens of the same encoded width can expand to genuine
title content.

The following offsets are absolute BOO payload byte offsets.  Each listed
token has encoded width one, has no independent row-origin prefix, occupies at
most three decoded display cells, and is the only difference between the
decoded `CMITEM` label and the canonical target title.

[cols="<26,<23,>10,>12,<21,<35",options="header"]
|===
|Fixture and topic |Logical record / target |BOO byte |Encoded byte |Decoded boundary text |BookServer-visible ending

|`SC31-711.boo` `2.4` |LR 70 / `2.4.2` |`0xE8EC` |`0x13` |`<` |`...AIX Workstation`
|`SC31-711.boo` `2.4` |LR 70 / `2.4.5` |`0xE91E` |`0x1B` |`"` |`Customer Information`
|`SC31-711.boo` `4.1` |LR 98 / `4.1.1` |`0x1064A` |`0x18` |`>` |`Generic Traps`
|`SC31-711.boo` `BACK_1` |LR 518 / `BACK_1.1` |`0x33184` |`0x13` |`<` |`...AIX Publications`
|`SC31-711.boo` `BACK_1` |LR 518 / `BACK_1.8` |`0x3320A` |`0x2C` |`can` |`AIX Operating System Publications`
