(`   lnm_agent_found Initiates the discovery of a specified agent.`, topic
1.3) and a key reaching the value column takes a row of its own
(`   /usr/OV/registration/C` above `                       LNM for AIX
registration files`, topic 1.2). Both readings come from the display lines,
not from the grid geometry. So a reader prints the grid's rows as the
native display lines, header row first, keeping the empty lines between
entries; joining a key and its value as running text would lose which word
is the key. The rows before the header are ordinary prose. Word for word,
those display lines are hosted's text for all three topics (117, 87 and
296 words).

[[markup_terminal_toc_and_numeric_message_marker_slots]]
==== Terminal TOC and numeric-message marker slots

The same requirement to retain encoded-token identity applies outside implicit
grids, but each consumer needs its own structural boundary. In the generated
SC31-711 table of contents, compact punctuation tokens occur after the visible
title and immediately before the next `CTOCE` control: logical record 7 token
70 is one-byte reference `17` resolving to `/`, token 115 is reference `18`
resolving to `>`, and token 161 is reference `13` resolving to `<`; logical
record 8 token 136 is another reference `13`. These tokens are outside the
preceding title and must not become its final character. Punctuation embedded
inside the title remains ordinary title content, and a visually identical
two-byte dictionary token does not establish this terminal-marker role.

Numeric `SRMSG` streams provide a separately bounded example. In logical
record 161, a one-byte token resolving to `a` is directly followed by the
exact three-space content-origin token and then `during`; that `a` occupies the
physical marker slot and is not part of the message sentence. Earlier in the
same sentence a genuine article `a` is followed by a 17-space token before a
separate three-space token and `station`. Direct adjacency to the established
row origin distinguishes the structural slot from the lexical article. The
decoded spelling and numeric reference are book-local evidence only; neither
is a universal marker vocabulary.

[[markup_flattened_prose_display_rows]]
=== Flattened prose display rows

Paragraphs may continue across logical records before a later font instruction.
In `SC24-546` topic `A.0`, record 966's final display line begins at
`0x5A7AE` with length byte `1B` (hex) and ends with `3 and 5 (if`.
Record 967 begins at `0x5A7CC` with length byte `19` (hex); its first
display line has a three-space origin and continues `the initial requirements
for storage could not be met) and 26 (if on exit`. This is ordinary drawn
text, not an operand of the later font instruction. That instruction begins
at record 967 token 239, whose length byte `25` (hex) is at `0x5A90A`,
and styles the later `HELP MSG DMSnnnE` example. A later font instruction
does not establish the meaning or paragraph membership of earlier rows.

The same record has an empty display line at token 108, file offset
`0x5A85D`, encoded as length byte `00`. Such native blank rows establish
paragraph separation; merely crossing the record boundary does not. Preserve
the drawing and spacing of complete native text rows independently of any
surrounding subject-index or font instructions.

A complete font instruction may instead end a logical record with its
target text at the beginning of the next record. In `SC34-425` topic
`2.4.9`, record 1483 ends with a line at `0x15BD83`: length byte `09`
followed by `CFONT 3 6 K 10 5 K` (tokens 329--335, nine encoded bytes).
Record 1484 begins at `0x15BD8F`: length byte `05`, three-space origin
token `11` (hex), dictionary key `D446` for `Return`, and key `B3D2` for
`Codes`. The target line's own cells place `Return` at column 3 and
`Codes` at column 10, exactly matching the preceding instruction's spans.
Code `K` selects the inline heading style `H4`; it does not open a new topic.
The following empty row begins at `0x15BD95` with length byte `00`.
Entry-headline origins need not equal these introduction coordinates: the
same topic's first message headline begins at column 8. Verify font columns
against the target line itself, not an origin inferred from later entries.

The same topic contains a source-positioned cross-reference in a message
field. Record 1487 at `0x15C0BE` stores length byte `06`, then the six
reference bytes `40 95 ED 0D C6 60`, decoding to `CSELECT 21 40 HDRTELDB`.
The next line begins at `0x15C0C5` with length byte `1F`; its columns
`[21,61)` draw `Chapter 17, "Ada/370 Setup" in topic 2.8`. These coordinates
identify that occurrence of the link label within the source line, even
when the same words appear elsewhere in the message. Reflowing a field
does not remove the reference or change its anchor identifier.

Ordinary prose topics of the 1.x books (SC31-711, QSYSINFO, SC24-5520-00,
FA1PLMM0, SC09-138, ...) store each formatted display line as tokens whose
spacing runs and one-byte slots encode the row geometry. BookServer renders
them inside `<pre width="80">` with one `<p>` per paragraph, which makes the
hosted page an exact oracle for the grammar below (checked over SC31-711
`2.2.1`, `2.2.3`, `2.3.2`, `3.2`, `2.4.5`, `2.4.7`, `PREFACE.1`, QSYSINFO
`1.1.5`, `1.1.6`, SH20-918 `2.2.2`, FA1PLMM0 `CHANGES.1`, `2.2.2`, ITPPIBOK
`PREFACE.2`, GG24-4302-00 `9.4.7`; hosted DTs in `AnalysisNotes/`).

[cols="<45,<45,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Token shape |Meaning |Evidence

|Two or more adjacent space-run tokens |Row break: every run but the last is end-of-row fill, the last is the next row's origin (indent). A one-byte word before the pair is visible text (the row's last word). |SC31-711 LR88 `is` + 18 spaces + 3 spaces + `managing`; hosted `...that it is` / `managing by`. QSYSINFO LR44 `of` + 6 + 3 spaces; the legacy route dropped these words.
|Two or more adjacent space-run tokens inside the display line the open row is drawing, with a `CFONT`/`CSELECT` span of that row naming columns past the cells written so far |Not a row break: one wide in-row gap stored as several runs (see <<markup_a_wide_gap_is_several_runs>>). Without such a span the row-break reading above stands. |SC33-033 `A.3.2` record 872 line 18 `     C*` + 63 + 3 spaces + `*  02100000` under `cfont 5 2 E 74 1 E 77 8 E`; hosted serves one 85-column row.
|One-byte visible token + one space run of 3 or more cells + visible token |The one-byte token is the row's marker slot (not displayed), the run is the origin. Applies to glyphs (`( ) - < > / = : "`), placeholder runs, and compact dictionary words alike. |SC31-711 LR57 `application` + 3 spaces + `__`, `a` + 3 spaces + `suggestions`; LR90 `action` + 3 spaces; hosted shows none of these words. LR57 `,` + 10 spaces + `log` renders `trapd` / `log` without the comma.
|One-byte visible token + a 1-cell space run |Not a row break: the run is a literal inter-sentence space and the token is text. |SC31-711 LR57 `2.1.` + 1 space + `Next`; hosted pass:[<literal>topic 2.1.&#160;&#160;Next</literal>].
|Standalone punctuation glyph + fill/origin pair |Marker slot in both shapes. Attached punctuation (`conditions:`, `useful.`, bare spacing token before it) stays text. |SC31-711 LR41 `Getting` `/` + `cfont` + 4 spaces + 3 spaces + `Started`; hosted `Getting` / `Started`. LR58 `useful.` + 13 + 10 spaces keeps the period.
|Bare spacing-prefix token (byte `00`, decoded prefix 1 alone) after the last visible token of a row |One `<p>` before the next row. Two bare tokens produce two `<p>`. A bare token followed by a visible token is attach glue (`Note` `:`, `AS` `/` `400`, `X'48'`). |SC31-711 LR88 `resource.` + bare + 18 + 3 spaces + `All traps` is a hosted `<p>`; LR58 tokens 52-55 `.` + bare + bare + `)` + 3 spaces give `<p><p>`; LR58 `useful.` + 13 + 10 spaces without a bare token stays inside the item.
|`0x2666` glyph (`◆`, hosted `°`) as the first cell after the origin, then a 2-cell run |Unordered list item; continuation rows use a deeper origin. Items are not separated by `<p>` in SC31-711 `2.2.3` but are in `2.3.2`. |SC31-711 LR41 `cfont 11 5 2 21 4 2` + 10 + 3 spaces + `◆` + 2 spaces + `The ovwdb`; hosted pass:[<literal>°&#160;&#160;&#160;The &lt;B&gt;ovwdb&lt;/B&gt;</literal>].
|Placeholder run (box-drawing `0x2500`-`0x25FF`, unmapped words) |Never text: a marker slot before an origin, trailing fill before a control, the slot glued between `ST` and the title, or a visual row marker (`\|`, `U+2502`) opening a row directly before its text. |GC23-046 LR151 `ST` + bare + `\|` + `CIDTABL`; ACPZMST1 LR78 `csourcefn ACPFWCMD` + `U+2510` + `ST` + bare + `U+2502` + `Querying`; hosted prints the `U+2502` rows as `\|`.
|`SI <term words>` up to the first row break |Hidden subject-index term; the row break after it starts body text. The term's last word can be the two-run marker word of that break. |SC31-711 LR88 `SI traps, working with` + 14 + 3 spaces + `LNM for AIX obtains`.
|===

Display columns for `CFONT` and `CSELECT` spans count the origin cells, each
visible token's cells and one synthetic space after every visible token
(none after a space run or after a prefix-2 token, none before a prefix-0/1
token): `cfont 11 5 2` on pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;◆&#160;&#160;The ovwdb</literal>] selects `ovwdb`, `cselect 62 13`
on pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;have form numbers ... in "Other AS/400</literal>] selects `"Other AS/400`
(QSYSINFO LR44). A span ends before attached punctuation (`AIX.`,
`"Bibliography"`), as hosted `<cite>AIX</cite>.` shows.

[[markup_structured_subject_index_display_lines]]
==== Structured subject-index display lines

A record payload is a sequence of display lines
(`<length byte><that many bytes of tokens>`, see <<logical-controls>> "Display Lines Inside A Record
Payload"), and an `SI` subject-index entry always occupies exactly one such
line.  Nothing on that line is displayed.

Beside the plain `SI <term words>` form, the compiler emits a _structured_
entry whose fields are separated by one-byte decoder placeholders (words the
dictionary does not map, printed as `?`):

----
SI <sep2> <format> <sep1> <flag> <sep1> <term words> [<sep1> <term words>]...
----

[cols="<40,<45,<29",options="header"]
|===
|File / record |Display line |Hosted

|`QSYSINFO.BOO` record 72, tokens 43-56 |`SI ??3HI1?0?Physical Planning Guide` |nothing (DT `19910524120827`)
|`SC09-138.boo` record 132, tokens 35-54 l|SI ??4XMP@?0?AGGREGATE?  compile-time option |nothing (DT `19910321130500`)
|`SC09-138.boo` record 132, from token 56 l|SI ??4XMP@?0?NOAGGREGATE?  compile-time option |nothing
|===

The `<sep2>` after the keyword is a one-byte token holding two unmapped
words, `<sep1>` one; `<format>` is a digit followed by a GML highlight or
example tag (`3HI1`, `4XMP@`) and `<flag>` has been `0` in every observed
entry.  The fields are not otherwise decoded here because no reader output
depends on them.

An `SI` line also occurs _inside_ an `SRTBL ... SRETBL` envelope, where it
is equally invisible and is neither a table row nor preformatted text.
`SC09-138.boo` 4.1.4 (DT `19910321130500`) wraps its `LANG` envelope around
the three entries `SI ENGLISH run-time messages`,
`SI UENGLISH run-time messages` and `SI KANJI run-time messages`, one per
display line between the grid rows; the hosted page for that topic contains
no `SI` byte at all.  `SC24-5527-02.boo` 4.1.1 record 380 opens the
`XSESDSK` envelope with `SI VMSES/E, service disks` on the first line after
`SRTBLXSESDSK` and the caption `Table 4-1. ...` on the next.

The earlier reading of a "visible tail after `?`" on an `SI` row was an
artifact of the flattened decoded string: the tail is the _next_ display
line.  `QSYSNEWG.BOO` record 40 holds `SI display station` on one line and
`| If your display station screen is blank, ...` on the next, and hosted
serves exactly the second line.

Because an `SI` line displays nothing, it is transparent to the paragraph
structure around it.  An empty display line before one or more `SI` lines
is still the paragraph break before the text that follows them.
`ITPPIBOK.BOO` 1.1 record 34 ends a paragraph with `   system testing.`
(line 17); line 18 is empty (length byte `00` at `0x3753F`); lines 19-20
are `SI TPNS, host processor requirements for` and `SI host processor, TPNS
requirements` (length bytes at `0x37540` and `0x37549`); line 21 opens
`    TPNS Version 3 Release 2 ...` (length byte at `0x37551`).  Hosted (DT
`19910628074854`) serves `system testing.`, `<p>`, then `TPNS Version 3`.
Across the 37 books examined this shape -- an empty line, `SI` lines, then
text -- stands before 1961 paragraphs in 794 topics of 27 books, and
hosted serves `<p>` at every one of the sampled ones.

The first display line after an `SI` line is sometimes set one column
deeper than the lines that continue its paragraph.  `ITPPIBOK.BOO` 4.3.5.4
record 158 line 10 is `SI schedule, example`; line 11 opens with a
four-cell blank run (token 43 at `0x3F84C`) before `The final section of
the test plan indicates the schedule to follow`, and line 12 goes on with
a three-cell run (token 57 at `0x3F85F`) before `during the test.`  No
empty display line stands between lines 11 and 12, and hosted (DT
`19910628074854`) prints both rows, the extra blank included, inside one
paragraph.  Over the corpus the step is always exactly one column (four
cells, then three) and always a sentence running on, in 144 paragraphs of
136 topics in 9 books, while more than two thousand paragraphs that follow
an `SI` line keep their margin on their first line.  The extra column is part of the first
row, not an outdent of the second, so a reader keeps such rows in one
paragraph with the margin of the rows that continue it.

[[markup_drawn_box_regions_in_prose]]
==== Drawn box regions in prose

A "screen", "note" or syntax-diagram box outside any `SRFIG`/`SRTBL`
envelope is drawn straight into the topic's display lines with the
box-drawing words `U+2500`-`U+2518`.  Hosted BookServer prints those lines
verbatim inside its `<pre width="80">`, applying the display glyphs of <<logical-controls>> (`_` for `U+2500`, `|` for
`U+2502` and the bottom/side junctions, blank for the top corners).

A region is

[cols="<11,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Row |Geometry

|top rule |`U+250C` at column L, `U+2510` at column R > L; the interior carries the rule and, when the box is labelled, the label (`___ In a Hurry? ___`)
|side |`U+2502` (or the `U+251C`/`U+2524` junctions) at exactly L and R; the interior is drawn content, including the rails and junctions of an inner grid
|bottom rule |`U+2514` at L, `U+2518` at R, only rules, junctions and spaces between
|===

with an optional change bar (the ASCII `|` word the reader prints in the left
margin) before column L on any row, and `cfont` control lines allowed between
the rows.  All rows share L and R; a candidate with no matching bottom rule
is not a region.

A standalone native display row can nevertheless begin with box-drawing
cells without belonging to a closed region.  SC24-546 topic `1.3.1`, logical
record 45 display line 16, occupies BOO bytes
`0x140B4`--`0x140C4` (payload offsets `0x00AE`--`0x00BE`):
`0F C6CE 08 00 DAA5 00 C7C0 00 D741 00 C667`.  `0F` declares the following
15 encoded bytes, `C6CE` decodes to the 22-column origin, `08` to
`U+2514 U+2500`, each `00` attaches its neighbours, and `C667` to
`U+2500 U+2518`; the complete 39-column row is
`                      |_optional_item_|`.  The preceding display line is
`cfont 24 13 V`, styling the 13 columns of `optional_item` while leaving both
box-drawing pairs visible.  The explicit empty native row follows at line 17.
Thus native line framing and column ownership, rather than the existence of a
closed box envelope, decide whether these leading glyphs are drawn.

[cols="<40,<45,<45",options="header"]
|===
|File / record |Region |Hosted

|`QSYSNEWG.BOO` record 18, tokens 75-204 |labelled note box `___ In a Hurry? ___` |DT `19910524085706`, 11 `<pre>` lines, identical
|`OFCUSEOV.BOO` records 151-153 |5250 screen box with a change bar on most rows |DT `19900805103816`, identical
|`SH20-918.boo` record 140, tokens 43-209 |syntax diagram with an inner grid of `U+2502` rails |DT `19910520154851`, identical
|===

Any display line whose every token lies in the opcode/operand range of a
control may stand between the rows -- not only a `cfont` one.  The line draws
nothing, and a control that carries display text keeps that text in payload
tokens outside the range, which end the candidate.  `CSELECT` is the shape
that proves it: GC23-046 `NOTICES` (DT `19920330095121`) draws a closed box
whose fourth row is the whole line `cselect 43 26 HDRNOTICES`, and hosted
prints the box with no line for the control -- the selector styles column 43
of the _next_ row (`<a href="FRONT_1...">&quot;Notices&quot; in topic FRONT_1</a>`).  SC24-5527-02 `3.10.4.4`, SC09-2417-00 `NOTICES`, SC31-711
`NOTICES` (DT `19941010174546`) and GG24-4302-00 `NOTICES` (DT
`19950308184737`) draw the same shape.

A cross-book `LNK` selector line draws nothing either, although its
`<...>` alternatives (<<markup_lnk_selector_alternatives>>) follow the
operands on the same display line: they are the selector's destination, not
display text.  SC24-5527-02 `1.0` (DT `19921218151459`) draws its `Note` box
around logical record 59 display line 29, BOO bytes `0x1D360`--`0x1D36E`:
`0D 4D C4 EE5C 9A 2D 2B 2B CFC4 2C CFB8`.  `0D` declares 13 encoded bytes;
they decode to `cselect`, `47`, `30`, `LNK`, `<BOOK>`, `<>`, `<>`,
`<SC24-5444>`, `<ANY>` and `<HCPA3>`.  Hosted prints the box unbroken, with no
line for the control, and links columns 47..76 of the next row to
`DOCNUM/SC24-5444`.

Hosted keeps the `CFONT` highlighting of words inside a box (`<B>In</B>`) and
resolves a `CSELECT` inside one into an `<a href=...>`; a preformatted
lowering carries neither, which is the only observed difference.

[[markup_the_regions_left_margin_is_content]]
===== The region's left margin is content

Column L is a display column, not padding, and hosted prints the region at
it: the box's rows keep the leading spaces the display lines give them and
must not be shifted left to column 0.  The margin belongs to the region, so
it varies inside one book and cannot be reinstated from a per-book constant.

[cols="<16,<16,>14",options="header"]
|===
|Book / topic |DT |Column L

|QSYSNEWG `1.0` |`19910524085706` |4 (frame at 3)
|GC23-046 `7.5.3` |`19910711093248` |1
|GC23-046 `6.5` |`19910711093248` |3
|OFCUSEOV `4.2.2` |`19900805103816` |7
|QSYSNEWG `7.3` |`19910524085706` |19
|===

Measured over all 856 drawn box regions the corpus emits, compared line for
line against the hosted `<pre>` runs: shifted to column 0 not one region
matches hosted; at their own columns 628 (73.4%) match exactly, and no region
that already differed for another reason changed.

[[markup_open_drawings_in_prose]]
==== Open drawings in prose

Not every drawing closes a box.  A panel excerpt may stop before its bottom
rule or start below its top rule, and a field template, a scale, a syntax
diagram's repeat arrow or a long-division bracket draws only the strokes it
needs.  Such a drawing still has one row that no prose row has: a display line
whose every non-blank cell is a box-drawing word (`U+2500`--`U+257F`), at
least one of them a corner, rail or junction rather than the plain rule
`U+2500`.  (A row of nothing but `U+2500` is the reader's horizontal rule; see
<<logical-controls>>.)  Hosted BookServer prints such a row, and every other
display row of the run of non-blank display lines around it, column for
column inside its `<pre>`, with the same display glyphs as a closed region.
The run is bounded by blank display lines; `cfont`/`cselect` lines and `SI`
lines inside it draw nothing, exactly as inside a closed region.

[cols="<34,<40,<30",options="header"]
|===
|File / record |Drawing |Hosted

|`IEAC6MST.BOO` record 83, lines 3..7 |ISPF panel excerpt: top rule and rails, no bottom rule |topic `2.1`, DT `19920124000100`, identical
|`OFCUSEOV.BOO` record 47 line 37 .. record 48 line 13 |lower half of a panel: rails and bottom rule, no top rule |topic `1.1`, DT `19900805103816`
|`SC24-546.boo` record 623, lines 16..20 |field template with column numbers and a `FIELDS:` label outside the drawn cells |topic `5.3`, DT `19940323131240`, identical
|`SC33-033.boo` record 493, lines 26..30 |scale: tick row `U+250C`/`U+252C`/`U+2510` over `U+2500`, then labels, `cfont` lines between |topic `4.77`, DT `19930422134757`
|`SC28-1881-05.boo` record 857, lines 38..40 |a list of the characters `<`, `>` and `U+2502`, the last spelled with the box word |topic `1.41`, DT `19930326130533`, identical
|===

Byte-level, the IEAC6MST top row is logical record 83 display line 3 at BOO
bytes `0x712E1`--`0x712EA`: `09 16 06 00 0D 00 CF43 00 05`.  `09` declares
nine encoded bytes: `16` a 7-cell space run, `06` `U+250C`, each `00` an
attaching spacing token, `0D` 63 copies of `U+2500`, `CF43` 19 more, and `05`
`U+2510` -- printed as eight blanks, 82 underscores and a blank.  The SC24-546
bottom row is record 623 display line 20 at `0x4074F`--`0x40762`:
`13 1B 07 00 C670 00 C6B5 00 C670 00 C6B5 00 C67A 00 09`, a 12-cell origin,
`U+2514`, `U+2500` runs separated by `U+2534` junctions, and `U+2518`.

A run is no drawing when it holds any other control line or a box word whose
hosted glyph is not established (FA1PLMM0 `C.0` record 781 line 16
underlines its column heads with `U+255A` runs).  One control does end a
run: a list-item closer `SRELIS<owner>` on a line of its own.  It ends the
list item the drawing belongs to, so the drawing cannot continue past it.
OFCUSEOV `1.8` record 118 line 24 opens the `Daily Calendar` panel, which
has a top rule and rails and no bottom rule.  Its last rail row is record 119
line 6.  Line 7 is the closer: the length byte `02` and the opcode word
`E9 E7` at `0x12A06`, spelling `SRELISse`.  Hosted (DT `19900805103816`)
prints the panel's 14 rows verbatim, nothing for the closer, and step 6 after
it.

[[markup_consecutive_drawn_boxes]]
==== Consecutive drawn boxes

Each closed box or open drawing is a region of its own.  When one region
follows another, what stands between them is printed between them:

* An empty display line (the length byte `00`) is one blank row of the
  `<pre>`.
* A picture figure (`SRFIG<id>`, a picture selector line, the placeholder
  row and `SREFIG`) prints its picture at that position.

Regions drawn directly under one another, with no display line between the
bottom row of the first and the top row of the second, print as adjacent
rows.  A `cfont`, `cselect` or `SI` line between two regions prints no row.

[cols="<30,<44,<30",options="header"]
|===
|File / record |Between the boxes |Hosted

|`IEAC6MST.BOO` `NOTICES` record 4 |line 16 is the `Note!` box's bottom rule, ending with `U+2518` at `0x6B3A4`.  Line 17 is the empty line `00` at `0x6B3A5`.  Line 18 is a `cfont` line.  Line 19 is the top rule of the `Production of This Book` box (length byte `0F` at `0x6B3B4`). |DT `19920124000100`: the two boxes with one blank row between them
|`QSYSNEWG.BOO` `3.1.2.3` record 93 |line 34 is the `F1=Help` box's bottom rule, ending with `U+2518` at `0x765E9`.  Lines 35--37 are `00 00 00` at `0x765EA`--`0x765EC`.  Lines 38--41 are `SRFIGFIGUNIQ43` (`02`, then `EB 5B` at `0x765EE`), `cselect 19 10 PIC43`, `PICTURE 43` and `SREFIG` (at `0x765FC`).  Lines 42--45 are four more `00` lines.  Then come an `SI` line, a `cfont` line and the `F3=Exit` box's top rule (length byte `0E` at `0x7660F`). |DT `19910524085706`: three blank rows, `<a name="FIGFIGUNIQ43">` around the picture's `<img>`, four blank rows, then the `F3=Exit` box
|===

The same topic repeats the shape for pictures 44 to 47 and the boxes after
them.  Read as one block, the boxes run together and the five pictures fall
behind the last box.  IEAC6MST `NOTICES`, N2AH1MST `NOTICES`, SC24-5527-02
`4.2.5` and `4.3.9`, SC33-033 `D.0` and QSYSNEWG `3.1.2.3` draw boxes
separated by empty lines this way.  Read one region at a time, every
drawing in these six topics matches a run of hosted `<pre>` rows exactly:
81 drawings in all, 65 of them in SC33-033 `D.0`.

[[markup_labelled_rules]]
==== Labelled rules

BookMaster marks the start and end of a passage with a rule that carries its
label: `General-Use Programming Interface` and `End of General-Use
Programming Interface`, or `Change for ...` and `End of Change for ...`.
Each is one display row:

* the opening corner `U+250C` or `U+2514`,
* rule text (dash words and the label, with `U+2500` allowed), and
* the matching closing corner, `U+2510` or `U+2518`.

A change bar may stand in front of the row.  No other box word is in the row,
and empty display lines stand above and below it.  Hosted BookServer prints the
row column for column in its `<pre>`.  The top corners print blank and the
bottom corners print `|`.  The row is a drawing of its own.  It is not a
sentence of the paragraph around it, and its corner is not a marker slot.

[cols="<30,<44,<30",options="header"]
|===
|File / record |Bytes |Hosted

|`SC33-033.boo` `4.132` record 813 line 23 |`0x4D624`--`0x4D63D`: `19` (25 bytes follow), `0B` a 3-cell space run, `CB 08` `U+2514`, `00`, `CB A0` 15 dashes, the label words, `CB A0`, `00`, `CB 0A` `U+2518`.  Empty lines 21--22 are above it. |DT `19930422134757`: `   \|--------------- End of General-Use Programming Interface ---------------\|`
|`GC23-046.boo` `CHANGES.1` record 29 line 10 |`0x9071`--`0x9084`: `13` (19 bytes follow), a 1-cell space run, `7B` the change bar `\|`, `06` `U+250C`, `00`, `DC B9` 22 dashes, `Change for Root Cause SPE`, `DC BA` 23 dashes, `00`, `05` `U+2510` |DT `19910711093248`: ` \|  ---------------------- Change for Root Cause SPE -----------------------`
|===

GC23-046 `CHANGES.1` record 31 line 3 is the closing `End of Change for Root
Cause SPE` row behind the same change bar.  FA1PLMM0 `15.4` record 647 line
7 (`End of Product-Sensitive Programming Interface`, DT `19910927114801`) and
SC24-5527-02 `7.4.5` record 674 line 2 (`For Modules, TXTLIBs and LOADLIBs`,
DT `19921218151459`) draw the same shape, and each is printed verbatim.

The rule's tokens do not always start a control segment of their own.  In
SC33-033 `4.132`, the `SRETBL` on record 813 line 20 decodes as a flattened
segment that runs on into line 23.  That segment covers the row's length
byte, its origin run, the `U+2514` and the attach after it; the label words
start the next segment at token 261.  The row is still drawn whole.  FA1PLMM0
`15.4` repeats the shape after `SREFIG` (record 647 line 3).

[[markup_box_enclosed_envelopes]]
==== An envelope drawn inside a closed box

An `SRTBL<id>` ... `SRETBL` envelope can stand wholly inside a closed drawn
box as the box's own markup.  It is then no table.  SC24-5527-02 `7.4.5`
records 675--676 draw a `Note` box titled `Obtaining File Type
Abbreviations`.  Its top rule is record 675 line 33 and its bottom rule is
record 676 line 23.  Between them are side rows, two `SI` lines, `cfont`
lines, two empty display lines and the envelope:

[cols="<16,<40,<44",options="header"]
|===
|Record, line |Bytes |Content

|676, 6 |`02`, then `E7 14` at `0x48C06` |`SRTBLTBLUNIQ492`, a line of its own
|676, 7--19 |-- |box side rows: `U+2502` at columns 7 and 76, text between them
|676, 20 |`01`, then `A5` at `0x48C86` |`SRETBL`, a line of its own
|===

Hosted (DT `19921218151459`) prints the whole box verbatim and emits no
`<table>`.  The opener becomes `<a name="TBLTBLUNIQ492">` wrapped around the
first box row after it, and the closer prints nothing.  An empty display
line inside the box is served as `<p>`, which is a blank row of the `<pre>`.
Topics `3.8.4.6`, `3.10.4.4`, `3.14.1` and `7.4.1` of the same book draw
the same shape, each matching hosted row for row.

The reading applies only to the proven shape:

* The box is closed: a top and a bottom rule at the same rails.
* Each opener and closer is a control line of its own.
* Openers and closers alternate, starting with an opener and ending with a
  closer.
* No other opener in the topic spells the same id.
* Every row an envelope spans is a side row that draws nothing but the box's
  own two rails and text between them.  Empty display lines are allowed.

An envelope whose rows draw structure of their own is a genuine table inside
a frame.  ITPPIBOK `4.3.5.4` record 158 line 23 opens `SRTBLTBLUNIQ34`
(`E8 EC` at `0x3F8DE`) inside the `SRFIGSCHED` frame.  Its rows are the
`| TASK | START | ...` grid, with `|_____|_____|` rules between them, and
that grid is a table.

[[markup_anchor_above_closed_box]]
==== An anchor directly above a closed box

A bare `SR<id>` anchor on a line of its own directly above the top rule of a
closed box names the box.  SC24-546 `4.3.21` (logical record 375) draws the
`DATE` syntax diagram this way.  Offsets are absolute file offsets.

[cols="<10,<44,<40",options="header"]
|===
|Line |Bytes |Reading

|12 |`02` at `0x2D72E`, then `E5 FC` at `0x2D72F` |`SRSPTFDATE`, a line of its own
|13 |`08` at `0x2D731`; `13`, `06` (`U+250C`), `00`, `0D` (63 x `U+2500`), `00`, `0C` (9 x `U+2500`), `00`, `05` (`U+2510`) |the box's top rule, columns 3-76
|14 |`05` at `0x2D73A`; `13`, `7A` (`U+2502`), `20`, `18`, `7A` |rails at columns 3 and 76, blank between
|15 |`04` at `0x2D740`, then `cfont 9 4 P` |the next control
|16--19 |-- |the diagram rows between the rails, with `cfont` lines between them
|20 |-- |the bottom rule, `U+2514` .. `U+2518`
|===

The decoded control string does not stop at the end of the anchor's line:
the segment that `SRSPTFDATE` opens runs on through lines 13 and 14, up to
the `cfont` of line 15, so its payload is the box's top rule and first rail
row.  Those are drawn rows, not part of the anchor.  Hosted BookServer (DT
`19940323131240`) serves `<a name="SPTFDATE">` wrapped around the top rule
and prints all eight box rows verbatim inside the topic's `<pre>`, with no
row for the anchor line.  Topics `3.14` (`SPTREF35`), `4.3.26` (`SPTDIGF`),
`4.3.32` (`SPTFORMF`), `4.3.34` (`SPTFUZZF`) and `4.3.62` (`SPTREF56`) of the same book
draw the same shape, each matching hosted row for row.

The reading applies only to the proven shape:

* The box is closed: a top and a bottom rule at the same rails.
* The anchor's line holds the one structural control and nothing else, and
  it ends exactly where the top rule's line begins.  No empty line or other
  line stands between them.
* Every payload token of the anchor's segment lies within the box's rows.

An anchor separated from the box by an empty line, or standing above an
open drawing, is not proven to name it.

The anchor can be a figure opener `SRFIG<id>` that opens no figure.
SC09-138 `3.1.9` (logical record 376) draws the `RENAME` syntax diagram
this way, and no `SREFIG` follows anywhere in the topic:

[cols="<10,<44,<40",options="header"]
|===
|Line |Bytes |Reading

|18 |`02` at `0x837F6`, then `DD A5` |`SRFIGFIGUNIQ30`, a line of its own
|19 |`0A` at `0x837F9`; `11` (3 spaces), `B0 46` (`U+250C`), `00`, `0C` (63 x `U+2500`), `00`, `08` (9 x `U+2500`), `00`, `B0 44` (`U+2510`) |the box's top rule
|20--25 |-- |rails, the diagram rows and their `cfont` lines
|26 |`0A` at `0x83866`; `11`, `B0 4A` (`U+2514`), `00`, `0C`, `00`, `08`, `00`, `B0 50` (`U+2518`) |the bottom rule
|===

The figure has no picture selector and no end marker.  Hosted BookServer
(DT `19910321130500`) serves `<a name="FIGFIGUNIQ30">` wrapped around the
top rule, prints the eight box rows verbatim, and continues with the
`where:` paragraph and the rest of the topic as ordinary text.  So such an
opener is the box's anchor, named `FIG<id>`, in exactly the shape above,
and it opens nothing.  The reading needs all of these:

* the opener is followed by no `SREFIG` in the topic;
* the figure has no picture selector;
* the opener directly precedes a closed box, as for any anchor above.

A figure opener whose `SREFIG` follows still opens a figure, and a table
opener `SRTBL<id>` above a box still opens a table.

[[markup_frame_rows_around_table]]
==== Frame rows around a table

A picture-less `SRFIG<id>` region may draw a frame of its own around the
table it encloses.  The frame is display rows of box words outside the
`SRTBL` envelope -- a top rule and rails above the opener, rails and a
bottom rule below the closer -- and the envelope's own rows carry the
frame's rails to the left and right of the table's grid.  ITPPIBOK
`4.3.5.4` (records 158-160) draws this for `SRFIGSCHED` around
`SRTBLTBLUNIQ34`.  Two-byte token values are big-endian; offsets are
absolute file offsets.

[cols="<10,<34,<44",options="header"]
|===
|Record, line |Bytes |Reading

|158, 19 |`02` at `0x3F8C1`, then `E7 F8` at `0x3F8C2` |`SRFIGSCHED`, a line of its own
|158, 20 |`0C` at `0x3F8C4`; `0A`, `D2 2F` (`U+250C`), `00`, `D2 48` (63 x `U+2500`), `00`, `D2 38` (9 x `U+2500`), `00`, `D2 2E` (`U+2510`) |the frame's top rule, columns 3-76
|158, 21-22 |`05` at `0x3F8D1` and `0x3F8D7`; each `0A`, `91` (`U+2502`), `13`, `0F`, `91` |frame rails at columns 3 and 76, blank between
|158, 23 |`02` at `0x3F8DD`, then `E8 EC` at `0x3F8DE` |`SRTBLTBLUNIQ34`, a line of its own
|158, 24 .. 159, 19 |-- |the envelope: two more rails, then the grid -- its top rule opens with `U+250C` and joins at `U+252C`, its inner rules open with `U+251C` -- between the frame's rails at columns 3 and 76, then one rail
|159, 20 |`02` at `0x3FB26`, then `E7 E0` at `0x3FB27` |`SRETBL`, a line of its own
|160, 0-1 |`05` at `0x3FB2A` and `0x3FB30`; each `0A`, `91`, `13`, `0F`, `91` |frame rails
|160, 2 |`00` at `0x3FB36` |an empty display line
|160, 3 |`05` at `0x3FB37`; `0A`, `91`, `13`, `0F`, `91` |frame rail
|160, 4 |`0C` at `0x3FB3D`; `0A`, `D2 30` (`U+2514`), `00`, `D2 48`, `00`, `D2 38`, `00`, `D2 31` (`U+2518`) |the frame's bottom rule
|160, 5 |`0E` at `0x3FB4A` |`   Figure 2. Example Schedule for a TPNS Test`
|160, 6 |`02` at `0x3FB59`, then `E7 DC` at `0x3FB5A` |`SREFIG`, a line of its own
|===

The length byte of each line spells a dictionary word like any other byte
(`05` spells `U+253C`, `0C` five spaces); it is a length and displays
nothing.

Hosted BookServer (DT `19910628074854`) prints the frame and the table as
one region of the topic's `<pre>`, 39 rows in source order (the `<p>`
counted as a row), with no `<table>`:

* `<a name="FIGSCHED">` wraps the top rule (record 158 line 20);
* the two rails of lines 21-22 follow;
* `<a name="TBLTBLUNIQ34">` wraps the first row after the `SRTBL` line
  (line 24), and the envelope's rows follow verbatim, frame rails included;
  the `cfont` line inside the envelope (line 27) prints nothing and styles
  `TASK`, `START` and `DATE` in bold;
* nothing is printed for `SRETBL`; the frame's rails follow, the empty
  display line as `<p>`, then the last rail and the bottom rule;
* the caption line follows as text, then `<p>` and the next paragraph.
  The `<p>` is record 160 line 7, an empty display line directly after
  the `SREFIG` line: its length byte `00` stands at `0x3FB5C`, and line 8
  (`Note that ...`) opens at `0x3FB5D`.  Although the flattened string
  places both lines in the `SREFIG` control's extent, they are ordinary
  body rows: the empty line is a paragraph break like any other, and line
  8's length byte opens its row.  DREICMST `1.2.1` (`Figure 5.`, record
  84) and `2.17.1.1` (`Figure 94.`, record 524 lines 20-22, where line
  22's length byte spells `U+251C`) close their frames the same way, and
  hosted (DT `19911219125856`) serves `<p>` after both captions.

So the frame's rows belong with the table's rows: a reader prints the rows
between the `SRFIG` line and the `SRTBL` line, the envelope's rows, and the
rows between the `SRETBL` line and the caption as one verbatim region, and
places the table anchor on the envelope's first row.  The region is
recognised only when the frame encloses exactly one table, every frame row
stands before the envelope's first row or after its last, and no display
line of prose stands between a frame row and the envelope.

A closed box that stands wholly inside the frame and holds no row of the
table is no frame row: it is a box of the frame's prose.  SC24-5527-02
`COMMENTS` (records 976-978) is the witness.  Its `SRFIGFIGUNIQ548` form
(the opcode word `E2 DE` at `0x61F43`) draws a closed `Note` box
between two of its paragraphs, well above the `SRTBLTBLUNIQ549` address
table:

[cols="<14,<46,<40",options="header"]
|===
|Record, line |Bytes |Reading

|977, 3 |`04` at `0x62024`; `cfont 8 4 2` |styles `Note` in the next row
|977, 4 |`08` at `0x62029`; `1A` (3 spaces), `06` (`U+250C`), `00`, `0A` (3 x `U+2500`), `9B` (`Note`), `13` (`U+2500` run), `00`, `05` (`U+2510`) |the box's top rule, titled
|977, 5-9 |-- |rails at columns 3 and 76, three rows of text between them
|978, 0 |`08` at `0x6207D`; `1A`, `07` (`U+2514`), `00`, `13`, `00`, `0C`, `00`, `08` (`U+2518`) |the bottom rule, the record's first line
|===

Hosted BookServer (DT `19921218151459`) prints the box row for row between
the paragraphs it stands between, with `<B>Note</B>` in its top rule, and
prints the other `cfont` lines of the form only as the bold words they
style.  So the frame's prose, its boxes and its `cfont` lines read exactly
as they would outside the frame, and only the frame's anchor and the table
are the frame's.  A closed box around the table -- the ITPPIBOK `4.3.5.4`
frame above, whose rails run beside the table's own rows -- holds table rows
and stays the frame's drawing.  Every other picture-less frame around a
table in the corpus draws no box word outside its envelope.

The end marker of a table or figure span is followed the same way.  GG24-395
`3.3.15` record 635 closes its `SRTBLTBLUNIQ21` span with `SRETBL` alone on
line 28 (token 228 at `0x13FED4`); line 29 is empty (length byte `00` at
`0x13FED6`) and line 30 opens the list
` | °   Need to reduce the cost of managing users ...` with length byte `14` at
`0x13FED7`, which the dictionary spells as five spaces.  Hosted DT
`19941215160749` serves `<p>` and then all three items at the same columns.
QSYSNEWG `A.4` record 320 has the same shape after `SREFIG` (token 73 at
`0x868A3`): three empty lines (`00` at `0x868A4`--`0x868A6`), then line 21
` | °   A command name consists of ...` at `0x868A7`.  Although the flattened
string places these lines in the end marker's extent, they are ordinary body
rows, and the first item stands at the same origin as its siblings.  Each
such line's length byte opens its row, and everything after it on the line
-- the origin run in front of the `°` included -- is that row's content:
only the blanks and the `.`/`,`/`;` the flattened string glues to the end
marker itself, before any length byte, are spacing.

`A.4` also carries a second list level that hosted (DT `19910524085706`)
prints inside its parent item: record 320 line 35 is
`       -   Using the command prompt display`, whose length byte is token 224
(`09` at `0x86961`), followed by the seven-cell origin (token 225) and `-`
(token 226, the one-byte value `0x27` at `0x86963`), and line 36 repeats the
shape for `Free format`.  The `-` is a content cell of its display line, not
a row-control slot, even though its value lies below `0x30`: only a line's
length byte is that slot.  The paragraphs at column 7 between the bullets
(`Some parameters are required ...`, `Either method can be used ...`)
continue the item above them.  QSYSNEWG `1.9` (record 37 lines 14-17,
21-23, 36-39 and 45-47) draws the same `-` sub-items.  The `-` rows are the
second list level described in <<markup_a_second_list_level>>.  The
continuation paragraphs at the item's text column are not settled: nothing
on their rows but the column ties them to the item above, and the same
column also carries ordinary indented paragraphs.

[[markup_list_item_inside_table_envelope]]
==== A list item inside a drawn table envelope

An `SRLIS<owner>` list item can be opened and closed inside an undeclared
`SRTBL<id>` ... `SRETBL` envelope whose rows are drawn character art.  Each
control stands on a display line of its own between two drawn rows.
Offsets are absolute file offsets.

[cols="<24,<36,<40",options="header"]
|===
|Book, record, line |Bytes |Reading

|IEAC6MST 512, 22 |`02` at `0x905E7`, then `E6 1D` |`SRLISone`, between two rows `   \|  \|  \|` of the drawn grid
|IEAC6MST 513, 8 |`02` at `0x906B3`, then `E4 5F` |`SRELISone`, between two such rows
|IEAC6MST 513, 13 |-- |`cselect 53 1 LISONE`, a link to the item from the next row's `1`
|SC24-5527-02 815, 15 |`02` at `0x532B6`, then `E4 40` |`SRLISreceivx`, inside the table's `Note` rows
|SC24-5527-02 816, 3 |`02` at `0x5332F`, then `E2 CC` |`SRELISreceivx`
|===

Hosted BookServer (IEAC6MST `7.9`, DT `19920124000100`; SC24-5527-02
`G.3`, DT `19921218151459`) prints both envelopes verbatim in the topic's
`<pre>`, with no `<table>`.  The opener's line prints nothing and the next
drawn row is wrapped in `<a name="LISone">` (`LISreceivx`) inside the same
`<pre>`.  The closer's line prints nothing and names nothing.  The
`cselect` row prints its `1` as `<a href="...#LISONE">1</a>`.

So inside a drawn envelope `SRLIS<owner>` on a line of its own is the
anchor of the row after it, and `SRELIS<owner>` on a line of its own is a
closer that prints nothing.  A closer is read this way only when an
`SRLIS<owner>` of the topic names the same owner, ignoring case, as in
prose.  Either control must have a drawn row of the envelope after it.

[[markup_inline_syntax_diagrams]]
==== Inline syntax diagrams

A railroad syntax diagram drawn into prose need not have any row made of box
words alone.  Its main path is a row of text -- keywords, variables and
terminators (`>>`, `><`, or the arrows `U+2192`/`U+2190`) -- joined by
`U+2500` rule runs, and the optional or alternative items hang below it.
Each branch leaves the main path through a `U+252C` junction and enters the
item row below through a `U+2514`, `U+251C` or `U+2502` in the same column;
an item row that continues the stack downward ends in `U+251C`/`U+2524`
junctions and the last one in `U+2514`/`U+2518` corners.  Hosted BookServer
prints `U+252C` as a blank and `U+2514`, `U+2518`, `U+251C`, `U+2524` as
`|`, so the junction columns look like gaps in the main path's rule.

SC24-546 `3.14` (logical record 238; offsets are absolute file offsets,
two-byte token values big-endian):

[cols="<10,<46,<38",options="header"]
|===
|Line |Bytes |Reading

|22 |-- |`cfont 11 5 P 17 5 P 23 6 P 32 4 P`
|23 |`23` at `0x22D91`; ... `0E` at `0x22DA7` and `0x22DAC` |the main path `>>__PARSE VALUE LINEIN() WITH__ ... __;__><`; each `0E` is `U+252C`, at columns 38 and 54
|24 |`04` at `0x22DB5` |`cfont 40 13 V`
|25 |`0F` at `0x22DBA`; `08` (`U+2514 U+2500`) at `0x22DBD`, ... `C6 67` (`U+2500 U+2518`) at `0x22DC8` |`|_template_list_|`, the corners at columns 38 and 54
|===

The row after line 25 is prose, with no empty display line between.  Hosted
(DT `19940323131240`) prints lines 23 and 25 one under the other, column for
column, with `PARSE`, `VALUE`, `LINEIN` and `WITH` in `<kbd>` and
`template_list` in `<var>`: the `cfont` lines style the row after them and
print nothing.  SC09-138 `1.1.2` draws the same shape across a record
boundary.  Logical record 70 line 46 (`1B` at `0x6B9B1`) is
`→→___statement_____ ___ ... ___→←`, with `U+252C` stored as `B0 A4` at
`0x6B9BF` and `0x6B9C5` (columns 26 and 42).  Line 47 is a `cfont` line, and
record 71 line 0 (`0F` at `0x6B9D7`) begins with `B0 4B` (`U+2514 U+2500`)
at column 26 and ends with `B0 61` (`U+2500 U+2518`) at column 42.  Hosted
(DT `19910321130500`) prints both rows verbatim, and likewise its two-row
stacks, the upper item row of which ends in `U+251C`/`U+2524` junctions.
SC28-1881-05 `1.2` (DT `19930326130533`) repeats the same examples.
GG24-4302-00 `5.3.2.2` (logical record 446, DT `19950308184737`) draws
`>>__ _/DEQUEUE_ __LUNAME__ ... ____________><` on line 17 with `U+252C` at
columns 7, 18, 53 and 62, and `|_/DEQ_____|` / `|_PURGE1_|` on line 19 with
`U+2514`/`U+2518` in the same columns; the main path's length byte `3C` at
`0x5DF16` spells a control name and is still only that row's length
(<<logical-controls_display_lines_govern_reflowed_prose_too>>).

The rows that belong to the diagram follow from the strokes alone:

* The main path is a display row of text, not of box words alone.  It holds
  at least one `U+252C`, and no stroke enters it from above (`U+2502`,
  `U+2514`, `U+2518`, `U+251C`, `U+2524`, `U+2534`, `U+253C`).
* Each next display row, with only `cfont`, `cselect` and `SI` lines
  between, enters from above in exactly the columns where the row before
  leaves downward (`U+2502`, `U+250C`, `U+2510`, `U+251C`, `U+2524`,
  `U+252C`, `U+253C`), and in no other column.
* The diagram ends at the first row that leaves nothing downward.

A diagram continued on a second main path is a second diagram.  SC24-546
`6.2.7` (logical record 707) ends the first main path with `>` and opens the
second with `>`.  Line 7 between them is an empty display line, and hosted
prints it as an empty row between the two drawings.

A main path whose strokes meet nothing -- an empty display line or another
control line below it, an item row shifted by one column, a stack that
leaves a stroke open -- is not proven to be a diagram.  A main path drawn
without a stack (`→→___statement___required_item___→←`) has no junction and
is not covered by this reading.  A run of display lines that holds a row of
box words alone is an open drawing (<<markup_open_drawings_in_prose>>) instead.

[[markup_vertical_arrows_in_prose]]
==== Vertical arrows in prose drawings

A drawing in prose may point at a word or a row with a vertical arrow: an
arrowhead word (`U+2191` up, `U+2193` down) whose shaft is a vertical
stroke in the same column of the adjacent drawn row.  An up arrow's shaft
runs down into the next display row, a down arrow's comes from the row above.
The shaft cell is the rail `U+2502`, the ASCII `|` a book may draw the same
stroke with, or a box word whose stroke runs on toward the head (`U+2514`,
`U+2518`, `U+251C`, `U+2524`, `U+2534`, `U+253C` under an up arrow; `U+250C`,
`U+2510`, `U+251C`, `U+2524`, `U+252C`, `U+253C` above a down arrow).  Such a
drawing need not have a row of box words alone, nor a railroad junction,
and hosted BookServer prints every row of its blank-delimited run at its
native columns.  The arrow words and their hosted display bytes are listed
in <<encoding_hosted_display_bytes_for_the_non_ascii_graphic_words>>; the arrows below
are stored as two-byte dictionary references.  Offsets are absolute file
offsets:

[cols="<18,<44,<38",options="header"]
|===
|Topic |Bytes |Reading

|`DREICMST` `1.5.5.4` |record 162 lines 29..37; line 37 has length byte
`06` at `0x1B688` and ends with `CB 34` at `0x1B68D` |nine rows of a
collating-order listing, each with `U+2502` at column 14; the last row,
`      GEN     `, has `U+2193` at column 14.  Hosted (DT `19911219125856`)
prints the nine rows, the arrow as `0x19`.
|`SC09-138` `B.0` |record 1894 lines 5..12; line 6 has length byte `12` at
`0xFB3FF` and six `B0 3B` words from `0xFB401` |`COPT('FL(E) SEARCH(...)')`,
then `U+2191` at columns 11, 31, 39, 49, 62 and 64, then (after
`cfont 34 1 R` on line 7) the rows `|`, `|__1__|_________|`, `| |`, whose
column 11 is `U+2502`, and the closing `U+2514` bracket on line 12.  Hosted
(DT `19910321130500`) prints lines 5, 6, 8, 10 and 12 one under the other,
each arrow as `&quot;`.
|`SC28-1881-05` `1.29` |record 701 lines 14..33; line 17 has length byte
`04` at `0x65A8F`, a 41-cell space run `C8 F4` and `C8 3A` at `0x65A92`
|`TMARGIN value`, `U+2191` at column 41, two ASCII `|` rows, the two text
rows, three `|` rows, `U+2193`, `BMARGIN value`, with a `cfont 41 1 E` line
before each arrow and bar row.  Hosted (DT `19930326130533`) prints all ten
rows, the arrow and bar cells in `<samp>`.
|===

Not every arrow of a drawing meets a stroke: on `SC09-138` `B.0` line 6
the arrows at columns 39, 49 and 64 stand two columns right of the bracket
ends below them, and hosted prints them there.  One arrowhead that meets
its shaft proves the drawing; the rows of the run are then printed as
stored.  The run is delimited as an open drawing's is: by empty display
lines, with only `cfont`, `cselect` and `SI` lines between its rows, each of
which styles or indexes and prints nothing.  An arrow that meets no stroke
proves nothing.

In all three books the flattened decoded string puts the arrow in no
control segment.  Its ASCII projection is `?`, and the splitter trims a
trailing `?` from the segment the arrow ends; only the display lines place
the arrow, in the row they frame.

[[markup_verbatim_cz_regions]]
==== Verbatim `CZ` regions

The `CZ` dialect names these verbatim regions (`SYNTAX` and `LINES`, below,
are two more), each opened by `cz OFF <tag>` and closed by `cz OFF E<tag>`,
and hosted BookServer serves each inside its own `<pre>` block, character for
character:

[cols="<8,<34,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Tag |Hosted marker |Evidence

|`XMP` |`<pre width="80">` |packet `2.4.1`
|`SCREEN` |`<pre width="80">` |SC09-2417-00 `3.2.3`, served as `SC09-241` DT `19961114175628` -- the `PURCHASE ORDER FORM` display, reproduced line for line including its drawn frame
|`LBLBOX` |`<pre width="132"><!-- lblbox -->` |SC09-2417-00 `NOTICES`, same DT -- the `___ Note! ___` box, and hosted names the region in the comment
|`ADDRESS` |`<pre width="80"><!-- * -->` |GA19-5486-13 `EDITION`, served as `GA19-548` DT `19951012161206` -- the twelve-row mailing address, reproduced line for line at column 7
|===

`ADDRESS` is the BookMaster address block, and it is a verbatim region exactly
like `XMP` and `LINES`.  In `GA19-5486-13.boo` topic `EDITION`, logical record
5, segment 13 is `cz OFF ADDRESS` (the tag word `ADDRESS` is the two-byte
token at `0xA03BD`--`0xA03BE`), followed by the twelve address rows from
`IBM United Kingdom Ltd.` to `Internet:  nigel_adams@uk.ibm.com`, and segment
14 is `cz OFF EADDRESS 0 0` (tag word at `0xA0450`--`0xA0451`).  Hosted opens
`<pre width="80"><!-- * -->` at the opener and prints every row in it at its
stored column 7.  The same pair wraps both addresses of `SC21-8295-03.boo`
`FRONT_1` and the two of its `EDITION` (served as `SC21-829`, DT
`19970409135933`), and the address inside the first list item of `DFHP7A00`
`BACK_4`.  `SC21-8295-03` `EDITION` also proves the closer rule: its first
`cz OFF EADDRESS 0 0` carries `or you can fax your comments to:`, the body
text that follows the address, at `left` 3 -- the same place `cz OFF EXMP`
puts the text it carries.  Hosted keeps such closer text inside the open
`<pre>` between two `<p>` breaks for `ADDRESS` and `XMP` alike (packet
`2.4.1`, DT `20260614112503`, prints `Note that zeros are omitted` that way),
so the two tags do not differ there either.

Inside a verbatim region a box-drawing run is display content, not row
geometry: the frame words occupy their own display columns and hosted prints
their display glyphs.  Drawn box _regions_ are a flattened-dialect shape and
are not planned inside the `CZ` dialect, which names its verbatim blocks
itself.

A verbatim region's rows also keep their *left margin*, exactly as a drawn
box region does: hosted serves the block at the display columns the rows
occupy, and the margin is a property of the region, not of the book.
SC09-2417-00 `3.1.3.5` (DT `19961114175628`) proves the per-region part on
one page -- its `#pragma mapinc` example stands at column 5 and the three DDS
listings under it at column 10.  Measured over all 486 `cz OFF XMP` blocks
the corpus emits (SC09-2417-00 263, packet 223): shifted to column 0 not one
block matches hosted line for line; at their own columns 454 (93.4%) match
exactly.

This is not the margin the flattened-dialect row model _infers_ from a line
with two leading space runs (above, "Title pages are the shape that proves
it").  That inference is guarded to the flattened dialect precisely because
the unguarded form re-indents SC09-2417-00 `4.1.9.4`'s COBOL listing by ten
columns it does not have; the rows here are already at their read columns and
are simply not shifted.

[[markup_cz_object_regions_table_fig]]
==== `CZ` object regions: `TABLE`, `FIG`

Besides the verbatim regions above, `cz OFF <tag>` / `cz OFF E<tag>` also
delimits the two _object_ regions the book already carries as `SR` structural
spans.  The `CZ` directive is a boundary only: it draws nothing itself, and
the object's rows belong to the `SRTBL`/`SRFIG` span, exactly as they do in
the flattened dialect.

[cols="<8,<32,<22,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Region |Directive pair |Object span |Order

|Table |`cz OFF TABLE` … `cz OFF ETABLE` |`SRTBL<id>` … `SRETBL` |the opener stands *before* `SRTBL`, the closer *after* `SRETBL`
|Figure |`cz OFF FIG` … `cz OFF EFIG` |`SRFIG<id>` … `SREFIG` |`SRFIG` stands *before* the opener, the closer *after* `SREFIG`
|===

Byte-level evidence, `packet.boo` (hosted id `packet`, DT `20260614112503`):

* Topic `2.4.4`, logical record 65: segment 11 `cz OFF TABLE`, segment 12
`SRTBLTBLUNIQ17` with the caption row `Table 1. IPv4 Address Classes`, the
header row `Class` / `Range` / `Default Netmask` and the five class rows,
then record 67 segment 3 `SRETBL` and segment 4 `cz OFF ETABLE 0 0`.
* Topic `1.3`, logical record 29: segment 2 `SRFIGFIGUNIQ5`, segment 3
`cz OFF FIG`, segment 4 pass:[<literal>cselect 35 9 PIC1 … PICTURE 1&#160;&#160;&#160;Figure 1. VHF/UHF LMR audio frequency range</literal>], segment 5 `SREFIG`, segment 6
`cz OFF EFIG 0 0`.  Hosted serves that region as
`<pre width="132"><!-- figure --><a name="FIGFIGUNIQ5"> <a href="picture-1?mode=zoom"><img … alt="PICTURE 1"></a></a>` followed by the
caption.

[[markup_cz_off_table_is_the_only_mark_of_a_genuine_table]]
===== `cz OFF TABLE` is the only mark of a genuine table

The BOO file carries *no table structure of its own*.  An `SRTBL` control is
an object id followed by pre-rasterized character art: `SRTBLDBCTL51`
(`GG24-4302-00` `10.2`, record 713) opens a 120-character box-rule run, the
caption `Table 15. DBCTL 5.1 Overview`, and further rule runs, with no column
definitions and no cell boundaries anywhere in the envelope.  The
BookMaster/SCRIPT compiler flattened `:table` and figure markup into a
fixed-width grid at build time, so the file holds a _picture_ of a table.  Many
`SRTBL` envelopes are not data grids at all but captured terminal screens --
`OFCUSEOV` `1.11`'s `Work with Mail` frame carries an option list, a spanning
`------From-------` label over three narrower headings and a function-key
footer inside one frame, and `OFCUSEOV` `1.1` puts a menu list and a calendar
date grid side by side in one frame.

The one place the source records that a region _was_ a `:table` is the
`cz OFF TABLE` directive, and the hosted BookServer's own rendering follows it
exactly.  Measured over all 861 corpus topics for which Geist recovered column
geometry (32 books, hosted pages fetched 2026-08-30 at the DT of the matching
document number):

[cols="<22,<45,>8",options="header"]
|===
|`cz OFF TABLE` present |hosted emits `<table>` |topics

|yes |yes |32
|no |no |826
|yes |no (hosted still marks the region `<!-- table -->`, then falls back to `<pre>` on its own page width) |3
|no |yes |*0*
|===

Hosted marks the region `<pre width="132"><!-- table -->` and then, when it can,
emits an HTML `<table border cellpading="3">` whose cells carry the same
rasterized text padded with `&nbsp;` and `<br>` (`SC09-2417-00` `4.1.4.1`,
`TBLLNKSPEC`, DT `19961114175628`).  Without the directive it emits no
`<table>` element anywhere on the page and reproduces the art verbatim inside
`<pre>` -- `GG24-4302-00` `10.2`, `SC31-711` `FRONT_1.1`/`2.4.1`/`NOTICES`,
`SC31-605` `2.1` all serve `<table>`=0, `<pre>`=1.  The three exceptions are
`GX27-3999-00` `A.0` and `SC41-485` `1.2.4`/`1.3.4`.

The directive is written by the later compilers only: every corpus book that
carries it (`SC09-2417-00` 1996, `GX27-3999-00` 1995, `SC41-485` 1995,
`packet`) postdates the books that do not.

Some rasterized table text is already malformed in the stored token stream.
In `GA19-5486-13` topic `3.10`, logical record 95, the `RELATIVE SYSTEM`
row contains four repetitions of `&dagg` + U+2502 + `r`, followed by a
period.  The first repetition starts at file offset `0x000A7E36`:

[cols="<22,<22,<56",options="header"]
|===
|File offset |Encoded bytes |Decoded interpretation
|`0x000A7E36` |`C6 D9` |`&`
|`0x000A7E38` |`00` |No-space token
|`0x000A7E39` |`CD 6A` |`dagg`
|`0x000A7E3B` |`00` |No-space token
|`0x000A7E3C` |`70` |U+2502, vertical box rule
|`0x000A7E3D` |`00` |No-space token
|`0x000A7E3E` |`DC 07` |`r`
|`0x000A7E40` |`00` |No-space token
|===

The four repetitions occupy `[0x000A7E36,0x000A7E61)`; byte `01` at
`0x000A7E61` supplies the terminal period.  Neither an `e` nor a semicolon
is present, so this is not a stored `&dagger;` entity.  In an HTML
representation the literal ampersand requires escaping as `&amp;`; escaping
does not cause the malformed spelling.  The intended text cannot be
established from these bytes alone.  Preserve the stored characters rather
than interpreting incomplete entity-like text as markup or guessing a repair.

Two consequences follow from the object span owning the region:

* The opener carries no display rows of its own; the closer carries the body
text that follows the object as ordinary paragraphs at its own
`left`/`indent`, the same rule `cz OFF EXMP` and the list closers follow.
* The pairing is not always one-to-one.  A figure region may frame a table,
in which case only the `ETABLE` closer is written: `SC41-485` `1.2`,
record 8 segments 22-25 are `cz OFF LBLBOX`, a `CFONT` header row,
`SRFIGTBLUNIQ1` and `SRTBLTBLUNIQ1`, and record 9 segments 1-4 are
`SRETBL`, `SREFIG`, `cz OFF ETABLE 2 2`, `cz OFF ELBLBOX 0 0` -- with no
`cz OFF TABLE` anywhere.  A reader must therefore treat the directive as
corroborating evidence and take the region extent from the `SR` span.
* Inside the labelled box those `SR` controls each stand alone on a display
line, and the box's rail rows that follow them are display rows of the box,
not part of the control.  Record 8 token 235 (bytes `0x7802`-`0x7803`) is
`SRFIGTBLUNIQ1`; byte `0x7804` is the next display line's length byte (6),
and that line's five tokens are a three-cell space run, `U+2502` (value
140), two space runs and `U+2502` again -- the blank rail row
`   |` .. `|`.  Read as a flattened character stream the control's text runs
on into that row, because nothing in it is a word; the display-line framing
is what ends the control at its own line.  Record 9 lines 9 and 11 are the
same rail row after `SRETBL` and after `SREFIG`.  Hosted BookServer (DT
`19951003131222`) prints every one of these rows inside the `lblbox` block:
five blank rail rows around `Required Parameter Group:` above the grid and
four below it, and `1.3` to `1.6` of the same book draw the same shape.

An envelope can cross a logical-record boundary, and its rows continue at
the start of the next record exactly as they would have continued in the
same one.  `SC21-8295-03.boo` topic `COMMENTS`: logical record 968 ends with
`SRTBLTBLUNIQ161` (token 253, bytes `0x77D2D`--`0x77D2E`) followed by the
envelope's top rule, its `1`..`5` header row and the rule under it.  Record
969 opens with the envelope's only body line: length byte `13` at
`0x77D8D` (its dictionary spelling is three blanks), the three-blank origin
run at `0x77D8E`, `U+2502` at `0x77D8F`, then `Overall` and `satisfaction`;
the rule under it follows, and `SRETBL` stands at token 45 (`0x77DC8`).
Nothing in record 969 repeats the envelope opener: the reader must carry the
open envelope across the boundary and read that first line as one of its
rows.  Hosted BookServer (`SC21-829`, DT `19970409135933`) serves the
envelope as a `<table>` whose rows are the empty header cell over `1`..`5`
and `Overall satisfaction` with five empty cells -- the same shape it serves
for the next envelope, `TBLUNIQ162`, which lies wholly in record 969.

[[markup_revision_bar_beside_a_box_table]]
*Revision bar beside a box table.*  A row of a box table that was revised
in this edition carries the revision bar in the margin, one cell left of the
box's own left border, and the border stays where every other line of the
box puts it.  `SC33-1369-09` topic `2.1`, logical record 94, envelope
`SRTBLTBLUNIQ35` under `cz OFF TABLE`:

[cols="<16,<12,<16,<56",options="header"]
|===
|Offset |Bytes |Line, cell |Content

|`0x0FE52` |`1E` |17 |length byte of the top rule (30)
|`0x0FE53` |`22` |17, cells 0--2 |a three-cell blank origin
|`0x0FE54` |`06` |17, cell 3 |`U+250C`, the top-left corner
|`0x0FEFF` |`1F` |23 |length byte of the revised row (31)
|`0x0FF00` |`20` |23, cell 0 |a one-cell blank origin
|`0x0FF01` |`90` |23, cell 1 |`U+2502`, the revision bar
|— |— |23, cell 2 |the blank the reader draws after the bar's word
|`0x0FF02` |`90` |23, cell 3 |`U+2502`, the box's left border
|`0x0FF03` |`24` |23, cells 4--9 |a five-cell blank run (and the reader's blank after the border)
|`0x0FF04` |`BA` |23, cell 10 |`Y`, the first cell's text
|`0x0FF1E` |`90` |23, cell 96 |`U+2502`, the right border
|`0x0FF1F` |`1E` |24 |length byte of the bottom rule (30), `22` three-cell origin, `08` `U+2514` at cell 3
|===

Every other line of the envelope opens with the three-cell origin and puts
its border in cell 3; only line 23 opens with a one-cell origin and the bar.
Hosted BookServer (DT `19971205131216`) serves the region inside
`<pre width="132"><!-- table -->` with the line as drawn,
` | |      Y     |      Y     | ...`: the bar is margin, the seven `Y` words
are the cells of a body row under the `Digital OpenVMS` .. `Windows NT`
header.  A reader that takes cell 1 for the left border misaligns the line
against the box and loses the row; the bar belongs to no cell.  The same
shape recurs in 22 more topics of the book (`2.3`, `2.4`, `2.6`, ... `2.62`).

Corpus census of the directive pairs, recounted by display line over all 34
fixtures. *Six* books carry any `CZ` control, not five: `SG24-204.boo` has a
single unmatched `cz OFF LBLBOX` and no other `CZ` directive anywhere. Two rows
of the earlier table were wrong.

[cols="<14,<16,<12,<11",options="header"]
|===
|Book |`TABLE`/`ETABLE` |`FIG`/`EFIG` |was

|`SC09-2417-00` |39 / 39 |29 / 29 |correct
|`GX27-3999-00` |25 / 25 |3 / 3 |had 19 / 19
|`SC41-485` |60 / 65 |0 / 0 |had 10 / 15
|`packet` |7 / 7 |9 / 9 |correct
|`XWEBDEMO` |0 / 0 |3 / 3 |correct
|`SG24-204` |0 / 0 |0 / 0 |not listed
|===

The `SC41-485` asymmetry the earlier table pointed at is real and larger than it
looked: 60 openers against 65 closers, five figure-framed tables written with an
`ETABLE` closer and no `TABLE` opener. Corpus totals: 131 `OFF TABLE` against
136 `OFF ETABLE`; 44 `OFF FIG` against 44 `OFF EFIG`.

Complete corpus census of every `cz OFF <tag>` region name, with line counts:

[cols="<45,>26,<45",options="header"]
|===
|Opener / closer |Lines |Status

|`XMP` / `EXMP` |543 / 543 |Verbatim region, modelled.
|`TABLE` / `ETABLE` |131 / 136 |Object region, modelled.
|`EUL`, `EOL`, `ESL`, `EDL`, `ENOTEL`, `EPARML`, `ENT` |272, 50, 18, 73, 10, 5, 86 |List closers, modelled.
|`FIG` / `EFIG` |44 / 44 |Object region, modelled.
|`SCREEN` / `ESCREEN` |24 / 24 |Verbatim region, modelled.
|`SYNTAX` / `ESYNTAX` |19 / 19 |Verbatim region: railroad syntax diagram.
|`LBLBOX` / `ELBLBOX` |15 / 15 |Verbatim region, modelled.
|`ARTWORK` / `EARTWORK` |13 / 10 |Verbatim region holding a `PIC<n>` selector; three openers close with `cz OFF EHP0` (3 lines) instead — `GX27-3999-00` `2.4`, `SC41-485` `COMMENTS`.
|`LINES` / `ELINES` |10 / 10 |Verbatim region: fixed lines, address blocks and forms.
|`TOC` / `ETOC` |5 / 5 |Front-matter region.
|`COVER` / `ECOVER` |4 / 4 |Front-matter region.
|`FIGLIST` / `EFIGLIST` |2 / 2 |Front-matter region.
|`TIPAGE` / `ETIPAGE` |2 / 2 |Front-matter region.
|`MSGL` / `EMSGL` |1 / 1 |Message-list region; its messages are `SRMSG<id>` anchors.
|`TLIST` / `ETLIST` |1 / 1 |Front-matter region.
|===

Nothing else occurs. `cz BREAK` accounts for 773 lines, `cz FLOW` for 5,855 and
`cz OFF` for 2,147, a total of 8,775 `CZ` display lines.

Books outside that census add two more verbatim region names:
`ADDRESS` / `EADDRESS` (see "Verbatim `CZ` regions"): `GA19-5486-13`,
`SC21-8295-03` and `DFHP7A00` each wrap their mailing addresses in it, and
`GA19-5486-13` adds `TABDATA`, closed by `EHP1` (see "`cz OFF TABDATA` closing
with `cz OFF EHP1`" below).

[[markup_the_six_region_names_that_had_no_modelled_meaning]]
==== The Six Region Names That Had No Modelled Meaning

All six are ordinary regions of the two kinds this chapter already describes:
`SYNTAX`, `LINES` and `ARTWORK` are *fixed-column verbatim regions* that
hosted serves inside `<pre width="80">`, and `MSGL`, `PROBD` and `ORESP` are
*message-list blocks*. A reader needs no new machinery for any of them.

*`cz OFF SYNTAX` … `cz OFF ESYNTAX <left> <indent>`* wraps a railroad syntax
diagram. `SC09-2417-00.boo` topic `PREFACE.2.1` (`How to Read the Syntax Diagrams`), logical record 29, segment 4 opens one and segment 6 closes it; the
body is segment 5, whose `CFONT` reads `cfont 11 9 P 22 13 V`. Hosted at DT
`19961114175628` serves it as

[source,html]
----
</pre><pre width="80"><!-- * -->
       &gt;&gt;__<kbd>STATEMENT</kbd>__<var>required_item</var>____________________&gt;&lt;
</pre>
----

so column 11 length 9 covers exactly `STATEMENT` and column 22 length 13 covers
exactly `required_item`: the `CFONT` operands are absolute display columns of
the stored row, left padding included, exactly as everywhere else. The
region's drawn characters (`>>`, `__`, `|`, `><`) are display text and must
not be reflowed or stripped.

*`cz OFF LINES` … `cz OFF ELINES <left> <indent>`* wraps a block of fixed
lines that must not be reflowed — an address block or a form. `SC09-2417-00.boo`
topic `EDITION`, logical record 4, segment 14 stores five rows beginning
`IBM Canada Ltd. Laboratory`; hosted serves them one per line inside
`<pre width="80">`. `SC41-485.boo` topic `COMMENTS` record 459 segment 16 and
`GX27-3999-00.boo` topic `COMMENTS` record 148 segment 6 use the same region
for a reader's-comment form whose rows are `Name . . . . ______`.

Verbatim regions can be nested in either order.  In `GX27-3999-00.boo` topic
`2.6.1`, logical record 45 stores `LINES` at bytes `0xe097`--`0xe099`, `XMP`
at `0xe09c`--`0xe09e`, `EXMP` at `0xe0cd`--`0xe0cf`, and `ELINES` at
`0xe0d4`--`0xe0d6`.  Logical record 51 stores the inverse order: `XMP` at
`0xe9be`--`0xe9c0`, `LINES` at `0xe9c3`--`0xe9c5`, `ELINES` at
`0xea5d`--`0xea5f`, and `EXMP` at `0xea64`--`0xea66`.  Thus each inner pair
is balanced before the outer closer; its delimiters are structural and do not
interrupt or reorder the enclosing fixed-column rows.

*`cz OFF ARTWORK`* opens a fixed-column region holding a picture reference,
and its picture is a `CSELECT` whose target is `PIC<n>`.
`GX27-3999-00.boo` topic `FRONT_1`, record 9: segment 10 is `cz OFF ARTWORK`,
segment 11 is `cselect 3 9 PIC1` with the row text pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;PICTURE 1</literal>], and hosted
at DT `19950730184057` serves that row as

[source,html]
----
</pre><pre width="80"><!-- * -->
   <a href="picture-1?mode=zoom"><img
     src="/bookmgr/pictures/GX27-3999-00.19950730184057.P1.GIF" alt="PICTURE 1"></a>
----

*`ARTWORK` closing with `cz OFF EHP0` is a region close like any other.* The
same record's segment 12 is pass:[<literal>cz OFF EHP0 0 0&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;The adapter kit consists of:</literal>],
and hosted emits that text and then `</pre>` — so `EHP0` ends the `<pre>`, the
text the control carries belongs to the closing region, and what follows is
flowed prose again. Three of the thirteen `ARTWORK` openers close this way
rather than with `EARTWORK`; the two forms are interchangeable in the output.
`GA19-5486-13.boo` closes all 55 of its `ARTWORK` regions with `EHP0`.

*`cz OFF TABDATA` closing with `cz OFF EHP1`* is the same shape for a
fixed-column table of data.  `GA19-5486-13.boo` topic `FRONT_2` (`Key to
Abbreviations`), logical record 33: segment 11 is `cz OFF TABDATA` (the tag
word is the two-byte token at `0xA283A`--`0xA283B`), its rows run from
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;K&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;=&#160;&#160;1,024&#160;bytes</literal>] through
pass:[<literal>&#160;&#160;&#160;dpi&#160;&#160;=&#160;&#160;dots&#160;per&#160;inch</literal>] in four groups
separated by blank rows (segments 11-13, two `CFONT` runs marking an
italic `M`), and segment 14 is `cz OFF EHP1 0 0` (tag word at
`0xA298B`--`0xA298C`), the topic's last directive.  Hosted (`GA19-548`, DT
`19951012161206`) serves every row in one `<pre width="80"><!-- * -->`, the
blank rows as `<p>` breaks, and the `CFONT` words as `<I>M</I>`.  `EHP1`
occurs nowhere else in `GA19-5486-13`, `SC21-8295-03`, `DFHP7A00` or
`EZ30AD01`, and `HP1` opens nothing in them, so the highlight end closes the
`TABDATA` region and nothing else.  The highlight level differs by region --
`EHP0` closes `ARTWORK`, `EHP1` closes `TABDATA` -- and a reader should not
take one for the other.

*`cz FLOW MSGL <left> <indent>` / `cz OFF MSGL`* opens a message list. The
messages themselves are `SRMSG<id>` anchors, one per message, and hosted emits
`<a name="MSG <id>"><hr></a>` before each. `GX27-3999-00.boo` topic `B.0`
(`Appendix B. NDIS MAC Driver Messages`), logical record 104, segments 1 and 2
open and close the region and segment 3 is `SRMSG LTC0008`. The complete
message-list grammar is under <<markup_cz_message_lists>>.

*`cz FLOW PROBD <left> <indent>`* and *`cz FLOW ORESP <left> <indent>`* are
the two body blocks of a message: the problem description and the operator
response. Both are ordinary flowed paragraphs indented to the `indent` operand,
and both lead with a bolded label. In `GX27-3999-00.boo` `B.0` every one of the
23 `PROBD` blocks leads with `Explanation:` and every one of the 23 `ORESP`
blocks with `User Action:`; hosted serves record 105's pair as

[source,html]
----
          <B>Explanation:</B>  The value provided for the parameter in the IBMMPC
          module in PROTOCOL.INI is not the correct type.
...
          <B>User</B> <B>Action:</B>  Edit the PROTOCOL.INI file to change the value for
----

at column 10, the blocks' `indent` operand. In the 34-book census they occur
only in `GX27-3999-00.boo`, 23 each; the wider library uses them and eight
more message body tags (see <<markup_cz_message_lists>>).

[[markup_cz_message_lists]]
==== CZ Message Lists

A message list is far more common than the 34-book census suggests. A random
sample of 500 of the 8,236 books in the hosted BookServer's library (498 of
them readable) holds 210 books with `CZ` controls, and 43 of those (8.6% of
the sample) open at least one `cz FLOW MSGL`. Those 43 books carry 659
`cz FLOW MSGL` openers, 659 `cz OFF MSGL` and 659 `cz OFF EMSGL` closers; in
650 of the openers the `cz OFF MSGL` follows directly in the same logical
record, and the other nine were not followed across a record boundary.

*Grammar.* A message list is

----
cz FLOW MSGL <left> <indent>      opener, draws nothing
cz OFF MSGL                       always directly after the opener, draws nothing
  message ...                     one or more messages
cz OFF EMSGL <left> <indent>      closer; rows after it are ordinary body text
----

and each message is

----
SRMSG <id>                        a display line of its own
<rule row>                        U+2500 cells only
<headline rows>                   the message number and text
cz FLOW <body tag> <left> <indent>  one block per body tag, in source order
----

The headline rows belong to whichever directive precedes the `SRMSG` (the
`cz OFF MSGL` for the first message, the previous message's last block for
every later one); the anchor and rule before them separate them from it.
A body block may contain ordinary nested structure -- a `cz FLOW P`
paragraph, an `XMP`, `SL` or `UL` region closed again before the next body
tag -- exactly as anywhere else in the dialect.

*The `SRMSG` line.* `GX27-3999-00.boo` logical record 104 stores the opener,
its `OFF`, the first message's `SRMSG` row and its rule as

[cols="<20,<16,<64",options="header"]
|===
|Offset |Bytes |Decoded reading

|`0x1303C` |`06` |row length 6
|`0x1303D`..`0x13042` |`4E 96 E3 47 D0 D0` |`cz` `FLOW` `MSGL` `3` `3` (`E3 47` is the two-byte `MSGL` token)
|`0x13043` |`04` |row length 4
|`0x13044`..`0x13047` |`4E A9 E3 47` |`cz` `OFF` `MSGL`
|`0x13048` |`03` |row length 3
|`0x13049` |`B6` |`SRMSG`
|`0x1304A`..`0x1304B` |`E2 9C` |`LTC0008`
|`0x1304C` |`04` |row length 4
|`0x1304D` |`17` |three blanks
|`0x1304E` |`10` |a 63-cell run of `U+2500`
|`0x1304F` |`00` |bare spacing token
|`0x13050` |`0C` |an 11-cell run of `U+2500`
|`0x13051` |`2B` |row length 43: `cfont 3 7 2 11 3 2 ...` for the headline
|===

The `SRMSG` row holds the opcode and the message id and nothing else, so the
id is the rest of that one display line. The served anchor is `MSG ` followed
by that text, exactly as for the `SRMSG <id>` metadata line of an
individually indexed message topic: hosted `GX27-3999-00` `B.0` (DT
`19950730184057`) serves

[source,html]
----
<a name="MSG LTC0008"><hr>
</a>
   <B>LTC0008</B> <B>The</B> <B>value</B> ... <B>is</B> <B>not</B>
          <B>the</B> <B>correct</B> <B>character</B> <B>type.</B>
----

and prints neither `SRMSG` nor the id from the `SRMSG` row: the headline's
`LTC0008` is the headline row's own word. The rule row is the `<hr>`; it
prints no character. A message id may be any text: `SC31-6242-02.boo`
(DT `19950803212024`) serves `SRMSG 1` and `SRMSG 100` as
`<a name="MSG 1">` and `<a name="MSG 100">`, `SC31-8224-01.boo`
(DT `19980821071949`) `SRMSG 00000004` as `<a name="MSG 00000004">`, and
`GC30-3845-01.boo` (DT `19960718115511`) `SRMSG TOKEN-DOS-211` as
`<a name="MSG TOKEN-DOS-211">`.

*Body tags.* Each body block is an ordinary flowed paragraph at its `indent`
column whose label (`Explanation:`, `System Action:`, ...) is the `CFONT`
bold run it opens with; hosted adds no markup of its own. The 43 sampled
message-list books use these body tags, with their whole-book `cz FLOW`
counts:

[cols="<14,>12,>10,<64",options="header"]
|===
|Tag |Lines |Books |Labels the blocks open with

|`XPL` |26,914 |37 |`Explanation:`
|`URESP` |20,545 |30 |`User Response:`, `Action:`
|`DEST` |15,485 |12 |`Destination:`
|`SYSACT` |14,823 |21 |`System Action:`
|`MODULE` |7,172 |13 |`Module:`
|`ORESP` |5,404 |11 |`Operator Response:`, `User Action:`
|`PRESP` |1,176 |11 |`Programmer Response:`, `Application Programmer Response:`, `Action:`
|`SPRESP` |733 |9 |`System Programmer Response:`
|`SEVERITY` |633 |5 |`Severity:`
|`PROBD` |150 |6 |`Explanation:`, `Cause:`, `Problem Determination:`
|===

The label is ordinary display text of the block's first row, styled by its
own `CFONT` triple; the tag does not supply it and a reader must not
synthesize it.

`SC31-6242-02` `APPENDIX1.2.1` (DT `19950803212024`) shows four of them in one
message:

[source,html]
----
<p>   <B>Explanation:</B>  Operating system error occurred in component %i, instance
...
<p>   <B>Action:</B>  Check the error messages before and after this one in the log
...
<p>   <B>Module:</B>  INTERNALSYS
<p>   <B>Severity:</B>  WARNING
----

The same books also write `NUMBYTES` (3 books) and `CODEL` (5 books) flow
tags, and three of them a `cz OFF MSG` directive (133 lines in
`dfhtmp12.boo`, `cbcvmu00.boo` and `SC33-1654-09.boo`). In `dfhtmp12.boo`
`3.10.1` it stands between the `SRMSG 0` row and the rule row (record 696
segments 12--13), so the rule and headline are its rows. Their hosted reading
is not yet established.

*Closing `SRMSG`.* A list may end with a bare `SRMSG` -- the opcode alone on
its display line -- directly before `cz OFF EMSGL`. It is the anchor
`MSG` and draws nothing (`GX27-3999-00` record 120 segments 1--2, served as
`<a name="MSG"></a>` with no `<hr>`). When another list opens at once,
`SC26-7132-01.boo` `2.3.2` record 256 segment 24 through record 257 segment 3
(`SRMSG`, `cz OFF EMSGL 0 0`, `cz FLOW MSGL 3 3`, `cz OFF MSGL`,
`SRMSG ILU901A`), both anchors precede the next message's rule: hosted
(DT `19980422095213`) serves `<a name="MSG"></a>` and then
`<a name="MSG ILU901A"><hr>`.

*An announced list.* A topic whose menu lists one topic per message ends with
the opener pair and nothing after it; the messages and the list's
`cz OFF EMSGL` are in the topics that follow. `GC35-0271-02.boo` `6.0` record
628 segments 18--21 are `cemenu`, `cz BREAK 3`, `cz FLOW MSGL 3 3`,
`cz OFF MSGL`, the last directives of the topic, and hosted
(DT `19980714150144`) serves the pair as an empty `<pre width="80"></pre>`
after the menu. `SC33-6740-00.boo` `A.0`, `SC31-8129-01.boo` `APPENDIX1.5`,
`SC26-4834-03.boo` `3.0` and `GC33-1694-01.boo` `1.8` end the same way.

An announcement may be followed by further directives that own no display
row; they draw nothing either.  `GC33-1177-01.boo` `1.81.54` ends its
message with `cz OFF EMSGL 0 0`, `cz FLOW MSGL 3 3`, `cz OFF MSGL` and an
empty `cz FLOW P 4 4`, and hosted (DT `19990318184419`) serves
`<p><p></pre><pre width="80"><!-- * -->` and `</pre>` after the last
message row -- nothing visible.  A display row after the announcement would
be content of a list the topic never closes; no such topic is described.

*Closing an earlier topic's list.*  The last message topic of a list
carries the closer of the list an earlier topic announced.  The line matches
only the generic `CZ OFF E` prefix, which prints nothing when no region is
open.  `GC31-8601-00.boo` `1.1.3`, logical record 21, ends with the display
line `07 2F E3 02 DA D8 A8 A8` at `0xA7AF`: length byte `07`, `cz` (`2F`),
`OFF` (`E3 02`), `EMSGL` (`DA D8`), `0` (`A8`), `0` (`A8`).  Hosted
(DT `19970728124306`) serves `<B>Procedure</B> <B>Name:</B>` from the row
before it and then only the page footer.  Every word of the hosted body is
reproduced by this reading in one such topic of each of the sixteen books
listed under the next paragraph.  A list the topic did open itself is closed
once; a second closer of it has no observed meaning.

*Message topics outside a list.*  The individually indexed message topics
that follow such an announcement carry no `cz FLOW MSGL` of their own: the
`SRMSG <id>` is a metadata line before `ST`, the rule and headline rows
precede the first directive, and the body blocks follow as
`cz FLOW XPL`, `cz FLOW SYSACT`, ...  A body tag is matched by no prefix of
its own; it is a generic `CZ FLOW` (see "Reading a CZ body"), so it closes
the open `<pre>` and its rows flow as one paragraph, exactly as inside a
list.  `GC33-1694-01.boo` `1.4.1`, logical record 85, stores the first body
block as the display line `08 60 79 91 3E C3 31 2D 38` at `0x2D712`: length
byte `08`, then `cz` (`60 79`), `FLOW` (`91 3E`), the two-byte `XPL` token
`C3 31`, `3` (`2D`) and `4` (`38`), followed by the `cfont 4 12 2` line and
the row `    Explanation:  The CICS subsystem identified ...`.  Hosted
(DT `19990719095008`) serves the heading `<a name="MSG DFH0100"><h6>`, the
post-heading `<pre width="132">` holding the rule and the headline
`DFH0100 CICS SUBSYSTEM IS NOW INITIALIZED`, and then

[source,html]
----
</pre>
    <B>Explanation:</B>  The CICS subsystem identified in an entry in an IEFSSNxx
    member of SYS1.PARMLIB has been successfully initialized.
</pre><pre width="132"><!-- * -->
</pre>
    <B>System</B> <B>Action:</B>  None.
----

-- one flowed block per body tag, each ended by its empty row.  Every word
of the hosted body is reproduced by this reading on two or three message
topics of each of sixteen books: `GC33-1694-01`, `GC33-1177-01`,
`GC33-0790-04`, `GC31-8601-00`, `GC31-8368-00`, `GC24-5850-00`,
